Launchpad: Novation's Launchpad S Programmer's Reference Manual 1.02 confirms a note-on per pad on channel 1, red in bits 0 to 1 and green in bits 4 to 5 with off as 0Ch (16 x green + red + 12), control changes 68h to 6Fh for the top row, and B0 00 00 turning all LEDs off and restoring power-on settings. "400 messages a second" is not in that document and the claim is withdrawn; 200 a second is this program's own limit. It is not confirmed from a primary source that the Mini (1235:0036) shares the Launchpad S protocol. nanoKONTROL2: Korg's Parameter Guide, page 9: LED mode covers the transport and S, M, R buttons, and an LED lights on its button's On Value and darkens on its Off Value, so 127 and 0 on channel 1 hold only with the factory On and Off values and channel. MIDI Mix: a 2018 blog post by Tero Heikkinen and open scripts; nothing about it is confirmed from a primary source.
Cost / rules out: a wider sandbox for every box, including the ones with no controller. One more thread per lit controller and one that looks at the box three times a second, with one player status call (about eleven questions to mpv) at most every 0.6 seconds while any light is on. A light is addressed like its control, so a controller whose lights use other numbers cannot be described yet. If the power is cut, lights stay as they were. The fade lights needed a word on the panel's fader (`label`), a small edit in `pvj/api.py`.
## D54. A look is a theme that names a style from a fixed set; Signal is the second style, with its fonts on the box and one colour per area

2026-10-04. The owner chose direction "D. Signal" in the redesign's Figma file ("i like style direction 4 so far. let's try that") and wants to try it on the box before it replaces anything. A theme was seven colours, and Signal is more than colours: type, shapes, sizes, a switch drawn another way. So:

- **A theme may name a style, and a style is a block of the panel's own stylesheet.** `STYLES` in `pvj/themes.py` is the fixed set (`default`, `signal`). The name puts `data-style` on the root element: the server writes it into the `<html>` tag when it serves the page (so the first paint is right and the connect screen has it, with no inline script), and the panel sets it again when the look is changed. `app.css` has one marked block per style, every selector under `html[data-style="..."]`; a test reads the block and refuses a selector that is not. A theme file still carries no CSS: beside the seven tokens it may give `areas` and `states`, fixed names with `#rrggbb` values, and the box works out the text colour for each. A well-formed style name this version does not know gives the default look and keeps the theme's colours.
- **The look is still one setting.** What is stored, exported and imported is the theme's name (and the accent), as before; the style comes from the theme file on the box. Two themes use the style: Signal (dark room) and Signal light. The Look page did not need a mode switch: they are two more buttons in the row of themes.
- **One colour per area, by rebinding the accent.** The root element also says which area is open (`data-area`), and the Signal block sets `--ac` and `--on` from it, so everything that already used the accent follows: the title block, the open tab, primary and chosen buttons, the On half of the switch, a slider's fill. Room yellow, Shaders pink, Live and Media green, System blue are Figma's; Mix violet is ours. A theme with `areas` has no single accent, so Look hides the swatches for it.
- **No state is an area's colour; Active is cyan `#00e0ff`. For the owner to confirm.** Figma's board gives Active the fixed yellow, which is Room's colour, and its Shaders screen draws Active pink, that area's colour. The first build followed the screens (Active in the area's colour) and the coordinator reversed it the same night: a state must never be confused with an area. Cyan is the hue furthest from the five area colours, from amber and from red; white is already Ready. Off, Set up, Active and Problem are in the theme file, a test refuses a theme whose Active is within 100 (in RGB) of an area or of another state, the browser test refuses a state chip in the open area's colour, and every chip carries its word. Still close, and Figma's own: Set up amber and Room yellow, 44 apart. An error line is red in Signal, not the accent as in the default look.
- **In the light, the area colour is only ever a filled block.** Yellow on the off-white page is 1.2 to 1. The box computes `--ink` per area: the area colour where it reads at 4.5 to 1 on the page and on a surface, else the text colour. Rings, stripes and dashed outlines use it.
- **The fonts ship with the panel.** Archivo (one variable file, weights 400 to 900) and JetBrains Mono Medium, Latin subsets, 35 KB together, in `pvj/web/fonts` with each family's OFL text. The server's list of static files is now built from files only, plus the `.woff2` files of that one folder; the policy header gains `font-src 'self'`; a font may be kept by the browser for a day (its name holds the start of its checksum, so a rebuilt font has a new name), everything else is still never stored. The default look never asks for them. The map of static files never holds a symbolic link, and a damaged theme in the settings gives the default look, not an error, because the page is served before anyone has paired (both from the independent review).

Why this way: a style chosen by a free-form field, or CSS in a theme file, would let an add-on theme or an imported settings file put text into the page; a name from a list cannot. Rebinding the accent meant the 400 lines of existing CSS did not have to be written twice. Writing the attribute on the server costs one string replace and avoids both a flash of the default look and an inline script.

Cost / rules out: the Signal block is about 240 lines that must follow every new part of the panel by hand; a screen built after this and never looked at in Signal will work but may look unfinished (the browser test's Signal pass checks sizes, overflow and contrast, not taste). `index.html` must keep the literal `<html lang="en">`, or the style is silently not written (a test compares). The panel has two lists of style names, in `themes.py` and in `app.js`. The fonts are Modified Versions under the OFL (subsets), which is allowed because neither family reserves its name, and must be rebuilt by hand if a glyph outside Latin-1 is ever needed; text in another script falls back to the device's font. Capitals are wide: titles of pages wrap at 390 px. The Signal block writes two strings as CSS content, "Live" and the product's name, to title the Live screen with its area without touching the default look; they must follow if either changes. A slider's fill in Signal depends on the panel's script setting `--fill` (four times a second and on input); with the script stopped a slider shows its thumb and an empty bar. The number is 54 because the open pull request #82 uses D53.

## D55. An effect is an ISF filter hooked at NATIVE that converts the colours itself; a third shader layer that never takes the screen

2026-10-05. Effects: filters over whatever plays (`pvj/effects.py`, [pvj/SHADERS.md](../pvj/SHADERS.md#effects)), pull request #81.
Why: the owner asked for "a more robust shader playback and control system" and pointed at Vidvox's ISF-Files, most of which is filters (the survey, D44). **The stage** was settled by experiment in CI, as D38's was. A filter must sit before the brightness that opacity, the fades and Blackout use, or a filter that adds light undoes a Blackout: an invert hooked at MAINPRESUB and at MAIN turned a blacked-out picture white in all three ways of drawing (a test asserts it, so the reason cannot go stale). mpv applies that brightness inside its conversion to RGB, and the one stage before it, NATIVE, is YUV for a video. So an effect hooks NATIVE and converts each pixel it reads to RGB and its result back, with the matrix and range the player's output was given and the exact inverse on the way back; the amount mix is done after that, so amount 0 is the pixel that was read. The text holds the filter twice, as a hook that binds the picture's LUMA plane and one that binds its RGB plane: mpv leaves out a hook whose bound texture is missing, so a playlist that mixes a video and an RGB picture is right from the first frame without a new text. A worker looks once a second at what plays and writes a new text when the matrix, the range or the frame rate has changed. **The layer:** the player's shader list is source, effect, mapping; a source and an effect are never on together; an effect has its own serial, and what waits for the worker carries that serial and the player's epoch, so D18's rule holds: nothing asked for before the screen changed hands lands after. **Its life:** one at a time (a chain is a later step); it stays across clip changes, because a performer plays clips through one look; Stop, a generator, Vibes, the module going off, a player restart and a panel restart take it off; nothing puts one on but a person or a controller. **TIME** is mpv's frame number over the clip's frame rate (exact in CI at 25, 30, 50 and 60 a second). **Flash safety** reuses D46's opt-in: a filter that reads the clock or says it flashes keeps speed 1 or below unless "faster" is on; the box cannot analyse a filter's output and the docs say so. **Kept apart from the generators:** own folders (`pvj/effects.d`, `<state>/effects`), own routes (`/api/effects...`), own list, so no filter can reach Play, a rotation set or Vibes, where it would read the carrier's frame counter as a picture. Presets are the generators' machinery under another key (`fx_presets`) in the same settings section, with no schema change. A filter's weight is a count from its text and says so; an upload over 64 reads or 256 loop rounds a pixel is refused. **The buffers:** on the boards that run mpv with its cheap scaling (a Pi 4) the player draws in 8-bit buffers while an effect is on, as D28 and D38 do for the mapping and a generator; elsewhere an effect leaves the buffers alone, because with mpv's default scalers a screenshot right after that setting was black in CI.
Cost / rules out: the colour arithmetic is the player's word about the picture, so for up to a second after a change between kinds of video a filter works with the last clip's matrix or range (a filter that changes nothing is right regardless). The two-hook text relies on a behaviour of mpv that is asserted in CI on 0.37 only. A change of the speed makes TIME jump: a clip has no carrier to count from. The per-board choice of buffers rests on one measurement for the mapping and on CI's software GPU; nothing about effects is measured on a Pi. No chain of effects, no transitions, no passes, no persistent buffers (the survey's steps 2 to 4). A broken upload flashes black once while the GPU looks at it. An effect does not survive a restart, so the vignette for the painting wall has to be put on again by hand for now.
After the independent review (the same day): **an Off always ends what was asked for before it**, also when no effect is on (the player's effect serial moves on, and the engine counts its own requests), since the first version let a job the worker had already taken go on after an Off. **What a controller sends is answered from memory and done by the worker**: no question to the player and no wait for its lock on the thread that reads the controller; the price is that Next and the one button answer "ok" with nothing playing and then come to nothing, with the reason in `error`. **Switching through the worker is held to one every 0.35 seconds** unless "faster" is on (Off never waits; a filter put on directly from the panel's list is not held back). **An upload is taken only when its work can be counted**: the count is a rule over tokens that refuses what it cannot bound (no `while`, only plainly counted `for` loops, no counter written in its body), not an estimate that guesses; it bounds honest work per pixel and proves nothing about cost on a GPU. **A pack file whose text matches a public snippet its credit does not name comes out, whoever recognises it**: two did, five more went as a precaution on the coordinator's decision, and the project's own filters stand in for a twirl, a ripple and a ring. I kept honest names that end like the player's (cell_size) allowed against the review's wider rule, because that rule would refuse ordinary files.

Amended on 2026-10-07: **the price is now paid by controllers only.** "Answered from memory" is decided by who calls, not by the route: a call the box makes for a controller (the devices `midi`, `osc`, `dmx` and `room`, `effects.CONTROLLERS`) asks the player nothing, as before; a paired device's request for Next, Previous, the one button's On or a preset of another effect asks the player first and gets 409 with the reason when no effect could go on. The panel already greyed those buttons out; this is for a panel whose state is a few seconds old and for a script. Cost: a panel's step waits for the player as putting an effect on always did, and the two kinds of caller can hear different answers to the same request.

## D56. A bundled filter's class is what the Pi 4 measured over a 1080p and a 720p clip; an upload keeps the count from its text

2026-10-05. Every bundled filter has a row in `PI4` (`pvj/effects.py`): the class, and the filter's own pass and the dropped frames a second over an H.264 clip of 1080 lines and one of 720 lines at 30 pictures a second, each at full size and with Half resolution, from the run in the journal ("effects on the Pi 4"). **light** holds 30 frames a second over the 1080 line clip at full size, **medium** over the 720 line clip only, **heavy** drops frames over the 720 line clip too. "Holds" is fewer than 0.5 dropped a second, the generators' line (D51, `shaderlive.HOLDS`), where the guard says "ok". The class is derived from the two full-size rates (`weigh`) and a test checks every row; nobody types a class by judgement. `weight` in `GET /api/effects` is that class for a bundled filter, with the numbers in `measured` and the largest clip it held at full size and at half in `holds` and `holds_half`; for an upload it is the count from the text, which every row still carries as `estimate.weight`.
Why: the rule is the generators' rule with two clip sizes in place of two drawing heights, so one word means one thing in both lists. The count (D55) was all there was before a board had run a filter, and the board contradicted it: its "light" filters took 8 to 21 ms and its "medium" ones 12 to 30.
What it gives on the Pi 4: 36 medium, 1 light (isf-duotone, near the line), no heavy. That is a true and nearly useless sorting, and it is kept because it is true: on this board the clip's size decides, and the card says so for each filter ("holds a 720p clip, a 1080p clip at half resolution").
Cost / rules out: the numbers are one board, one 75 Hz screen at 2560 x 1440, two clips, one run; a class near the line can flip. The Half resolution columns are recorded and do not enter the class. A copy of a bundled file uploaded under another name gets the count, not the row. **Not decided here:** whether Half resolution goes on by itself on a Pi 4. The numbers say it should for a clip above 720 lines; it changes the look of filters that count in pixels, so it is the owner's call.

## D57. Signal on every page: the area's colour belongs to the screen (ambience on Room is yellow), Set up is orange, and one list of screens feeds both the pictures and the checks

2026-10-05. Signal (D54) had been built and looked at on seven screens; effects, controller lights and most System pages had never been seen in it. The owner's order for the redesign is style first, then projection control, then performing, so the style was carried through every screen, page and state, with most care on Room and Projectors. Three lasting choices came out of it. The first two are **for the owner to confirm**.

- **Ambience on Room is Room yellow, not Shaders pink.** It starts shaders, and the Shaders page is pink, so pink was the other candidate. But D54's rule is that the area's colour says which screen is open: the title block, the open tab, what is chosen and what is on all take it. A pink block on the yellow screen would be a second area's colour there, and would read as "this part is another place" or as a state. Staff call it ambience and do not need to know what it is made of. So on Room its label and its button while it plays are yellow; the same rotation is green on Live (the Vibes button) and pink on its own page. One control, the colour of wherever it is shown.
- **Set up is orange `#ff9500`, not Figma's amber `#ffb020`.** The amber is 44 from Room yellow in RGB. As blocks side by side (a group warming up on Room, a "Set up" row on the index next to yellow) they read as two shades of one colour, and a state must never be taken for an area. `#ff9500` is 66 from Room yellow and 102 from Problem red: clear of both (much further from yellow would bring it under 100 from red), with the red channel kept full so it is still plainly a warm "not ready yet". Black on it is 8.94 to 1. Both theme files; the unit test holds Set up 60 from every area and every state 100 from every other.
- **One list of screens, pages and states (`tests/ui/signal-pages.js`), used by the screenshots and by the browser test.** Each entry brings a screen up (a projector in each power state, a question open, a change waiting to be confirmed, a presenter's phone, the pairing screen); the pictures are taken from it at 390 and 1366 px and for some in the light, and the test runs one check on each at 390 and 1366 px in the dark and 390 px in the light. The rules the check holds are written in `pvj/THEMES.md`: capitals only for titles, actions and short labels; a word in capitals never cut; numbers in the number face; 44 px to tap and 56 px where staff press on Room and Live; contrast 4.5 to 1; a state never in an area's colour; in the light no line in an area's colour; danger in red.

**The owner's word on the first pass, the same day:** "Signal looks good", and "feel free to use the lighter font style on smaller button, and you can use more modern UI components". So Black capitals are kept for screen titles, a panel's big name and the largest actions, and everything smaller is SemiBold or Bold in sentence case; small sets of choices are segmented controls, a slider's value is a block of its own, the page's message line is a toast above the tab bar, and on a phone each section starts under a rule. Navigation and what is on which screen were left alone: the owner is choosing a new overall layout separately. A bottom sheet for "More" was not built: it needs a way to close it, which is a new element in the page, and the hooks here are classes and attributes only; More opens in place as a grid.

Also settled, and written in THEMES.md rather than here because they follow from the rules: a state the default look says only in a line of text is a chip in Signal (a projector's power, a group's state), drawn by the stylesheet from an attribute; On and Off of a group are the two halves of one switch with the half in force filled, the way every switch in the look is drawn; what destroys or stops the room is a red block and what removes one thing a red outline.

Why this way: the style block cannot see whether a page was ever looked at, so the list is what makes "every page" checkable, and a page added later is pictured and checked by adding one entry. Hooks in the panel's code are classes and attributes only (`sent` on a card's first line that is a sentence, `del` on a delete button, `data-power` on a projector's row), which draw nothing in the default look.

Cost / rules out: the Signal pass of the browser test now opens about fifty screens three times, some minutes of CI. The chip beside a projector's name is drawn by the stylesheet (`::after`), so its words are in `app.css` and must follow the panel's own words for the states. Colour as the only sign is ruled out everywhere: each chip has its word. The Signal block has grown by about 120 lines, with the same duty as before: it follows the panel by hand.

## D58. Effects choose their own working size on a small board: Effect detail, Automatic on a Pi 4, from what the board measured

2026-10-05. The owner, after the first effects run (D56): "If running on a Pi4 or 3, let's auto-scale clips to 720p or 1080, whichever performs better overall." The on/off Half resolution of D55 becomes **Effect detail**, one setting for the box: Automatic, 540 lines, 720 lines or Full. With a number the filter works on a copy of the picture whose shorter side is at most that many lines, and the player scales the result to the screen; a clip at or below the cap is not scaled. **The rule for Automatic:** per board, the largest of the caps at which a filter held 30 frames a second over a 1080 line clip on that board ("held" is fewer than half a dropped frame a second, D51's line); the next cap down for a filter that did not hold there; the careful value for a filter nobody measured (an upload); nothing scaled on a board where nothing was measured. The table is `AUTO` in `pvj/effects.py`, and a Pi 4's row is made from the measured numbers (`PI4_720`) by that rule.
What the Pi 4 measured (the journal, "effect detail on the Pi 4"): at full size every filter drops frames over a 1080p clip; at 720 lines 36 of 37 hold with nothing dropped and isf-edge-blowout drops 1.6 a second; at 540 lines all hold. **So Automatic on a Pi 4 is 720 lines, isf-edge-blowout 540, an upload 540.** A Pi 3 would get 540 lines, unmeasured, and effects are still not offered there. A Pi 5 and x86 have Full as their default and Automatic scales nothing there: nothing was measured on them, and a default that softens the picture on a board that may not need it would be a guess.
Why 720 and not 540 for all: 540 holds with more room (the GPU 67 to 84 percent busy against 75 to 96), but it is the worse picture, and the owner asked for the better of the two that performs. 720 lines keep a pixel-counting filter's dots and lines at one and a half times their size over a 1080p clip; 540 doubles them.
Why the mix stays at the working size: mixing the filtered picture with the untouched clip at full size (a second pass) would keep the unfiltered share sharp at an amount between 0 and 1. It was built by hand and measured: the second pass costs 6 to 7 ms on this board, and the cheapest filter then drops frames at 720 and at 540 lines. So only **amount 0** is the clip at its own size: the text tells the player to leave the filter's pass out.
Why the shorter side: the player turns a picture before the stage an effect hooks, so "height" would mean another thing for a phone's upright video than for the same clip lying. The shorter side is the same number either way, it is what "720p" names, and the size lines do the arithmetic for both ways without being told which.
Kept: `"half": true` still works for one effect (540 lines at most) and `false`, which every saved preset holds, follows the box; the setting is `fx_detail` in the Shaders and Vibes section, absent while the board's own default is in force, so a box where nobody chose follows a better table later.
Cost / rules out: **the look.** Under a cap the picture is softer and fine patterns are larger, and that now happens by itself on a Pi 4 over any clip above 720 lines; the card says so in one line and Full is one tap away for the owner. The numbers are one board, one 75 Hz screen at 2560 x 1440, one clip, one afternoon; two filters run the GPU at 94 to 96 percent at 720 lines and will be the first to drop frames with the mapping on or on a hotter box. Automatic does not follow the guard: it is a table, not a controller, and an effect that turns out too heavy stays on until someone lowers the detail. At an amount between 0 and 1 the whole picture is the softer one. Nobody has looked at any of it on a display.
2026-10-08, added. The owner decided that edge glow (`fx-edge-glow.fs`) and corner colour tint (`isf-corner-color-tint.fs`) also work at 540 lines in Automatic on a Pi 4. This is an exception to the rule above, made for room, and not a new measurement: on 2026-10-05 both held the 1080p clip at 720 lines with nothing dropped, but with the GPU 94 to 96 percent busy, so the mapping on top would tip them; at 540 lines they dropped nothing with the GPU 77 to 78 percent busy. `PI4_720` keeps the measured numbers; the two are named in `PI4_HEADROOM` in `pvj/effects.py`, which only Automatic reads, and a test holds that every name in it is a measured filter that held at 720. So Automatic on a Pi 4 is now 720 lines for 34 of the 37, and 540 for these two, for isf-edge-blowout and for an upload. Cost: over a clip above 540 lines these two are softer and edge glow's outlines are twice as thick as at full size, where 720 lines gave one and a half times; the owner can still choose 720 lines or Full for the box, and that is not overridden. The six filters at 90 to 92 percent stay at 720 lines. The change was not run on the Pi.
## D59. A card that rebuilds itself gives the cursor back; the browser test waits for what the panel shows; each test job gives its own answer

2026-10-05. The test failures that came and went, pull request #86 (the journal entry of that day has the evidence).
Why: the mapper step's failure was the panel taking a name field away from the person typing in it, because every answer rebuilds the mapping card. Two patterns are now allowed for a card that redraws: do not redraw while a field in it has the cursor or a question waits (the Shaders page's `held`, the projectors' and the access card's checks), or redraw and give the cursor, the typed text and the place in it back (`keepCursor` in `pvj/web/app.js`, used by the mapping card, whose every change must be drawn at once). A card with neither is a defect. In `tests/ui/panel.test.js` a step that goes on after a change waits for the panel to have drawn it, not for the box to know it: the box knows a few milliseconds earlier. The `test` matrix in `.github/workflows/pvj.yml` runs with `fail-fast: false`.
Cost / rules out: `keepCursor` finds the control again by its id, so a control without an id loses the cursor as before, and a text being composed with an input method is cut off by the rebuild. A failing commit now costs both Pythons' full run instead of one. Job names are what the branch rule requires and were not changed.
After the independent review (the same day): the cursor comes back by id, **the typed text only into the same field**, that is one the card built from the same content as the old one; a field whose text the card keeps itself is marked `data-kept` and is never written to by `keepCursor`.

## D61. A MIDI controller can put a one-time pairing code on the box's display: off by default, a hold that ends, and never through the API

2026-10-07. The owner: "i want there to be a way to pull up a join code or owner code from a controller if i dont have a paired device with me". Until now the PIN was on the display only until the first device paired, and join codes came only from a paired device. Now two MIDI actions, `code_join` and `code_owner`, make the box draw a code on its own display. This hands out access to whoever can touch a controller, so each choice below is about that.

- **Its own code, not a join code and not the PIN.** One slot in `auth.py`, apart from `_joins`: single use, 120 seconds, 6 digits, a role of presenter (`join`) or full (`owner`). A join code could not be used: one exists per role and a new one replaces it (a hold would have ended the guest or presenter code people were using), `list_joins` gives its digits to the panel and the QR route, and it cannot be full. The PIN could not be used: it does not expire and does not work once.
- **"Join" pairs a presenter, not a guest.** A guest can only watch, which is no use to someone standing at the box who wants to run it; and whoever holds the controller already plays and mixes. What a presenter code adds for a stranger is that the phone keeps working after they leave, until it is removed.
- **Off by default, two switches, full access only.** `controller_code: {"enabled", "owner"}`, settings schema 14, the migration writes both off whatever the file held. `owner` cannot be on without `enabled`. Anything in the file that is not exactly `true` is off. `auth` reads the setting when a code is made and again when it is used, so switching off ends a code even if nothing else ran. The section is in boxcare's `NEVER`: not exported, refused in an import, off after a factory reset. It is not under `"auth"` because `_new_pin` replaces that whole section.
- **The gesture is a hold that ends: down for 3 to 10 seconds, then up; the request is made on the release.** Firing at 3 seconds while still held would let a stuck note (a note-on with no note-off) show a code. On release with an upper limit, a stuck note never asks, something lying on a pad never asks, a tap never asks, and a held control cannot repeat (it cannot go down twice). Cost: nothing tells the person that 3 seconds have passed. A program change has no release and a fader is not a button, so neither can have the action.
- **A press hides.** Any press of a control with either action ends a showing code at once, with no hold: ending is the safe direction. The hold that this press begins makes no new code.
- **No route makes a code.** Every network input (OSC, DMX, the schedule, a Room scene) and MIDI itself act by calling `Api.handle` as a presenter. So the two actions are not calls: the mapper plans a marker (`LOCAL_CODE`) that the hub hands to `controllercode.ControllerCodes` itself, and `Api.handle` has nothing for it. The one new route, `POST /api/access/controller` (full), takes the switches and Cancel and refuses every other key; it sits under `/api/access`, which the support tunnel refuses whole, and pairing is refused there too. The hub asks only for a controller whose ALSA card has a USB id, so a virtual or network MIDI port cannot ask.
- **The digits go to the display and nowhere else.** `auth.controller_digits` is read by `pinscreen` only. The API gives a full-access device the state without the digits; the log has no digits; no light is tied to the action. The display is not a request kept in `pinscreen` (`manual`): each tick asks `auth` whether a code is active and draws it over anything else, so it leaves the display when the code ends for any reason, and what a full-access device had put up comes back. `access_on_screen` counts it, so snapshots for lesser devices carry no text. The presenter kind has a QR code, the full access kind is text only, as the PIN is.
- **6 an hour, and the PIN's lockout.** Both kinds together, counted when made. A wrong guess counts against the same per-client and global lockout as a wrong PIN; a right code does not reset the count.
- **A default on one controller only.** The Launchpad Mini's one spare pad (top row, eighth; note 7) shows a presenter code. The nanoKONTROL2 has no spare button. The MIDI Mix's spare button is Solo, a shift that is held while playing, so it stays spare. No layout has the full access code.

**After the independent review (2026-10-07), four rules changed or were added.** (1) The hold is timed by when the press and the release were READ from the device: `MidiInput` now reads and stamps on one thread and acts on another, because the acting thread waits for the player and a tap could be handled seconds apart. A hold is also dropped when Learn starts or ends, when the map is saved or a MIDI switch changes, and when the reader's queue overflowed. (2) A control that sends a controller number counts only as a button: 127 down, 0 up, any other value ends the hold. Notes only would have been stricter and was what the review suggested first, but the nanoKONTROL2's buttons all send controller numbers, and it is one of the owner's three controllers. (3) A presenter paired with a controller code goes after 7 unused days, like a guest (a presenter from a join code still stays: an owner made that code); a full-access device paired so never takes one of the 20 places kept for the PIN. The code is used up after its device is in the list, not before. (4) The lockout is unchanged, and the display says "Pairing is locked for N minutes after wrong guesses"; when the lock outlasts a code, none is made. Also: a picture for a device without full access is checked again after it is taken and retaken without text if something secret appeared, and a picture with text taken while a code or the PIN was up is never handed from the cache to a lesser device (that second half was true of the PIN before this branch).

Cost / rules out: a reader and a worker thread per controller instead of one; a guesser on the network can still deny the code to the person at the box for 5 minutes at a time (said on the display, not prevented); a new settings schema, so a settings export from this version is refused by an older one and a rolled-back program needs the settings backup the updater keeps (it restores it). Tests that said "no schema change" by pinning the number 13 had to be touched. A lockout provoked by a guesser also blocks the person at the controller, who has no paired device to lift it; they wait it out. A code is refused when the player cannot draw (no player, no code), so the feature needs the player running. The check for USB reads `/proc/asound/card<n>/usbid`; if a kernel does not give it, the request is refused and only the log says why. If a held button's release is lost (unplugged mid-hold), that hold is forgotten. Two people at two controllers share one code and one limit. Nothing here has run on the Pi or with a real controller.

## D60. A theme is a bounded set of tokens the owner can add through the panel; never CSS; contrast is enforced

2026-10-05. The owner is building the panel's UI in Figma to experiment: "Please make it so I may build out new 'themes' and layouts to try and maybe even share across devices." Layouts are designed there and built by hand afterwards. Themes are the half the box can take as data. A theme was colours and the name of a style (D54), and the style's shapes and type were fixed CSS. So:

- **A theme may set design tokens, a fixed list with a range or a set for each** (`DESIGN` in `pvj/themes.py`): corner radius of controls and of panels (0 to 24), border width (0 to 4), density (compact, regular, roomy: one factor), the height of a control (44 to 72) and of a large one (56 to 72), the case of titles, the weight of titles and of text (the weights the shipped Archivo carries), the fonts of titles, text and numbers (only those on the box, and the device's own), and whether the open tab and a primary action are filled or outlined. Whole numbers by type (`True` and `8.0` are refused), words from lists, an outline only with a border.
- **Never CSS, and nothing of the file's text reaches the page.** `/theme.css` gets `--tk-*` variables whose values are numbers the box formatted or strings from tables in `themes.py` (a font stack, `uppercase`, `var(--ink)`). The Signal block reads each as `var(--tk-..., <its own value>)`. A theme that sets none is Signal exactly: the block with the fallbacks substituted back was compared with master's and no line differs, and a test holds the fallbacks equal to the defaults and the two lists of names equal. The built-in theme files are untouched. The default look does not read the tokens.
- **Contrast is enforced where a theme is validated, not advised.** Every pairing of text and ground the look draws is computed, and one under 4.5 to 1 refuses the theme with the pair named ("Text on the Room colour is 2.1 to 1; it needs 4.5"). A state colour within 60 in RGB of an area colour is a warning. The floors of D57 hold: a control is never under 44 px, what staff press never under 56.
- **One validator for every way in.** `themes.checked` reads an untrusted file (16 KB, UTF-8, no key twice, whole numbers only, only the known keys, id and name by whole match) and is what the Look page's "Add a theme", a settings import and the loader of the add-on folder all call. A theme put in the folder by hand that fails it is skipped, where before only its form was looked at.
- **Added through the panel, kept in the add-on folder** (`<state>/addons/themes/<id>.json`): the name comes from the validated id, the folder is fixed, nothing is read or written through a link, a file is written whole and renamed. At most 16. A bundled id cannot be taken; the same id again replaces the owner's own. Removing the look in use puts the box back on Dark stage first. Full access only, and adding and removing are refused through the remote-support tunnel (they write a file on the box); choosing a look and saving one as a file are not.
- **They travel with the settings.** A settings export carries the added themes whole under `themes` in the envelope, only when there are any, so a box without them writes the file every earlier version reads. An import checks each with the same code, refuses the whole file if one fails, and writes them after the settings are safe. A factory reset removes them (it used to leave the folder alone).
- **"Save this look as a file"** gives the look in use as the same JSON; a bundled look gets an id of its own (`my-signal`), and a Signal theme is written with every token, so the file is the list of what can be changed.
- **`tools/figma-theme.py`** turns tokens exported from Figma into a theme file, with the box's validator and the contrast report. It knows the names of the Figma file "nxlx.mastercontrol Signal UI kit" (`colour/page`, `area/live`, `shape/corner`, `font/words`), the list first agreed (`color/page`, `radius/control`) and the code names of `app.css`. It is not in a release (a release is `pvj`, `bin`, `install`).
- **The Look page shows each look as a small picture drawn from its own tokens.** Colours are set through the script after a whole match, fonts from a list in `app.js`.

Why this way: the owner wants to try looks freely and move them between boxes, and a look is loaded by every paired device before pairing. Free CSS, a font or an image in a theme would make every theme file, and every settings file from elsewhere, a way to put content into the panel. A bounded list costs expressiveness and gives a file that can be accepted from anyone. Variables with the style's own values behind them meant Signal did not have to be rebuilt, and that "unchanged" could be shown by substitution. Refusing unreadable text at the gate is cheaper than finding it on a wall in a dark room.

Cost / rules out: a token is a promise the Signal block must keep everywhere; a new part of the panel written with a literal `3px` or `44px` will ignore a theme (THEMES.md says to write `var(--tk-...)`). The picture on the Look page is a second, small rendering of a theme in `app.js` and can drift from the stylesheet. The Look page now asks the box for the two fonts in the default look too, which D54 said the default look never does: the pictures are in each look's own type. No font was added: the list is Archivo, JetBrains Mono and the device's own. Density is one factor for gaps and padding, not a separate scale per part. An outlined open tab is not a block of the area's colour, which the Signal pass of the browser test asserts for the built-in Signal only. A settings file that carries themes is refused by a box older than this (it does not know the `themes` part). An add-on theme dropped in by hand that fails contrast, which loaded before, is now skipped. Layout stays in code. The number is 60: #87 took D58 and #86 took D59 while this was open.

After the independent review of #88 (2026-10-05), which found no way to put CSS, a header or a path through a theme and four low things, the decision gained these: **an accent chosen under Look is held to the contrast rule too** (the API and a settings import refuse a faint one, the page offers only swatches that pass, and a faint one already stored is dropped at draw time with a line on the Look page, never an error); **a theme file that is not used is logged and shown to the owner**; **two files with one id are one theme**, and all go when it is removed; **a theme may not take the name of a bundled look**; **the settings import reads each theme with `themes.checked` itself** (written out as text again), so there is truly one reader; a theme is not added or removed while an import or a reset runs; removing a theme takes the file first and changes the look after; and a guest or presenter gets the looks' names and styles, not their tokens. Cost: a settings file exported before this with an accent that fails on its theme is now refused on import (choose another accent or Default on the old box, or edit the file); on Light only one of the six swatches passes.

## D62. NDI comes in through its own unprivileged service that loads the owner's copy of the runtime; raw UYVY through a pipe to the one mpv; the runtime is never in the repository, the image or an upload
Why: the owner asked that "the NDI playback module" run smoothly, and only its manifest existed. NDI's runtime is closed source and reads packets from the network, and neither mpv nor Debian's ffmpeg can receive NDI. So: `pvj-ndi.service` (account and group `pvj-ndi`, not in group `pvj`, so it cannot reach the player's control socket, the PIN or the settings; no capabilities or devices; network limited to private, link-local and multicast addresses) loads `libndi.so.6` with `ctypes`, lists senders, and writes whole UYVY frames (the library's "fastest" format, newest frame wins) into a pipe in `/run/pvj-ndi` that the player reads with the raw video demuxer, the way the capture input already does. The panel asks it over a socket that answers only the panel's account, plays by an id the helper makes from the name, and reloads the pipe when a sender changes size. Names, addresses and frame sizes from the network are checked against fixed bounds before use. The runtime is fetched by the owner (NDI publishes no separate Linux runtime; it is inside the SDK, behind its licence) and put in `/opt/pvj-ndi` by a root command, never by the installer on its own, never by a panel upload (a library is a program; an upload would let a full-access phone run code). The page carries NDI®, the trademark sentence and a link to ndi.video, as NDI's licensing page requires. Rejected: NDI inside the player or panel, a helper under the panel's account, a compiled helper (breaks standard library only), our own ffmpeg build, y4m. Cost: no sound yet; a second or two of no picture when a sender changes size; interlaced senders refused; a keyboard or SSH is needed once per box; schema 15 (`ndi.addresses`; it was written as 14 and D61 on the branch, and took the next numbers when the controller code, #93, merged first with those). Not confirmed: the SDK's License Agreement and its list of exclusions were not read (they are only in the download), so whether this box may use the royalty-free SDK at a venue is the owner's to check. Never run with a real library, a real sender or mpv; see [pvj/NDI.md](../pvj/NDI.md).


After the independent review and a run against the real library on a Mac (2026-10-07), D62 gained these. **A source is a name on a machine**: the id is made from the name and the announcing host, so another machine with the same name cannot take over a choice; a sender whose address changes is chosen again. **The list is sorted before it is cut**, keeps the source on screen and senders at added addresses, and says when it was cut. **Only progressive frames are shown**; all three interlaced frame types are refused alike (the "fastest" format always allows fields, so the library does not de-interlace). **A still picture is not a lost sender** (the real Test Patterns source sends one frame and then only sound). **The later choice of what is on the screen always stands**: everything that loads or clears the player cancels a connecting source, and waits while the NDI pipe is being loaded. **The panel treats the helper as hostile**: one deadline for a whole exchange (in `netd.exchange`, shared by all helpers' clients), a bound on nesting, every field checked again, and no helper answer can stop the panel starting. **Off means no NDI code running**: the helper ends when the module goes off. **The helper is bounded** (512 MB, 96 tasks, three cores, an 8 MB `/tmp`; memory and cores chosen by reasoning, not measured; the tasks were 64 until 2026-10-12, when a measurement by hand on a Pi 4 gave 35 with one connection and 46 to 47 with the sound source's connection beside it, plus about five threads of the helper's own, D87; a box that is set up for NDI gets the new unit at its next install or update) and cannot reach 127.0.0.1; it CAN reach the box's own LAN address, which lies inside the private ranges it is allowed (corrected 2026-10-11 after the review of `b46410c`: see the trust boundary below and in SECURITY.md). **The licence**: the SDK's agreement was read (not accepted); its free licence does not cover, among other things, embedded devices running Linux with a fixed function, so whether a Pi running this image may use the library is an open legal question for the owner, and the page and manual say so where the install steps are. See [pvj/NDI.md](../pvj/NDI.md).

**The owner's answers of 2026-10-08, and what D62 is now.** (1) **The helper is opt-in per box**, in his words "install the helper only when NDI is wanted; one extra command at the box, alongside the runtime install". This replaces the part of D62 above in which the installer put the helper on every box, idle, and added avahi where it was missing. A plain `install.sh` (also `--offline`, an update from the panel, and the image build) now leaves a box without NDI as it is on master: no `pvj-ndi` unit, account, group or folder, no avahi package added, the units of the panel and the player unchanged, nothing of NDI on the network. The one command is `sudo pvj-ndi-runtime install <the NDI SDK>`: the library first, then root's setup in `pvj/ndisetup.py` (avahi if missing, the account and group, the unit from `pvj/systemd/`, two drop-ins that give the panel and the player the extra group, reload, enable, start, and a restart of the panel and the player). Why that command and not a flag on the installer: a box made from the image or updated from the panel has no installer on it, only `pvj/` and `bin/`. Why drop-ins: systemd does not start a unit that names a group the box does not have, so the group cannot be named in units that every box gets. The panel's drop-in is the mark of the opt-in; an install or update that finds it writes the unit and the drop-ins again through the same code (one writer) and keeps the helper enabled, so an opted-in box stays opted in. `pvj-ndi-runtime remove` opts out: the helper, its unit, the drop-ins and the library go; the account, the group and the avahi packages stay (avahi may have been there before), and the command says so. `install.sh --uninstall` removes the unit and drop-ins and leaves the library unless `--purge`. A box that is not set up says so on the NDI page in plain words with the command, and its routes answer without trying a socket. Cost: opting in restarts the player (the screen goes dark for a moment); `/usr/local/bin/pvj-ndi-runtime` and the program files are on every box. (2) **Remote support: the same as Streams.** A full-access support session may change the NDI addresses exactly as it may change Streams. That was already so; nothing changed, and a test now pins that the two stay the same. (3) **Sound: later**, after the picture has been measured on the Pi. The input is picture only and every document says so. (4) **The licence.** The owner accepts the NDI SDK licence himself, for testing on his own test Pi only; whether a venue box may use the library he decides later, and it stays his question to settle with NDI. The library is never in the repository or the image; `pvj/NDI.md` gives the agreement, and what NDI's documentation pages say, in this project's words only, because the SDK treats its text and documents as confidential; the one sentence kept as NDI writes it is the trademark sentence NDI asks to be shown. Not confirmed: none of the opt-in has run on a Pi or on any Linux box with a display; its root part runs under the CI runner's systemd without a library (`tests/real_install_test.sh`), and with fake commands in `tests/test_ndi_setup.py`.

**After the independent review of the opt-in (2026-10-08; nothing high, 2 medium, 6 low, all fixed with a test each).** The setup now looks at everything that can refuse it before it changes anything (the install folder, the unit's template, an account `pvj-ndi` that is already there), and the installer writes the install folder one way (no slash at the end, no doubled one). A library that is refused leaves no `/opt/pvj-ndi`. A step that fails after the files were written undoes them on a box that was not set up before, so a failed setup leaves no mark. The command watches the helper for three seconds and says whether it stayed up; one that did not is left in place and is an error. An account `pvj-ndi` that is root, shares its number or has a login shell is refused. `remove` goes on past a file it cannot remove. **Rollback:** the helper's unit has `ConditionPathExists=` on its program, so a rollback to a release from before the NDI input skips it and does not fail it every two seconds for ever, and the updater restarts a running helper with the panel and the player. **A helper's unit without the mark is removed by the installer** (chosen over a line in the device steps: it is five lines of shell behind the same test as the mark, it also clears up a setup that was cut short, and a box nobody opted in should not keep a helper because of how it got there). Still not run on a Pi or on any Linux box with a display.

**After the second read of those fixes (2026-10-08; nothing high, 2 medium, 2 low, notes; all fixed with a test each).** Writing the three files is now inside the part of the setup that is undone, for any error, and a link or a folder in the place of one of them is refused before anything is changed. The group `pvj-ndi` is checked like the account: not gid 0, a number of its own, no members, nobody else's own group. **Members are refused** on purpose: the panel and the player reach the helper's folder through their units, so a name in the group's list is an account nobody decided to let in. In the installer, removing a helper that nobody set up can no longer stop an install (it says WARNING and goes on), looks for the mark once more right before it removes, and also removes the link that enabled the unit. **A link where the mark goes is removed by the installer**, the link itself and never what it points to: systemd reads a drop-in through a link, so leaving it would give the panel the group on a box the installer treats as not set up, and `rm` on a link cannot touch its target. The updater restarts the network helper and the NDI helper in two calls. A `.` is refused as a folder name in the install folder. **Left as it is, on purpose:** the unit's restart every 2 seconds without a limit. The helper does not end when the library is missing or does not load (it stays up and says so); it ends when the module is switched off with the library loaded, where the quick clean start is wanted, and on a crash in the library, which an exit status cannot tell apart from anything else in closed code. `pvj/NDI.md` says what that costs.

**The first run on a Pi 4 (2026-10-08; the owner's test Pi, Debian 13, mpv 0.40, by the coordinator over SSH, nobody at the monitor).** Now shown, once: a plain install leaves nothing of NDI on the box; the opt-in command works on the board (the library for aarch64 is picked from the SDK folder, the account and group are made, the unit runs, the panel gets its extra group from the drop-in); the library loads inside the sandbox and finds senders through avahi from inside the unit; a progressive UYVY source is received and played by mpv through the raw pipe (1764 x 992 at 30 for 61 seconds, 0 frames dropped by the helper, 1 by mpv; right way up, no shear, plausible colours in one preview picture); the unit's network rules stop a connection to the internet from the helper's account while the sender stays reachable. **Still not shown:** sound (not built), latency, anything by eye on the monitor, 60 frames a second, 1080p60, NDI HX, an interlaced sender, a run of five minutes or more, and device steps N8, N11 and N13 to N18; the figures are one short run from a sender on Wi-Fi. **Three things it found, and what was decided.** (1) The SDK's `libndi.so.6` is a link and was refused: a link given as the file is now followed, only to a plain name in the same folder and at most two deep; everything else that is a link stays refused and the open still follows none. (2) Two device steps could not be run as written (systemd takes no `localhost` in a list of allowed addresses given on one `-p`; the image has no `socat`): rewritten. (3) A still source left mpv at frame 0, idle, with no snapshot to be had. Decided: the helper writes a frame that nothing follows once more after 0.3 seconds, on the reading that mpv shows a frame only once it holds the next (remembered from its source, not read again, **not tried**), and a snapshot the player cannot make of an NDI source is answered in plain words (409) and no longer as a fault of the player (503). Cost: a pause of a third of a second in a sender doubles one frame. If step N12 shows the picture is still not on the screen, the repeat is the wrong cure and comes out again.

**Sound (2026-10-08; the owner: "go ahead with sound", after he had looked at the moving source on the Pi's monitor and said "The pi looked good", which is the first look by eye, at a moving source only).** Built and measured on the dev Mac; **nobody has heard it**. What was weighed. (a) *A second pipe of raw samples beside the pipe of raw frames.* Least code, and wrong for this helper: two raw streams have no times, so the player tells the time by counting, and this helper drops a frame whenever the screen is slow and writes a still frame twice; each of those would shift the picture against the sound by a whole frame and never give it back. Nothing could be tuned to cure it. (b) *One pipe that carries both, with a time on each frame and block, in a container the helper writes itself.* More code in the helper, one reader, and the player keeps the two together. (c) *Leave sound to the room's own path.* No code; the owner asked for sound. **Chosen: (b), as Matroska**: uncompressed video with the FourCC UYVY and 32-bit float PCM, one small cluster for each frame and each block, a segment without a length. Why Matroska and not NUT: mpv reads Matroska with its own reader (`demux/demux_mkv.c`, read for this at v0.35.0, v0.37.0 and v0.40.0: all three take `V_UNCOMPRESSED` with a four-byte colour space as raw video, `A_PCM/FLOAT/IEEE`, and clusters and a segment of unknown length "for streaming"), needing no libavformat probing, and writing what that reader needs is about forty lines with no checksums. The frame is written after its few bytes of heading from the buffer it is already in: no copy was added to the picture path. **A source without sound, and every source with sound switched off, keeps the bare frames and raw options that ran on the Pi**, so the picture path that was measured is unchanged and the switch is also the way back to it. **Whose clock:** the helper's. Measured against real senders, the time the library gives a block of sound is its sending time and jitters by tens of milliseconds around blocks of exactly 100, and a still frame carried a time more than two hours older than its sound; so frames are placed where they arrived and sound by counting samples, held within 0.3 s of the clock (behind: start again at the clock; ahead: leave a block out). The sender's times are not used, which also makes absurd ones harmless. **Level:** NDI's 1.0 is a reference level, and the SDK's header advises 20 dB of headroom when receiving into a full-scale format; one constant, `AUDIO_HEADROOM_DB = 20`. Test Patterns' tone peaks at exactly 1.0 and comes out at -20.00 dBFS. **Channels:** the first two (NDI gives no layout to mix down by); a mono source as one. **Changes** of rate or channels, and sound that starts late, end the stream as "changed" and the panel opens the source again, as for a change of size. **Hostile input:** every figure of a block is bounded before a byte is read; a bad block is counted and left out; NaN, infinity and absurd samples become silence; at most one second of sound ever waits for the player. **The setting:** on by default (the owner asked for sound; a source's sound belongs with its picture as a clip's does; volume and mute apply), stored as `ndi.sound` with no new schema. **The sandbox is unchanged:** the helper opens no sound device; mpv plays. **The connection** is given back to the library only when both the picture's and the sound's threads are out of it. Cost: a second or two without picture, once, when a source starts or stops sending sound; the conversion is a Python loop over every sample (77 ms for ten seconds of stereo on the Mac; unknown on a Pi 4). **Not shown:** anything heard; delay; lip sync and drift; the level of a program sender (Scan Converter sent no sound when tried; if such senders put full scale at 1.0 their sound will be 20 dB quiet, and the constant is what changes); what mpv does at a left-out block or at filled-in silence; mpv 0.35 and 0.40 running it (CI's mpv reads the stream; it plays to no device). Device step N10.

**The evening of 2026-10-08 on the Pi 4, and two things decided by it.** (1) **The repeat of a frame that nothing follows is taken out again**, as the paragraph before last promised for this case. A camera pointed at the monitor showed a still source's one frame on the screen, on the build without the repeat: mpv does draw it, and the reading that it would not (made from memory of mpv's source, not tried) was wrong for this box. The repeat changed the picture path for no shown benefit and never ran on a box. The snapshot of a still source fails all the same while its picture is on the screen; the panel's answer stays the plain 409 and its words no longer suggest that the picture may be missing. Why mpv refuses is open (step N12). (2) **A live source plays at speed 1.** The Mix speed applied to NDI: at 1.86 mpv dropped some 4800 frames in five minutes. A source that arrives in real time cannot be played faster than it comes, and the capture input and streams had the same fault unnoticed. So when NDI, the capture input or a stream is played, the panel sets the player's speed to 1 and keeps the Mix speed (which may be changed meanwhile: stored, not applied); the next clip gets it back. Done in the panel, in the one place every play passes (`_started_playing`), and not as a per-file option of the player, because the older mpv on Bookworm takes no per-file options and the panel must know the state anyway to show it. Cost: one more command to the player at each change between a live source and a clip. **Also shown that evening, once each:** a sender that changes size carries on (twelve changes); five minutes at 1764 x 992 with 28 of 6228 frames dropped by the helper and 7 by mpv; mpv's clock, which counts bare frames, was 98 seconds behind the wall clock after five minutes because the Wi-Fi sender delivered about 20 frames a second and announced 30, which is the same reason the stream with sound carries real times.

**After the independent review of the sound (2026-10-08; 2 high, 4 medium, 8 low, all answered).** Decided by it: (1) **The helper never decides, by its answer alone, what the player parses.** The pipe is read as Matroska only when the owner's sound switch is on AND the helper says the pipe carries sound; with the switch off it is bare frames whatever the helper answers. For the Matroska load the player is narrowed to the two decoders the stream needs (`vd=rawvideo,-`, `ad=pcm_f32le,-`, no subtitle track, no ordered chapters, no cover art from beside the pipe, no fonts from the stream), set as properties before the load and taken back before the next. Rejected: giving them as per-file options of `loadfile` on mpv 0.38 and later, which would undo themselves, because it was not established that mpv refuses a load whose per-file option it cannot take (if it only logs it, the stream would play without the narrowing), whereas a property that is refused is an error the player sees and the load is not made; and CI's mpv 0.37 then runs the same path as the Pi's 0.40. **Accepted cost, stated plainly:** with sound on, mpv's Matroska reader runs on bytes a taken-over helper chooses, which bare frames never allowed; that is what sound costs, and switching sound off is the way back. (2) **A block of sound is at most a quarter of a second**, and the player's queue is sized from that (one second of picture, 32 to 256 MiB). Rejected: taking blocks up to a second and making the timing cope, because the sound in the pipe trails the picture by a block's length and the player would have to hold up to a second of uncompressed picture (250 MB at 1080p60) on a Pi; the real senders measured sent 0.1 s at most. A longer block is refused in words on the page, where before it was played as silence for ever. (3) **The sound's count is brought back to the clock, not just bounded.** More than 0.04 s ahead (proved by one block, since a block cannot arrive before its last sample exists): that much sound is cut. Behind for every block of a second: that much silence is put in. Real samples either way, so the times in the pipe stay one after another. Rejected: nudging the times a little at a time, because the times would then overlap or leave holes for the player to interpret, and resampling in the helper, which is the right answer to a sender's clock that differs and was not built; the cost of what was built is a tick at each correction, nobody has heard how bad. (4) The helper plays no sound unless the panel says so in so many words (the default is the panel's). (5) The speed hold of a live source is taken and given back under one lock with the speed request, ends on Stop and when the status finds nothing live playing, and ends only when the player has taken the Mix speed.

**The one pipe is handed from source to source in a made order (2026-10-12).** Since M3 the pipe is root's, made once, and every source goes through it; before, a pipe per source kept the last source's reader and the next source's writer apart without anyone having decided it. The Pi showed the same day what that costs: a source that changed size was opened again on the same pipe and lost the player at once, twice out of twice, and was gone without a word. Decided now: before a source is opened the panel tells the player to let go of the pipe if the pipe is what it loaded last (the player did not let go by itself on the Pi); the helper's open holds the new writer back until it has seen the pipe without a reader, and answers the panel only then; the panel loads after the answer. Why the helper's look and not a word from the panel alone ("the player was told"): mpv answers a command before it has let go of the stream, so a word would still leave a window, and the pipe itself says when the old reader has gone. Why in the helper: it needs nothing the helper cannot already do (open and close the pipe it may write) and gives it nothing new; the pipe stays root's and is never made or unlinked by the helper. Both waits are bounded (1.5 seconds for mpv's word that it is idle, 2 for the helper's look), and when either runs out nothing is written: the open is refused with the reason, the page shows it, and the watch tries again. There is no path left on which a writer opens over a reader that was not confirmed gone. And the panel, not the helper, decides what a lost reader means: while the NDI pipe is what the panel loaded last, "the player stopped reading" is answered by showing the source again, not by taking it for something else having been played. Cost: the screen is black from the press (or from the change of size) until the new source's first frames, where before the last frame may have stayed; and a press of a source that turns out not to be on the network takes the playing NDI source off for the few seconds the watch needs to bring it back. Not confirmed with a real mpv; `pvj/NDI.md` says what to try.

**Brought up to master on 2026-10-11** (the panel of D72, the roles of D80, the crossfades of D71, HTTPS and owner recovery): what changed in the decision.

- **No settings schema change.** The branch had moved the schema to 15 for one key, `ndi`. The house rule since is to add keys an older release ignores: `ndi` is now read with defaults everywhere (`ndi.saved_addresses`, a missing or damaged section counts as empty, sound on), the migration is gone and the schema stays 14. Checked with master's own `pvj/settings.py` against a file this branch wrote with an address and the sound switch: master loads it, keeps the key, and a file master wrote loads here. The test Pi's schema 15 file from the trial of 2026-10-08 is only a backup now; neither master nor this branch loads it.
- **Roles (D80).** `GET /api/ndi` is the Operator's (`live`, held to no legacy list: a controller or a support session below full gets nothing new); `POST /api/ndi` (addresses, the sound switch) is the Owner's and a full support session's, as Streams (the owner's choice above). `{"ndi": id}` on `/api/play` is refused to a Guest with controls open, as a stream is: a source on the network is not a pad already on the box. No pad can hold an NDI source.
- **The panel.** One Setup page, "NDI input", beside Streams under Show tools (role `op`), drawn by `pagePart()` like every Setup page: the senders with Play, the Sound switch, the addresses. Choosing a source stays on that page because that is where Streams are chosen on master too; the Play library holds clips, pads and shaders. The strip's Speed says "(live: 1.00x)" while a live source plays (the Mix picture screen has no speed slider any more).
- **The locks (D71's order).** The NDI play now decides and loads under the player's lock like every other play (`Api._as_newest` with a `ready` check), so the helper's "screen" lock is only the ticket's leaf. `ndi` (place after `capture`) and `speed.hold` (the hold at 1 and the one write of the speed) have places in `pvj/locks.py`; the checker in the tests holds them to it. The player knows which pipe it plays (`Player.pipe_is`): the capture helper is stopped when the NDI pipe loads and the other way round, where before `pipe_playing` alone would have left the other helper writing into a pipe nobody reads.
- **The licence, the owner's decision (2026-10-11, his words):** "We aren't going to worry about NDI's SDK terms regarding embedded linux appliances. This project so far is merely for personal and proof-of-concept use and exploration. When the project is ready for mass production, then i will contact NDI." So: the question of section 1b is settled for now as personal and proof-of-concept use; he contacts NDI before any mass production. Unchanged by this: the box never bundles, downloads or installs the runtime by itself; accepting the licence and running the SDK's installer stay his act. He also said: "i want pads to hold NDI sources" (not built here; a hand-over for it is in the journal of 2026-10-11), "sound is off by default, but allow for easy turning on" (the one switch on the NDI page), "guests cannot see NDI page in setup" (so built: the row is the Operator's and `GET /api/ndi` refuses a Guest).
- **Sound off by default** (the coordinator's call after the independent review of `b46410c`, 2026-10-11, by the owner's standing direction that technical defaults are the coordinator's): the sound is the one path never run on hardware, and the one that hands mpv's Matroska reader to whoever controls the helper. `SOUND_DEFAULT` is False; the page says "Sound from NDI is new and has not been heard on a box yet". Guests see no NDI page, as built.
- **The pipe is root's** (the review of `b46410c`, M3). The helper used to make the pipe in its own folder, and between the panel's look at it and the player's opening of it (by name) a taken-over helper could have swapped it for a link to anything the player can read. Now `pvj-ndi-runtime install` writes tmpfiles lines (`/etc/tmpfiles.d/pvj-ndi.conf`) that make `/run/pvj-ndi-pipe` (0750 root:pvj-ndi) and the pipe in it (0660 root:pvj-ndi) once, at boot and at the install; the helper writes the pipe by its group and can neither unlink, rename nor replace it; the panel checks the pipe's owner (root) and that the folder is root's and writable by nobody else; the helper never unlinks a pipe (it was its own "remove"). Where nobody made one (a desk, the tests) the helper still makes one in its own folder.
- **The trust boundary of the helper, written down** (M3). What a taken-over helper holds: a client's place on the LAN towards the panel (as an unpaired stranger: /api/hello, /api/pair with its throttle, the https probe), towards OSC when that module is on and its D78 locks are open, towards sync when it is on, and towards every sender and box on the network; the pipe's contents (what the player decodes: raw video of a size the panel set, or, with sound on, raw video and float PCM through mpv's Matroska reader); the system D-Bus as any unprivileged account; 512 MB and three cores. What it does not hold: the player's control socket, the settings, the PIN, the media, root's helpers, the internet, any device, any name in root's pipe folder. **Denying the box's own address** is not static in a unit (the address is not known when the unit is written and may change): the honest rule chosen is to write this boundary down, to recommend the OSC locks (D78) on a box with NDI, and to try on the Pi an `IPAddressDeny=` of the box's own addresses written by `pvj-ndi-runtime refresh` at each install and update (a stale line denies a stray address, which is harmless; a new address is open until the next refresh), rather than teach the panel, OSC and sync to refuse peers at the box's own addresses, which costs a list of addresses at every connection in three listeners and refuses the owner's own `curl` from an SSH session. `SystemCallFilter` is not added blind; it is a device row.
- **Crossfades (D71).** A live source is cut to, never crossfaded in (as the capture input and a stream are): `_as_newest` ends a transition on its way. A clip after it may crossfade from it, since the still is of whatever is on the screen; where the player cannot copy the picture (a still NDI source on the Pi 4) the clip is cut to, as the transition's own rule says. An effect over an NDI source is an effect over the player's picture like any other; untested on hardware.

**Pads hold NDI sources, and the box looks where the owner says (2026-10-11; built on the branch, never run on a box).** The owner's words: "i want pads to hold NDI sources", and later the same day "make sure to incorporate the NDI group and discovery policies. i can lock the NDI network with a group policy." (1) **A pad stores the id alone** (`{"label", "file": "", "ndi": id}`), a third kind beside the clip and the shader of D73; no schema change; master loads the file and sees an empty pad. (2) **Allowed sources** live at `ndi.sources` (`{"id", "name", "from"}`, at most 32). **An Operator allows a source by putting one that is on the network NOW on a pad**; chosen because he may already play it by hand and edits pads (D80), so the list adds only that the choice lasts, and because a source that is away, a typed name and a typed address are all refused. **Only the Owner removes one**, and only the Owner manages addresses, groups and discovery servers. (3) **A pad follows a name on a machine**: the id is made of the announced name and the announcing address, so a same-named sender elsewhere is refused (and named in the refusal), which is stricter than the hand-over's "follows a name" and costs a new choice when a sender's address changes. It is not proof of who sends: names are announced without authentication and an address can be taken; the editor, the manual, NDI.md and SECURITY.md say so and say to give the sender's address on a network the owner does not control. Two senders under one id are refused, never picked between; the live row's name and machine are compared as well as the 48-bit id. (4) **A press** checks at once (module, allowed, helper, on the list now) and then goes through `Api.play_ndi` and nothing else. (5) **Guests** are refused in the gate, in a scene and in the handler, and get no names with the pads; **MIDI, OSC and DMX** are answered at once and the source connects on its own thread, a refusal kept for the pads; **a Room scene and the schedule** wait; `LEGACY_LIVE` is unchanged; autostart does not take an NDI pad. (6) **Groups** go into the finder's `p_groups`, kept as typed; **discovery servers** go into NDI's own file (`ndi.networks.discovery`) in a folder root makes for the panel's account, which the helper reads and cannot write, and a change starts the helper again. (7) **No mDNS switch**: NDI documents no setting that stops a finder using mDNS, and a finder with a discovery server uses both; the page says that groups and servers are not a password, and that the real narrowing is giving every SENDER the server. (8) The ctypes structures and signatures were checked against the owner's copy of the SDK (6.3.2): all match. Rejected: a pad that follows the name wherever it is announced (the trap, made the rule); a free string on a pad; the helper writing its own configuration; a switch that does nothing. Cost: a restart of the helper for a discovery change; boxes set up before this need their set-up written again and a restart; a DHCP change breaks a pad. See [pvj/NDI.md](../pvj/NDI.md).

## D63. The owner's answers of 2026-10-08: layout C is built, the transport sits at the bottom, and nine smaller choices

2026-10-08. The owner answered the open questions on the page made for him in the night (https://claude.ai/artifact/KiNYctjMGB55E8iGsM8e8A). Each is his choice; where a recommendation was given and he chose otherwise, that is said.

- **The layout: build the real panel from the layout C click-through** (https://claude.ai/artifact/QfEhxoRxV7pbVHi2dCxzFW): four areas (Play, Shape, Room, Setup), a side menu from 600 wide, one screen one job. The first pull request is the shell only, every page moved in unchanged, and he looks before it merges.
- **The transport strip sits at the bottom at every width**, as drawn: on a phone it shows Previous, Stop, Next and Blackout and opens in place for the rest. Not at the top, and not always open on a phone.
- **Today's panel is repaired first** at 320 wide, on a sideways phone and from 600 to 899 (step 1 of the resizing study), with a width sweep in the browser test. The branch that does it writes its own decision.
- **Signal (D57): both colours stay.** Ambience on Room in Room yellow, Set up in orange `#ff9500`, not Figma's amber. The active cyan (D54) was not asked this time.
- **Effect detail (D58): edge glow and corner colour tint step down to 540 lines** in Automatic on a Pi 4. The branch that does it adds to D58.
- **A code from a controller (D61) keeps pairing a presenter**, and the Launchpad Mini keeps it on its last spare pad by default. He asked what the owner switch is: `controller_code.owner`, off by default, which lets a mapped control with the action `code_owner` show a one-time code that pairs a full-access device; no bundled layout has that action.
- **NDI (D62).** He accepts the NDI SDK licence himself, to test on his own test Pi only, and decides about the venue later (his note: "proceed regardless"). Running the SDK installer and answering it stays his act. The library goes to the test Pi only, never into the repository or the image. **The helper is installed only when NDI is wanted** (one more command at the box), not idle on every box as first built. **A support session may change NDI settings as it may change Streams today**; the recommendation was to refuse it, and he chose the same rule as Streams. **Sound waits** until picture has been measured on the Pi.
- **Agents' pushes: no rule is added.** When the permission check refuses an agent's push, the coordinating session pushes, as in the night.
- **Deploys to the test Pi: he says so in the session** ("deploy to the Pi"), rather than a standing rule.
- **CI's install step: he adds the `workflow` scope to the login** and the workflow is then fixed with a retry.

Cost / rules out: a helper that is not installed everywhere means switching NDI on needs a root step at the box, which the panel cannot do. A support session that can change NDI settings can name hosts for the box to connect to on the owner's network, as it can with Streams. Testing NDI on the test Pi under his own acceptance settles nothing about the venue box; the licence question for that box is still open with NDI.

## D64. Today's panel is repaired at 320, short heights and 600 to 899, and a width sweep in the browser test keeps it

2026-10-08. The owner's yes, the same day, to step 1 of the resizing study (HANDOFF, "The resizing study"): mend today's layout where it breaks, move nothing, and leave the new shell to its own branch. Pull request #100.

What was decided:

- **The faults are the ones a measurement finds.** `tests/ui/sweep.js` resizes a screen through thirteen windows: 320, 360, 390, 600, 768, 899, 900, 1200, 1366 and 1600 px wide (where each of the study's size classes begins and ends: compact under 600, medium 600 to 899, expanded 900 to 1199, large 1200 to 1599, extra large from 1600) and three phones held sideways (667x375, 844x390, and 932x430, which is short and wide enough for the laptop's columns). At each it holds: the page does not scroll sideways; no control is wider than the window or sticks out of it or of its card; a control is 44 px tall and wide where its token says 44 (in Signal every control, and 56 px for what staff press on Room and Live); no words are clipped inside a button, a chip or a pad, run out of it, or are cut in the middle of a word on a button; the tab bar is whole and inside the window; the transport and the tabs can be brought into view and pressed, and in a window under 480 px high every control can; the bars that stay put leave half of a short window to the page.
- **Every screen, in both looks.** The sweep runs on the list of `tests/ui/signal-pages.js` (D57), 45 screens and states: in Signal it rides on the first round of that look's checks, so no screen is opened twice for it; the default look, the one a box comes with, gets a round of its own after the test has put that look back. One run collects every fault before it fails.
- **A repair is the smallest rule that mends one measured fault,** by the study's means: an auto-fit grid with `clamp()` for the days of a schedule entry (seven across, or four and three where seven of 44 px do not fit), `clamp()` for a scene's name on Room in Signal, wrapping rows, and a few media rules. Control heights are the tokens, never scaled. The rules both looks share sit in one block above the Signal marker of `app.css`; `tests/test_modules_themes.py` holds everything after that marker to the Signal style.
