  | a stream, a live input, the test pattern on, a test tone | bumps |
  | Stop, the test pattern off, a sync server's stop on a client | bumps, and puts the picture's level back |
  | Next and Previous | bumps (the operator asked for another clip) |
  | a generator the player took: one chosen by hand, Vibes' start, a rotation whose epoch is still good | bumps |
  | a rotation or a queued generator change after a tap | neither: refused by its epoch |
  | the player's restart from the panel | bumps |
  | Blackout, Fade out, Fade in, Reset, the Opacity slider, a change of the mapping | neither: they end a blend; the clip loads |

- **What is not asked for by the operator and can still be the newer wish:** a schedule entry and a sync server's clip on a client. **The schedule against a tap: newest wins, both ways.** A schedule entry that comes due while a tapped clip's still is taken plays instead of it, and a tap after a schedule entry plays instead of that; the schedule is the operator's own earlier wish with a time on it, and the box cannot tell which of the two he means now. A sync client follows its server by design: the server's clip replaces one tapped on the client at the next message, as it did before. A sync server's messages do not load anything while the client already plays the server's clip, and its stop comes once.
- **A Stop during a dip does not leave the picture dark.** Stop, the test pattern off and a sync server's stop take the fader and put the level back to what the mix says (`Api._level_back`), unless Blackout is on or the operator's own Fade out is. Before, the dip's way down stayed where it was, the mix said 100, and a shader started next rotated on a dark screen.
- **A clip that is overtaken does not stop the live input that overtook it.** The live input's helper is stopped by the clip that loads, under the lock a live input is started under, and only if that clip is still the newest wish. The pairing PIN still goes from the display at the tap.
- **The dip (older than this branch, the same question).** The clip's load hung on the fader's token, so anything else that took the fader during the way down (Blackout, Fade out, Fade in, Reset, an opacity change, a MIDI fader that moves) meant the tapped clip was silently never loaded. `Fader.ramp` now also says when it was cut short, and the clip loads then as well: as a cut, at the level that action set. Only a newer wish drops it, by the ticket, as on the blend's path.
- **Lows.** `abandon` takes its own still off and is not an `end()`: a play that waits its turn keeps its blend. What a hold froze is handed to the next hold and thawed by whichever is the last (`_held`, `_paused`): no pause is left without an owner. **A play that fails is still the newest wish**: what it overtook is not brought back; the operator hears of the failure and the clip that was on the screen plays on. A play that became stale during its own still has frozen the clip for that moment and has ended the transition that ran before it; it thaws the clip and takes nothing else. **A play that was overtaken answers `{"playing": null, "superseded": "<its name>"}`**, not that it plays; a controller's play under one of these transitions answers `{"playing": "<name>", "pending": true}`, because it answers before the load.
- **How many locks and counters:** three and three, as before; the table is in `Transitions`' docstring. The player's own lock is used, not a new one.

**After the fourth review of #111, and a test of another kind** (one high, one medium, some low; and a seeded stress test, asked for because four reviews had each found an order of events the hand-written tests did not have).

- **Nothing is done under the fader's lock (the high one, a slip of the round before).** `Fader.ramp` called its `cancelled` callback inside `Fader._lock`, so a dip that was cut short loaded its clip under that lock: every Blackout, opacity change, Stop and play waited for the load, and where live inputs exist the start of one (which holds the capture lock and wants the fader) and that callback (which held the fader and wanted the capture lock) waited for each other until the panel was restarted. The token is read under the lock and the callback called after it. The rule is written at the lock: no callback, no call to the player and no other lock while `Fader._lock` is held.
- **A dip whose clip does not load puts the level back by itself** (`dipped` in `Api.play`): if the clip was overtaken or its load failed, and nobody has taken the fader since the way down, the level goes back to what the mix says. So Next and Previous, which are newer wishes and do not touch the fader, no longer leave the picture dark, and neither will a newer wish added later. A load that fails after the way down is a line in the journal (it was an unhandled error on a thread, and the old clip stayed dark).
- **One write of the level at a time** (`Fader.stepping`, the fader's second lock; the transitions still have three). A ramp's step looks at its token and writes inside it; Blackout, the Opacity slider, Reset, a Fade in, a play's own level and the level put back after a Stop take the fader and write inside it too. **Window left: none for those.** A step that was on its way can no longer land after a level set since, and of two that set a level at the same moment the later one reads the newer mix. `Fader._lock` is taken inside `stepping`, never the other way.
- **The live input's helper is stopped by what plays, not by who asks** (`Api._stop_capture`): under the lock a live input is started under, the helper goes unless the pipe is what the player plays. (First built as the review suggested, by counting starts of a live input; the stress test then showed the same fault at Stop and at every other caller, where there is no ticket to count from, and the look at what plays covers them all without a counter.)
- **Order.** The generation moves before the fader is taken (`_settle`, `shaders.show`); the screen is cleared before the level goes back (Stop, the test pattern off, a sync server's stop). **A dip answers `"pending": true`** like a controller's blend: it answers before its clip has loaded.
- **The fader is taken when a clip loads, not at the tap.** A play used to cancel a running fade at once; a play that was then overtaken before it loaded (its still, a Next) had stopped a Fade in half way with nobody to finish it.
- **What the stress test found** (`tests/test_transitions.py`, class `Stress`: 300 rounds over five fixed seeds in about seven seconds, each round three to six random actions from several threads against the fake player with the real one's lock and epoch, then what must hold whatever the order was; a failure prints its seed and its actions). Five faults, none of them in the transitions' own locks, **four of them older than this branch**:
  1. A Fade in that met a Blackout could run its ramp up under a Blackout the mix said was on (the switch was set outside the step that writes the level). The switch, the fader and the level are one step now, in both.
  2. **A Fade out under Blackout showed the picture**: its ramp began at the mix's level. Under Blackout there is nothing to fade and no ramp is started.
  3. A tap that took the fader and was then overtaken left a Fade in unfinished (above).
  4. **A Stop, a clip or a generator that met the start of a live input stopped the new input's helper**: the pipe played with nothing writing into it (above).
  5. The first version of the helper's rule, by counting, which did not cover Stop.
  After these: six runs of the five seeds and one run of forty other seeds (2,400 rounds) with nothing found.
- **What it checks after every round:** every call returned and every thread ended (no deadlock); no still on the screen and no still's file; the clip not frozen; nothing loaded after a Stop that came last; if a play, a generator or a live input came last, that is what plays; the picture's level is what the mix says, and dark under Blackout or after the operator's Fade out; a live input's helper runs exactly when the pipe plays, and never two.
- **Older oddities of the dip, looked at because the review named them.** The way down after a Fade out began again from the mix's level, a flash: fixed (after a Fade out there is no way down; the clip comes up from black). A load that fails after the way down: fixed (above). **Open:** Vibes' rotation dips by its own steps through the player and does not go through this fader, so a clip's ramp and a rotation's step that meet are not ordered by `stepping`; the rotation is refused by its epoch once a clip has been tapped, which is why nobody has seen it.
- **How many locks and counters now:** the transitions three and three, unchanged. The fader two locks (`_lock` for its token and label, `stepping` for one write of the level) and its one token. No counter was added anywhere.

**After the fifth review of #111** (nothing high, no lock inversion; two medium findings and some low). The second medium was about the stress test itself: it did not catch most of what it claimed to.

- **One rule for the picture's level, for everything that loads** (the first medium). Every load path wrote the mix's level when its load was done, whatever had been asked for meanwhile: a Fade out made while a live input's helper started (a second or more) or while a clip loaded ended with the label saying "faded out" and the picture lit, and the older tap beat the newer Fade out. Now whatever loads something (a clip by a cut, a dip or a blend, a list, a preset, a stream, a live input, the test pattern, a generator) notes the fader's mark when it is asked for and, when its load is done, takes the fader and sets the level **only if nobody has taken the fader since** (`Api._level_mark`, `_show_level(mark)`; `Fader.mark`). Every wish for a level takes the fader (Blackout, the Opacity slider, Reset, Fade in, Fade out), so **a wish for the level made after the tap wins, however long the load took, and the label and the level never disagree.** A fade that was already running at the tap is older than the tap and is ended by it, when the load is done. A dip's mark is its own way down. No counter was added: the fader's token, which was there, carries it. The blend's own rule for the level ("was a blend ended since") is gone in favour of this one.
- **A newer wish and its own change of what plays are one step under the player's lock** (`Api._as_newest`, `_stop_screen`): Stop and its clearing of the screen, a list, a stream, the test pattern, a tone, a live input and its pipe, Next and Previous and their step, the player's restart. Before, they moved the generation and then acted: a Stop could move the generation, a clip that was asked for after it could load, and the Stop's clear then landed on the newer clip. Not named by the review; seen while writing down the order the stress test now checks, and the stress test fails within seconds if it is undone (the table's row k).
- **Lows.** Blackout, the Opacity slider, Reset and Fade in take the fader once before they wait for the level's lock: with a player that does not answer, every step of a ramp holds that lock for a whole answer time (2 seconds, 10 after a start), and locks are not handed over in order on the Pi's Python. The Blackout switch, the opacity's value and the level are set inside the lock. Stop looks at the fader's label inside the lock, takes the fader between its wish and its clear, and puts the level back only if nobody has taken the fader since: a Fade out that meets a Stop stands, and a dip that a Stop overtakes no longer shows the old picture at full before the screen is cleared. **Vibes' own dip writes its level under the same lock, with its look at Blackout inside** (older than this branch: a step could land after a Blackout's dark). A generator no longer takes the fader before it is on the screen (one the GPU refused had stopped a fade for nothing, and a dip it overtook stayed where it was). The way down's token is noted before its ramp can call back.
- **The live input's helper, again.** It was decided by the player's `path`, which changes a moment after a load and whose wait gives up silently after three seconds. It is decided by what this side last loaded: `Player.pipe_playing`, set and cleared under the player's lock with each load and each clear. This is not what the review asked for (a check of `path` on the Pi); it needs nothing from mpv. Still for the Pi's list: that a helper never stays under a clip (X18).
- **The stress test made real.** As first written it had a fake that answered at once, started its last action after the others had returned, and looked only at the end; on scratch copies with one thing broken it caught three of eight. Now: the fake answers late by random amounts (the level's writes, a load, a still, the helper's start, and its `path` a moment after a load); all actions overlap; **the order in which the wishes were accepted is written down as they are made**, in the fake's own list under the player's lock, beside every change of what plays, and what must be on the screen is worked out from that order, not from the end; the Blackout switch, the opacity and "a Fade out after every tap stands" are checked against the order the level's lock and the fader's token gave; Next changes what plays in the fake; a wish made outside the player's lock is a failure in itself. **The table of what it catches is in the class's docstring** (`Stress` in `tests/test_transitions.py`): eleven ways of breaking the code, each caught by the stress test alone in three runs of three, and each also by a test written by hand (the fader taken at the tap among them, which nothing caught before), except a callback under the fader's lock, where the hand-written run hangs. Twenty runs of the unbroken code: twenty passes; 250 rounds in about nine seconds.
- **What it does not give: a replay.** A round's actions and delays come from its seed and number alone, so a round can be run again by itself, but how the threads interleave is not fixed by the seed, and neither is which call draws which lag. A scripted scheduler at the fake's entry points would fix the order of the player's calls and not the order of the panel's own locks, which is where every fault so far was. So a failure prints its seed, its round, its actions with their delays and the order of wishes and loads that was seen, and `Stress.replay(seed, round)` runs that round twenty times.
- **Open.** A level somebody sets while Vibes' own dip is on its way is ordered by the lock now, but Vibes does not take the fader and is not ended by a Fade out; it looks at Blackout only. A play from a sync server on a client still goes through `_start_list` and is "another play" like any other.

**After the sixth review of #111: the order of the locks is written once and checked by a machine.** The sixth read found a deadlock that the round before had made (one high), and two medium findings. Six reads had each found an ordering fault, so the method changed: readers no longer keep the order.

- **The deadlock.** Stop held the player's lock and waited for the level's (`Fader.stepping`); a step of Vibes' own dip held the level's and waited for the player's (`Player.source_opacity`). With a rotation dipping, Stop, the test pattern off or a sync server's stop could hang, and then every Blackout, play and fade with it, until the panel was restarted. The round before had put the two takings in, each right by itself, in two files.
- **One written order** (`pvj/locks.py`, where every one of these locks is now made, and where the table below is kept with what each guards). **A thread never waits for a lock that stands earlier than one it holds.**

  | place | lock | made in |
  | --- | --- | --- |
  | 1 | `room.order` (one Room scene at a time) | `pvj/room.py` |
  | 2 | `vibes.work` (one change of the rotation; only ever tried) | `pvj/vibes.py` |
  | 3 | `shaders.engine` (one shader shown at a time; held while the GPU looks) | `pvj/shaders.py` |
  | 4 | `effects.engine` | `pvj/effects.py` |
  | 5 | `mapper.apply` | `pvj/mapper.py` |
  | 6 | `capture` (a live input from its pipe to its helper's start) | `pvj/capture.py` |
  | 7 | `transitions.holding` (one still at a time) | `pvj/transitions.py` |
  | 8 | `player` (`Player._lock`: one change of what plays; held over a load) | `pvj/player.py` |
  | 9 | `transitions.io` (one overlay command at a time) | `pvj/transitions.py` |
  | 10 | `transitions.mark` (the transitions' counters) | `pvj/transitions.py` |
  | 11 | `fader.stepping` (one write of the picture's level) | `pvj/api.py` |
  | 12 | `fader.lock` (the fader's token and label; nothing is done under it) | `pvj/api.py` |
  | 13 | `shaderlive.cfg` | `pvj/shaderlive.py` |
  | 14 | `pinscreen` | `pvj/pinscreen.py` |
  | 15 | the leaves `vibes.state`, `shaderlive.queue`, `mapper.state` (a few fields each; never held with another) | |

  Not in the order, because their holders do not call into these paths while holding them (read, not checked by the machine): the settings' lock, the MIDI, DMX, OSC and sync managers' own, the Room's `lock`, the projectors', the health checks' and the uploads'.
- **A second rule, for the level's lock:** while `fader.stepping` is held, nothing waits for the player but the one write of the level its holder came for, and never for the player's lock. That is why the player's lock stands before it. A load holds the player's lock for seconds (mpv is asked, and the wait for its new path goes on up to three); Blackout needs the level's lock and nothing else, so **a Blackout never waits behind a load**: it waits for at most the one level write that is in the player, and then makes its own. Vibes' step, which needs both, waits for the player first, holding nothing a Blackout needs. (Before, it held the level's lock while it waited behind the load: a Blackout took 1.7 seconds behind a load of 2.) **Stop is not a Blackout:** it clears what the load puts up, so it comes after the load, and waits for nothing else.
- **The checker** (`tests/lockrank.py`, put in for every test by `tests/__init__.py`; in production `locks.make` returns a plain lock at no cost). Each lock knows its place and each thread what it holds; a taking that breaks the order raises `AssertionError` with both names and the place in the code where the held lock was taken, and is written down as well, because the code under test catches exceptions in many places; the test that was running fails. **An inversion shows as soon as each of its two paths has merely run in some test, in one thread; the interleaving that hangs is not needed.** Put in before the fix, it failed an existing single-threaded test of a Vibes tick against the real player (`tests/test_effects.py`, `LifeTest`), which had passed for days. Over the 800 tests of the modules that touch these paths it found that one inversion and one test that took two locks in its own order. Trying a lock without waiting, and taking again an RLock that is held, are not breaches.
- **No lock-free stand-ins.** The fakes of the player in the tests (`FakePlayer`, `SourcePlayer`, `Screen`, the live input's `Helper`) take a lock of the right place where the real ones do. The Vibes test that replaced `source_opacity` by a function with no lock is gone, and two tests that replaced the transitions' worker by nothing run the worker.
- **The real player under test** (`tests/fakempv.py`: a stand-in for mpv on the control socket, which answers the player's commands one at a time, keeps what mpv would keep and can answer late). The stress test and a class of tests by hand (`RealPlayer`) drive the real `pvj/player.py` over it, so its lock, its `pipe_playing` and the order of its questions are what is tested, and a fault put into `player.py` shows.
- **The player's restart.** `stop_player` sent `quit` past the player's own bookkeeping, so `pipe_playing` stayed and the live input's helper kept the device open. There is `Player.quit` now, and `pipe_playing` also says no once the player is another process (it remembers the process the pipe was loaded into), so a player that came back by itself is covered too.
- **Found by the stress test on the real player, older than this branch: a player that has restarted shows at its own full brightness whatever the mix says.** Under a Blackout the next clip could be lit. The watcher that puts the overlay and the mapping back on a new player now puts the level back too (`Api.restore_level`; dark under Blackout and after the operator's Fade out; the fader is not taken).
- **Vibes and the operator's Fade out** (open after the fifth review): Vibes' own dip leaves the screen alone under a Fade out as it does under Blackout.
- **Lows.** Fade out takes the fader before it waits for the level's lock, as the other four do. Reset sets the opacity's value inside the lock. A dip's way down is started only if nobody has taken the fader since the tap (a Fade out between the two stood for a moment and was then replaced).
- **The stress test's holes.** It gained Vibes' own dip, a list, a stream, the test pattern on and off, a tone, the player's restart and Reset; rounds that begin with a generator or a live input on the screen; a tap counts only if what was tapped loaded; the helper is held against what mpv itself has loaded, not against the player's word; the Blackout switch and the opacity are also checked inside each wish's own step; a Blackout that takes more than a second fails the round. The table of what it catches is in the class's docstring and has seventeen rows now.
- **The fallback, if the order cannot be kept or a seventh read still finds an inversion:** one owner thread, fed by a queue, for every write of the level and every change of what plays, so that the order of locks stops being a question. Looked at while ranking: it is not the better road today. With one owner a Blackout queues behind a load of seconds, which the second rule forbids; with two owners (one for the level, one for the screen) the question of their order comes back in another form. The order above was met by moving one taking (Vibes' step), with no contortion, and it is now checked at every taking in every test. If it has to be done after all, the level's owner comes first: the fader already is one thread per ramp.
- **Not run on hardware.** Whether mpv answers a level write while it loads is the player's matter (the spike saw it answer while a pipe was waited for); the bound in the tests is against the stand-in.

**After the seventh review of #111** (nothing above medium, no deadlock, no ring of locks; three medium findings and some low). The reviewer judged it reasonable to try on the device after these.

- **A generator after a live input: the player goes by what it loaded, and by mpv's word, both.** `Player._play_pipe` does not wait for mpv's `path` to follow the load, and `Player.play_source` decided "the carrier already plays" from `path` alone. A generator chosen just after a live input, itself chosen after a generator, read the old carrier, loaded nothing and noted the carrier as playing; the pipe stayed on the screen and the helper was then stopped. Of the two repairs offered, **the record was chosen, not a wait in `_play_pipe`**: a wait would be held under the player's lock for up to three seconds, and a pipe's path may not appear before its helper writes, which is exactly what the Blackout rule and Stop must not stand behind. The record is the one the player already had (`_carrier`, cleared by every other load, by the clear and by the restart, and tied to the process by `_source_pid`); the carrier is now left alone only if **the record and `path` agree**. The record alone would never reload a carrier that mpv had dropped; `path` alone was the fault. When they disagree the carrier is loaded, which costs a restart of a blank picture.
- **The player's restart stops the helper in a `finally`.** `Player.quit` puts the pipe down as not playing before it asks, so the helper is stopped also when the player does not answer or refuses. `pipe_playing` keeps its answer when the question for the pid is not answered: no answer is no news.
- **The checker never loses a breach, and is on however a module is started.** `tests/__init__.py` used to throw away, at each test's start, what had been written down since the last one. Now a breach written between two tests fails the next test and names the test that was running; one that is left when the run ends fails the run (an `atexit` that was registered first, so it runs last, and ends the process with 1). Every `tests/test_*.py` imports the `tests` package (27 did not; one line each), so `discover -s tests -p test_x.py` runs with the checker and with the temp folder of its own; a test reads every module for that import, and two start such runs.
- **The level on a restarted player is offered until it lands.** `Api.restore_level` says whether the player took the level; the watcher offers it on five rounds (ten seconds) and logs the last failure. The pid is still noted at the first sight, so the autostart does not run twice.
- **Lows.** Row n of the stress test's table was not an equivalent mutation (a Fade in between a Stop's clear and its putting-back was cancelled and snapped to full): the table says so and a hand test kills it. Row h: the spelling tried here (`self.fader.cancel()` put in before the tap's mark) is caught by the stress test none of three times, twice over. The reviewer's (`tapped = self.fader.cancel()`) fails it three of three, here too, but not because the fault is seen: the stress test learns of a tap by wrapping `Api._level_mark`, that spelling no longer calls it, and a control that behaves exactly as the right code (`tapped = self.fader.mark()`) fails the same way with the same message. The row stays "0 of 3, by hand: yes", and the docstring names the coupling. `pvj/locks.py` said the holders of the uploads' lock never call into these paths; the factory reset does, and what keeps it out of the order is that it is only ever tried. The wall-clock bounds of the tests are written as two writes of a level at the stand-in's delay plus a named margin, and the load they are held against is four bounds long.
- **Not run on hardware.** X22 and X23 in `tools/DEVICE-TESTING.md` are the two new things to look at: how late a real mpv's `path` is, and what a restart during a live input leaves.

**On the test Pi 4, 2026-10-09** (the first run on a board; nobody at the monitor). At 2560 x 1440 the still took 1.7 seconds and the box gave up on crossfades at the first play. The player's screenshot is 0.6 seconds of it and stays: it is the freeze before a blend at that size. The other 1.1 were `read_still`, mostly an unpacker called once per 4 KB piece of the file; it is one call now (about 0.13 seconds in a trial beside the panel). `SLOW` stays at 1.0 second until the fixed code has been measured on the box; with 0.6 plus 0.15 it has little room, and whether a freeze of three quarters of a second is acceptable is the owner's to judge at the screen. The figures are in the journal.

**After the ninth read of #111** (a different model; nothing high; one medium about the give-up rule, two about the tests, some low).

- **The give-up rule was one strike, measured with more than the still, and nobody was told.** `took` ran from the hold's start over five questions to the player, the freeze and the making of the file, then the screenshot and its reading, against one second; on the Pi 4 at 2560 x 1440 a whole play answered after 0.93 to 1.09 s. One slow still made every later change a dip until somebody chose the transition again, and the panel never showed `mix.fallback`. Now:
  - **the still is timed alone**, from `Player.still` to the end of `read_still` (`last.still_ms`; the whole hold is `last.hold_ms`), and every still over `SLOW` is a line in the journal with both figures, so a box can be asked what it measured. **What the Pi 4 measures for the still alone on this code is not known yet**: about 0.75 s is an estimate (the 0.6 s screenshot measured beside the panel plus about 0.15 s of reading), not a measurement of this path. `SLOW` stays 1.0;
  - **one slow still is used** and nothing else changes; the box gives up after `SLOW_IN_A_ROW` (2) slow stills one after the other, or at once when a still took longer than `HOPELESS` (3 seconds). The first form of this rule gave up at once when a still took longer than the transition was asked to last; the tenth read showed that at the box's default of one second (and at half a second) every slow still was then the last one, so the duration is no longer part of the rule;
  - **it tries again by itself** `RETRY` (300) seconds after giving up, for every reason, and at once when the transition is chosen again, as before;
  - **the panel says why** under the picker (`mix()` in `pvj/web/app.js`; `transitionCard()` on `area-desks`), in the box's own words, with **Try again**, which posts the mix as choosing the transition does.
- **The hand test of "one step" counted nothing.** It checked that the wish and the change of what plays were each under the player's lock; with a Stop's wish under one taking and its clear under a second, every test by hand passed. It now counts the lock's takings from free and holds the wish and every change to one count. **A load thaws a hold's freeze** has a test on the real player (it failed only the stress test).
- **Lows.** After the operator's Fade out a clip played with a crossfade, a wipe or a slide comes up from black over half the duration, as the dip does (it snapped to full). Vibes' own `_undip` leaves the level alone while a tapped clip takes its still or its transition runs (`Transitions.busy`): brought back up there, the frozen picture went bright before the half-dark still came over it. The docs that still said nothing had run on a board say what ran on the Pi 4, by a script, unwatched. X11 and X12 ask for the one number nobody has: Blackout and Stop DURING the screenshot.

## D72. A rail of the areas, tabs for the open area's screens, and area desks from 1200 px; the Controls screen is gone, Speed and Loop are on the strip, and an area is one build

2026-10-09. Branch `area-desks`, stacked on the Workspace shell (D65, pull request #102), with its own pull request. Not merged: the owner looks first. (D70 is master's, above; D71 belongs to the open branch `transitions`.)

The owner had looked at the shell on a wide screen. His words:

- "When the layout starts to widen and the sidemenu appears, it doesn't make sense to me to have a lot of page links in the sidebar menu that just highlights that section of the page. please look for a better navigation solution."
- "playback controls such as speed and loop don't feel right to me, being on the controls page where they are."
- "and I'm not sure I like the separation of the effect controls."
- "on the smaller window size, i notice that the transport buttons (Freeze, Stop and Blackout) are shoved down a line" (mended on the shell's branch, D65's note of 2026-10-09).
- "try to get the shaders on the same page as the library in wide views."

He was shown four options in a click-through sketch with made-up data: the menu as it was (every area and every screen in a side menu); **rail and tabs** (a rail of the four areas, the open area's screens as tabs across the top, one screen at a time); **area desks** (the rail, and where there is room an area's screens side by side on one page, with only the tools that need the whole width as tabs); and a folding menu (the side menu with the areas folded shut but for the open one). He chose: "i do like the Area Desks. The Rail and Tbs is my second favourite." The rule the coordinator read out of his words (the reading, not his words): one thing lives in one place; no screen exists only to hold shortcuts to controls that live elsewhere; a wide screen uses its width instead of offering a link per small page.

What was decided, and built:

- **Under 600 px nothing of the navigation changes**: the areas as tabs at the foot, the open area's screens in a row under the title.
- **From 600 px a rail, not a menu**: 96 px at the left, one button per area the device has (Play, Shape, Room, Setup), nothing else. It replaces the side menu of 168 to 224 px with its 28 items; the workspace has that room back.
- **From 600 to 1199 px: rail and tabs.** The open area's screens are tabs across the top of the workspace (they stay there while the screen scrolls), one screen at a time. This is his second favourite, and it is what the panel is where a desk does not fit.
- **From 1200 px: area desks.** Play is Pads, Library and Shaders side by side; Shape is Effect, Picture and Sound (Effect the widest, 1.4 to 1 to 1), with Mapping, which needs the whole width, as a tab beside the desk ("Effect · Picture · Sound" is then one tab); Room is Scenes, Walls and Guests. An area whose screens all share the page has no tabs. A desk has only the columns the role has and whose module is on, and with one column left it is just that screen. Each column is a named region with its name as a heading; none is marked as the open one, and the title bar says what the area is for instead of naming a screen. Setup keeps its index as a column of 272 px at the left of the open page, so one press leads from page to page; the page's own Back to the index is not shown there, and the rail's Setup button brings the index back alone. Under 1200 px a row opens its page and Back leads to the index, as before. The phone's back gesture and the history entries of Setup's pages work as before at every width (a page opened from the index beside another takes that one's place in the history, so Back still leads to the index).
- **Why 1200 px, measured.** Every card is held at 320 px wide by the width sweep and the narrow check, so a column must not be narrower than that. Beside the 96 px rail a panel of 1200 px has 1104 px of workspace: Play's columns are then 344, 345 and 414 px (the Shaders column 1.2 to 1), Shape's 453, 325 and 325, Room's 368 each. At 1100 px (the sketch's number) Shape's two narrow columns would be 295 px. 1200 is also where the resizing study's "large" begins, so there is one threshold for the four areas. At 1366 px the columns of Play are 396, 397 and 476 px, at 1440 px 419, 420 and 504: the library and the shaders are on one page at both, with the pads, so there was no need to let two share first and the third join later. A page of Setup beside its index has 831 px at 1200 and 997 at 1366: its own two columns (which need 900) come from about 1270 px of panel. Measured in headless Edge on the dev Mac; the narrow check passed at 1200, 1366, 1440 and 1920 px in both looks with long names (below).
- **An area is ONE build with a column per screen, at every width.** D65 warned that two builds of one card on a page would share ids and timers. So Play is built once (the pads, the library and the Shaders page, each by the function that built it), and so are Shape (Effect, Picture, the Sound page) and Room (room.js's one build, its cards put into three columns). Which columns are shown is the stylesheet's affair: one under 1200 px, all from 1200. A tab of the same area and a resize across a threshold therefore draw nothing, and what is typed, an open question and the cursor stay; a field that has the cursor makes its column the open one, so it is the column a narrower window shows. There is one message line per build, above the columns and held at the top of the window. Mapping stays a build of its own, as it was (a page has one switch and its ids once): a press on its tab draws, as every tab did before D65. Measured: on every area at 390 and 1366 px no id is on the page twice, and in 15 seconds each poll runs once (Play: 15 of the status, 3 of the shaders; Shape: 15 and 1 of the effects; Room: 15 and 8 of the room).
- **A card in a column asks its column.** Every column is the container `ws` (the one screen of a narrower panel too), so the cards' own rules for a laptop, which ask `ws`, ask the column: a card in a 380 px column has the form it has on a phone, and no rule of a card was changed. The shell's own choices ask `app`, the window never.
- **A change of one column draws that column.** A bank of pads, Edit pads, a flip or a rotation of the picture, Reset mix, the list of clips after an upload and the mute button used to draw the whole screen; that would now wipe the column beside them. They draw their own column (`redrawPart`), and choosing a pad's clip no longer draws at all until it is chosen.
- **A press on a tab of the same area still reads the box's lists**, as a tap on a tab always did, and only a column whose list or setting changed meanwhile is drawn again, and not while somebody types in it.
- **The Controls screen is gone.** What it held:
  - **Speed and Loop are on the transport strip**, on every screen, with the id and the words they had. Under 600 px they are the last row that More opens. The strip is one line from 600 px with Freeze, Stop and Blackout at its right end, as D65 has it; with eleven controls it shows everything from 1440 px (measured: 1044 px of buttons, gaps and padding, and what plays and the place in the clip beside them); from 1200 to 1439 px More holds Speed and Loop; under 1200 as before, with those two behind More as well. So at 1366 px Speed and Loop are one press away, behind More.
  - **The effect's strip is removed as a duplicate.** It carried the effect's name, Previous, On or Off, Next and Amount, and a link to the Effects card. Each of those is on the card on Shape > Effect: `#fxname`, `#fxprev`, `#fxon` and `#fxoff`, `#fxnext`, `#fx-amount`. The strip had no MIDI buttons; the card has them (previous, on and off, next, Amount). A guest saw the name and why no effect can go on, and sees both on the card.
  - **The playing shader's strip is removed as a duplicate.** It carried Speed and the shader's first four controls, for a presenter and the owner (a guest never had it), and a link to the Shaders page. All of them are in the Controls card of Play > Shaders for both roles; nothing had to be moved there.
- **Two moves in the same spirit.** The Transition picker and its duration are on Play > Pads, under the pads (it decides what happens when the next pad is tapped); its code is one function, `transitionCard()`, with the names of the transitions in one list. Test pattern is on Shape > Mapping, in a card under the mapping card, for every role that has that screen. Picture keeps Opacity, Size, Position, Mirror, Overlay, Rotate and Reset mix.
- **Old names still land.** `go()` takes `controls` and `shape/controls` and opens Shape > Effect (Picture where the box has no effects), as it takes the five tab names of before D65.
- **The inventory holds it** (`tests/ui/inventory.js`, the list of before the shell unchanged). Four keys are listed as removed on purpose, each with the control that stands for it: `#livefxprev`, `#livefxon`, `#livefxnext` (now `#fxprev`, `#fxon`, `#fxnext`) and `#livefxmore` (the link; the card is a tab, and a column from 1200 px). The check also fails if one of the three that stand for them is missing, or if a key on that list is found in the page. The shader's strip has no key in the list (nothing plays while it is taken); `panel.test.js` holds its five controls on the Shaders screen while a shader plays. Taken on the dev Mac: owner 600 before and 596 of them now, presenter 238 and 233 (the four, and the row "Room" of D65), guest 68 and 67; nothing else missing.

Why this way: his choice. The desks answer "page links that just highlight a section": where a link would only scroll to something, the thing is on the page. One build per area is the only way to show three screens at once without a second copy of a card, and it is also what makes a tab and a resize cost nothing. Mapping was left a build of its own because its page has the one switch a build can have, and its canvas measures itself when it is drawn.

Cost / rules out:

- **Not done, and why:** nothing he asked for needed a card rewritten. Three things are a judgement and his to overrule: Test pattern stays on Shape > Picture on a device that has no Mapping screen (the mapper module off, or a board that cannot map), because before the shell every role had it with no module needed; the link "Shaders ›" beside the Vibes button is still there under 1200 px, where it does what the tab above it does (it was there under 600 px, which was not to change), and is not shown in the desk; the three rows of the Setup index that open a screen of Play or Shape are kept, with their Back to the index, since they are where a module is switched on.
- **The Shaders screen has lost its own laptop form at laptop widths.** Its three columns (the library scrolling by itself beside what plays and its controls) needed 1100 px of room, which it had from about 1320 px of window. As a column of Play's desk it has 414 to 500 px and the form it has on a phone: what plays, its controls, the presets, then the list, in one column that scrolls with the page. It has its two columns again from 1700 px of panel and its three from 1900 px (there the pads and the library keep 330 and 370 px). That is the price of "the shaders on the same page as the library"; if he would rather have the workspace back, Shaders can be a tab beside a desk of Pads and Library, as Mapping is beside Shape's.
- **A long column scrolls its neighbours away.** The page scrolls, as D65 decided; in a desk the library's list or the list of effects is longer than a window, and scrolling to its end takes the pads out of view. Columns that scroll by themselves would need the fixed-height panel D65 did not follow.
- **The Shaders page asks the box while another tab of Play is shown.** It is built with Play, so it reads `/api/shaders` every 5 seconds (3 while a shader plays) also while the Pads tab is open on a phone, where the pads alone asked every 20 seconds before. In return the Vibes row no longer asks every 4 seconds while a shader is on. Its keyboard shortcuts (space, arrows, 1 to 8) do nothing while it is not shown, and nothing while the cursor is in another column of the desk.
- **On a phone the open strip is one row taller**: Loop and Speed are a fourth row under More, 50 px, so the open strip and the tabs are 334 px in Signal and 338 in the default look, measured at 390 px wide, where D65 measured 284 and 288; closed they are 184 and 188, as before. In a window under 600 px high (a small phone such as 320 by 568, a phone on its side) the open strip is held to half the window less the tabs, as the width sweep asks, and its last row, Loop and Speed, is reached by scrolling inside the strip; nothing shows that there is more below.
- **Between 1200 and about 1270 px a page of Setup is one column beside the index**, where the same window gave it two columns beside the side menu before.
- **A press on a tab of Play or Shape no longer draws the screen**: what a card shows that the box changed meanwhile comes with the refresh described above (pads, clips, the picture's sliders, volume), not with a full redraw. Where the screens share the page there is no tab to press, and those columns show what they were drawn with until something in them is used or the area is opened again.
- **The owner is moved when a module goes off under him**: on Play > Shaders with Shaders and Vibes switched off (by him or from another phone), he is on the page of Setup with its switch, as before; a presenter or a guest is on the area's first screen.
- **Pull request #111 (crossfade, wipes and slides, D71) adds its transition names to the Transition card where master has it, in `mix()`.** Whichever of the two merges second moves those names into `transitionCard()`, the one list on Play > Pads; nothing else of the two touches the same lines.
- **`go()` with an old name is not held by a test**: it is not reachable from outside the panel's script.
- **In Room's desk the column "Scenes" has a card "Scenes" under it** (the heading twice), and a column that is a page (Sound, Shaders) has the page's own, larger title where its neighbours have a small heading. Left for his eye.
- **Words that named the old places** were changed in the panel, the manual, `docs/UI.md`, `pvj/THEMES.md`, `pvj/MAPPER.md` and HANDOFF. No message of the box named Shape > Controls. The pictures in `docs/images/ui` are older than the shell; new ones come from CI.
- **Measured in headless Edge (the dev Mac) and headless Chromium (CI). Nobody has seen it on a phone, in Safari or on the box.**

Changed 2026-10-09, after the coordinator had looked at the pictures (the same pull request). What follows replaces what the entry above says of the same things:

- **The words on the strip never break.** At 1280 px in Signal the time stood as "01:", "02", "/", "03:", "05" and the name was cut to a few letters: the owner's first complaint about the shell had been text that overran, and this was the same. The clip count and the time are each one piece now (no wrap, figures of one width); where the two have no room side by side the time stands under the count, whole. The name of what plays has at least 180 px from 600 px and is cut with dots, whole in its title. `stripText()` in `tests/ui/shell.js` holds it from 320 to 1920 px in steps of 40 in every look the box has (six), with a long clip name, "Clip 11 of 12" and a time of two hours ("62:03 / 123:04": the panel's clock counts minutes and has no hours); it was broken on purpose once and failed with 140 lines.
- **Speed and Loop are on the line from 1200 px, and the fold follows one order.** His order of who has the room first: Blackout, Stop and Freeze (always); Previous and Next; Loop; Speed; Fade in and Fade out; back and forward 10 s; the place in the clip, which needs 160 px. On the line: Previous and Next from 800 px, Loop from 940, Speed from 1080, the fades from 1280, back and forward 10 s from 1440, the place in the clip from 1640; the rest is behind More. So at 1200: Speed, Loop, Previous, Next, Freeze, Stop, Blackout. At 1280 and 1366: those and the fades. At 1440: those and back and forward 10 s. The place in the clip is on the line from 1640 px only, where under D65 it was there from 1000: it is the last in his order and the widest, and a laptop reaches it with More. Each width is where the line still holds in Verdana with wider letter spacing (wider than CI's face), with the long texts on it: measured at every 20 px from 600 to 1920 in both looks, one line at each, the name never under 180 px.
- **Every column of a desk has the same small heading.** A column that is a page (Shaders, Sound) shows the page's own title and its sentence only where it stands alone (tabs, a phone); on a desk it has the column's heading like its neighbours, with its switch at the right of it (Shaders' On and Off stays where the owner switches it). Room's Scenes card is not named a second time under the column "Scenes".
- **The link "Shaders ›" beside the Vibes button is not shown at any width**: wherever the pads are, the Shaders screen is the tab above them or the column beside them. The button is still built by the card (nothing of the card was rewritten); the stylesheet leaves it out. It is the fifth key the inventory lists as gone on purpose, for each role, with the tab that does what it did. The inventory on the Mac: owner 595 of the 600, presenter 232 of 238, guest 66 of 68.

Changed 2026-10-09, a third round (the same pull request), on the coordinator's word that the fold order of the round before was his and had put the place in the clip behind More on every laptop:

The keyboard reaches it and the arrow keys move it as they did; its name is "Position in the clip" as it was. With nothing to seek in it is disabled and an empty line. ## D73. A pad can hold a generator shader: two extra keys on the pad, no new schema, and the one road a generator has

The owner asked (2026-10-09) for shaders on pads. Branch `shader-pads`, stacked on #111 (D71), because the order of the locks, the checker and the rule for the level live there. **Nothing was run on hardware.**

- **What a shader pad stores.** `{"label": ..., "file": "", "shader": "<name>.fs"}` and, if one was chosen, `"preset": "<its name>"`. `file` stays empty and no `ending` is kept (a shader has no end; an ending sent with it is ignored, not refused). **The schema is not moved** (14 on master, 15 held by #94), as D71 did with `mix.style`.
- **What an older release does with it** (tried with master's own code, a4825ae, in a scratch copy): the settings load and the pad is kept as written; a tap answers "pad is empty"; the panel shows the label on an empty pad; editing ANOTHER pad or renaming a clip leaves it alone; **editing that pad drops the shader** (master's `set_pad` writes the whole pad); **an export or import on the older release drops it** (its check keeps label, file and ending only); and its autostart refuses to be SET to such a pad (400, "choose a pad that has a clip"); **a box whose autostart was already set to a shader pad starts black on the older release**, with "autostart pad: pad is empty" in the journal and in the card's last run; and **an import on the older release of a settings file whose autostart names a shader pad is refused whole** (400, "autostart: the pad it starts has no clip"), while the same file with the autostart elsewhere imports and loses the shader. Going back to the newer release, an untouched pad works again.
- **One road.** `Api.play` looks at the pad before it claims anything and hands a shader pad to `LiveEngine.play`, the call behind the Shaders screen's own Play: it ends Vibes, starts from the preset named (else the one called "default", else the file's values), takes the screen under the player's lock as the newest wish, and sets the level by the level's rule. The branch stands BEFORE the play's own claim for two reasons: the engine's lock stands before the player's in the order (calling the engine under the player's lock is a breach, and the checker fails eighteen tests if it is tried), and a ticket taken first would make two newest wishes of one tap.
- **A controller does not wait for the GPU.** A shader the GPU has not seen is watched for up to a few seconds under the engine's lock. From MIDI, OSC, DMX and a Room scene (`api.CONTROLLERS`) the tap is therefore queued for the engine's worker with the screen's epoch of that moment, exactly as their own shader actions are (`apply_preset`): the answer is `{"playing", "shader", "pending": true}`, Vibes ends at the tap, something played after the tap wins (the player refuses the worker's stale epoch), and a refusal by the GPU is kept where the Shaders screen shows it (`error`). The panel, the autostart and anything else that waits get the answer itself, a 422 with the GPU's words if it refuses.
- **What is refused, in words:** the module off (409, "turn on the Shaders and Vibes module in System first"); the shader gone or renamed since (404, "this pad's shader, X, is not on the box any more ...: choose another for the pad"), said at the tap and before Vibes is ended; the preset gone or renamed since (404, likewise); the GPU's refusal (422, the screen keeps what it had, as for a shader chosen by hand). `set_pad` refuses a name that is no shader's (400), a shader that is not there (404) or cannot be read (422), a preset the shader does not have (404), a clip and a shader together and a preset without a shader (400).
- **Who may.** The routes decide, as for everything else, and nothing new was invented: `/api/play` asks for live access, so a guest gets 403 and a presenter may tap a shader pad as a presenter may play any shader on the Shaders screen; `/api/pads` asks for full access. No shader is closed to a role anywhere in the engine today.
- **The Mix transition around a shader pad is what it is around a shader chosen by hand**, and this pull request builds no blend into a generator: INTO a shader is a cut whatever the transition (no still, no dip; the generator ends a transition that runs); OUT of one, a clip blends from a still of the generator (crossfade, wipe, slide) or dips, as from a clip. Pinned by tests that do both ways side by side.
- **Every reader of the pads:**

  | reader | what it does with a shader pad |
  | --- | --- |
  | `Api.play` (`{"pad": [bank, index]}`: the panel, and through it every caller below) | shows the shader (above) |
  | `Api.set_pad`, `Api.get_pads` | stores it; returns the pads as stored |
  | `Api._pads_using`, `rename_media`, `delete_media` | look at `file` only: a shader pad is never "using" a clip and is left alone |
  | `Api.set_autostart`, `Autostart.run_now` (mode "pad") | allowed ("choose a pad that has a clip or a shader"); the start waits for its answer, so it is not queued |
  | `boxcare.check_pads` (export, import, factory reset) | keeps `shader` and `preset`, checked as names and not looked up (the file may come to the box after the settings); refuses a clip and a shader together; keeps no ending |
  | `boxcare` import, the autostart's pad | accepted if the pad has a clip or a shader |
  | MIDI (`pad`, `bank_pad`, the built-in map), OSC (`/pvj/pad/b/i`, `/pvj/play/pad`), DMX (the pad channel), Room scenes (`pad`) | name the pad by its place and call the same play; queued, as above |
  | MIDI lights (`MidiHub._snapshot`, `light_state`) | "on" for a shader pad, "active" while its shader is the one on the screen (`pad_shaders` and `shader_on` beside `pads` in the snapshot; `shader_on` compares the player's count of what took the screen, because the engine remembers the last shader also under a clip) |
  | the schedule | cannot name a pad (it plays a file or starts Vibes): nothing to do |
  | sync | the server sends what the player plays, and for a generator that is "stop" with its Blackout, as for the test pattern and a live input: a client goes black under a shader pad as under any shader. Unchanged, and tested |
  | the panel (`padButton`, `live`, `sheet`, the autostart card's list) | below |
- **The panel.** A shader pad carries the word Shader beside its number and the shader's name (or the label), kept to two lines with the whole name in the title; it is lit while its shader is on the screen. The pad editor has two buttons, Clip and Shader; Shader lists the box's shaders, and a shader with presets asks which to start with. All of it is in new functions (`padShader`, `padShaderName`, `padShaderNice`, `padPlaying`, `padKinds`); the functions that exist on master and on `area-desks` (#112) are touched in a few lines each: `padButton`, `live` (one line), `sheet` (wrapped in `padKinds(...)`), and in the autostart card `padName` and the list of pads that can be chosen.
- **Not done, and why:** a blend into a generator (D71: the incoming picture would need the still's road, which a generator's start does not take); a shader pad in a sync client's picture; the shader's values on the pad itself (a preset is the way).

**After the independent read of #114** (nothing high; two medium, both in the controller's queued tap; five low).

- **Of two quick controller wishes the older could win, and this was inherited.** A queued job carries the player's epoch of the moment it was asked for, and the player refuses it if the epoch has moved. A second wish that came while the worker was on its way with the first (job taken, screen not yet) carried the epoch that the first then moved itself: the newer wish was refused and the older stayed on. The pattern was in `apply_preset` (a preset of another shader) and `step` since D46 and the pad's tap copied it; **it is now closed for all three in one place**. Of the two repairs offered the first was taken: **a queued job is refused only by something that is not the queue's own job before it.** The worker remembers the epoch its last job started from and the epoch that job itself left behind (`LiveEngine._chain`; what `play_source` returned, or the count after its own stop when the GPU refused it and the screen went black), and gives a job that carries the first the second. A clip, a Stop or a shader from the panel in between moved the epoch further and refuses the job as before. Replacing older jobs before they run, the other repair, cannot reach a job the worker already holds. A step now also counts from the job the worker holds, so two quick steps go two on.
- **The level's mark is taken at the wish, not by the worker.** `LiveEngine.queue_show` takes the epoch and `Api._level_mark` together and the job carries both to `Engine.show(mark=...)`: a Fade out pressed after a controller's tap, preset or step stands when the shader comes. Blackout and Stop after the wish already held and have their tests.
- **Lows.** A pad whose shader no longer reads says so at the tap (422) before Vibes is ended, from a controller as from the panel. The import's check of a shader's name is tested with what it refuses (NUL, newline, backslash, 300 characters, a direction mark, a list, an object, a number, a switch). A renamed preset carries the pads that start with it. The library says how many pads hold a shader (`pads` in each row) and the panel's Remove says it before it is done; the autostart's pad is counted as a pad and not named apart.
- **Which pad is "the one playing".** Its shader is on the screen, with the preset the pad starts it with (a pad without one: the preset called default, or none), and Vibes is not running. Before, every pad holding the shader was lit, whatever its preset, also while Vibes showed it. "The pad that was tapped" was the other choice and was not taken: the same picture chosen on the Shaders screen should light its pad, and nothing has to be remembered. The panel reads `player.shader_preset` in the status; the controllers' lights read the engine's own record. The preset lights and the shader pads are dark under a clip played after the shader (the engine remembers its last shader; the player's count of what took the screen is compared).
- **The pairing PIN on the screen.** A shader, from a pad as from the Shaders screen, takes it off when the shader is on the screen (`Engine.show` ends in `Api._started_playing`), not at the tap as a clip does. The difference is deliberate: a shader the GPU refuses leaves the screen as it was, PIN included. Tested for the panel's tap, a controller's and a refusal.
- **Not looked at:** the effects' own queue (`pvj/effects.py`), which has a job shape and a counter of its own.

**After the second read of #114** (nothing high; two medium with one cause, both in what the first repair left).

- **The first repair knew two of a job's epochs and a job makes up to three.** It kept (the epoch the job started from, the one it left). A wish that came while the GPU was looking at the job's shader (after it took the screen, before the engine says so: up to four seconds at a shader's first showing) carries the epoch the job's own showing made. Two steps then went to the first shader of the set instead of two on, and a second tap behind a first that the GPU refused into black was dropped without a word. **Now every epoch a job itself makes is written into the job the moment it is made** (`job["made"]`: what it was given, what `play_source` returned, what its own stop left; `Engine._made`, called under the player's lock where the epoch moves, so an exception afterwards cannot lose it), the worker keeps the set with the last of them, and a job that carries any of them is given the last. `step` counts from the job in the worker's hands while the player's epoch is one that job made. This is the reviewer's suggestion; nothing better was found.
- **What the worker keeps is the epoch a job was GIVEN, not the one it carried**, or the third wish of three in a row is refused; a test of three holds it.
- **A renamed preset and the pads that name it are one write.** They are in the one settings file: `preset_rename` changes both under the settings' lock and saves once, so no tap finds a pad naming a preset that is gone and a failed write leaves the file as it was.
- **The epoch and the level's mark of a wish are read one after the other with no common lock, on purpose** (the player's lock is held over a load and a controller's tap must not wait for it); `LiveEngine.moment` says why that is safe, and `step` hands on the one reading it made. `_chain` has no lock because only the worker calls `play_job`; its definition says so.

**After the third read of #114** (nothing high; the alias set held; two medium).

- **A controller's step is queued as what it is, a move, and the worker turns it into a shader.** Three quick steps (the second before the first took the screen, the third while the GPU looked at it) landed on the first shader of the set: the third step worked out its neighbour at the press, from a waiting job that it did not take for its own. That was the third fault in the same lookup, so the lookup is gone and not patched. `Changer.step` keeps each press as (direction, the player's epoch, the level's mark) of its own moment, and presses that wait are kept together. `LiveEngine._resolve_steps`, in the worker, keeps the presses that are still good (nothing but the queue's own jobs took the screen since: the epoch read through the alias is the player's now), adds them up (two Next make two, a Next and a Previous make none), and counts from the shader on the screen, which by then is what the step before it showed, else from the set's first (Next) or last (Previous). The set is read there too. Nothing is computed from a reading at the press, and no step is replaced or lost. The rules for refusal by a clip, a Stop, a panel shader or Vibes are the ones before: a press made before any of them is dropped.
- **Why this shape and not the narrower fix:** the worker is the only place that knows what the job before did, and it runs one job at a time, so "where am I stepping from" has one answer there. No reason was found against it. Two things follow that are changes: `POST /api/shaders/step` answers `{"ok": true, "steps": n}` (how many wait) and no longer names a shader, which it could only guess; and a Next after a shader the GPU refused goes past it, where it used to try the refused one again (a refused shader leaves the set and the one before it is back on the screen, which is where the next step counts from).
- **`preset_rename` writes through the save's own rules again.** The one write of the round before set the section itself and lost `LiveEngine._save`'s rule that a part which cannot be read is left in the file, and its step to version 2. The preparation is now `_prepared(cfg)`, used by `_save` and by the rename, which stays one write.

**After the fourth read of #114** (nothing above low; the step's new shape held).

- **The queue keeps the order of the wishes.** A pad's tap or a preset of another shader that waited between two steps was dropped without a word: the step after it replaced it, though the tap had already ended Vibes. What waits is now at most one whole shader and one batch of steps BEHIND it. A whole shader asked for supersedes everything that waited before it, steps too (a shader that is named does not depend on where the steps before it would have ended); steps pressed after it wait behind it, are shown after it, and count from it. For a step to be still good behind two of the queue's own jobs, the epochs a job made stand for the next job's too when that one started where it left.
- **The batch of steps does not grow.** Presses made under the same epoch of the player are one entry (their sum, the epoch, the level's mark of the newest), and at most `STEP_ENTRIES` (8) entries are kept, the newest: a new entry begins only when something else took the screen. A knob that sends hundreds of messages makes one entry.
- **The place after a refusal into black.** A step whose shader the GPU refused with nothing to go back to leaves no shader on; its place in the set is kept while nothing else takes the screen, and the next Next goes on from there (the shader that stood after the refused one stands in its place now), a Previous back from there. A set that was emptied between the press and the worker is said in the log; it is nobody's shader that failed, so `error` is not written with no name in it.
- **A test for the mark of the summed steps** (the newest press's, so a step pressed after a Fade out brings the picture back as any play after it, and steps that add up to none leave it dark): the code was right and nothing held it.

**After the fifth read of #114** (the whole branch, by another model: one high that four reads had missed, two medium, four low).

- **High: a controller's pad tapped while Vibes changed shaders left the screen black and said nothing.** The tap ends Vibes (`yield_screen`) and queues its shader with the player's epoch of that moment. Vibes' own change, which was in progress, went on: its shader took the screen (the epoch moved), it then saw that it had been ended and CLEARED the screen (the epoch moved again), and the worker's job, carrying the epoch from before, was refused without a word. The pad's own case, a Launchpad during a set with Vibes running. A controller's preset of another shader had the same hole, and a Room scene's Stop of Vibes followed at once by a pad. Every earlier test of Vibes tapped before its first tick or after it had settled.
- **The shape taken, both parts asked for and one more:**
  - **(b) A rotation that is ended for a chosen shader does not clear the screen.** `yield_screen` marks it (`_yielded`); a shader of its own that had already taken the screen stays until the chosen one shows. A Stop still clears.
  - **A rotation that was ended puts nothing more on.** `Engine.show(alive=...)` asks, under the player's lock just before the screen is taken, whether the rotation still runs. So the tap is not followed by a flash of the rotation's next shader.
  - **(a) What the rotation still moves on its way out is the queue's own.** The window between the look at `alive` and the taking of the screen, and a Stop's clearing (`engine.off(epoch, adopt=True)`), are noted for the queue IN THE SAME STEP as the move, under the player's lock (`LiveEngine.adopt`): the epoch from before stands for the one after. The worker, when the player refuses a job, looks again under that lock and tries with the new epoch (the move may have come after it read).
  - **Never silent:** a queued wish that is dropped after all (a clip or a Stop came after it) is a line in the journal.
- **The windows**, each with Vibes' own thread running and held there while a controller's pad and a controller's preset of another shader come, and the worker made to wait until the rotation has finished its way out (the worst order):

  | where the rotation is when the wish comes | before | now |
  | --- | --- | --- |
  | before it composes its next shader | that shader flashed on, then black, the wish refused | nothing more is put on; the wish shows |
  | while the GPU looks at its shader (up to 4 s the first time) | black, the wish refused | its shader stays until the wish shows |
  | after its shader took the screen, before it noticed | black, the wish refused | the same |
  | during its own dip to black | the wish showed (the level came back) | the same |
  | a Stop, while its thread clears the screen | black, the wish refused | cleared, then the wish shows |
  | a Stop while the GPU looks, then the wish | black, the wish refused | cleared, then the wish shows |
- **Not in the stress test:** its shape does not allow it. It has no rotation thread (it sets Vibes' running flag by hand for the dip) and the queue's worker outlives a round, which is why no queued wish is in it. The invariant asked for, "a queued wish that ended Vibes is on the screen or has said why", is held by the tests above and by the journal line.
- **Medium.** The light's rule "not while Vibes shows it" was held by no test (Vibes showed a shader that was on no pad): the test gives a pad that very shader. **A refusal by the GPU is said on the Live page**: the status carries the engine's last refusal (`player.shader_refused`), and `patchLive` calls the new `padRefusal`, which puts one line under the pads and marks the pad. The controllers' lights have four states (off, on, active, busy) in every profile and none for an error, so the pad's light does not change: recorded, not built. And a correction: after a refusal "the screen keeps what it had" only if a shader was on before; with a clip or nothing on, the refused shader leaves the screen BLACK (the engine has no clip to go back to), and the message says so.
- **Low.** The queue's record of its own epochs keeps the job before only and does not grow. After the operator's Fade out a shader chosen by hand or by a pad comes up from black over half the Mix duration (`_show_level(rise=True)`), as a clip does. A pad whose preset was deleted shows its shader with the shader's own start and says so in the answer (`note`) and in the journal, as Vibes does for a set; the preset's Delete says how many pads start with it (`preset_pads`). A second tap of the pad whose shader is on goes on from the time it has reached, as Play on the Shaders page does: left, and said in the manual.

**After the second model's check of d3dc27c** (nothing above low; it judges the branch ready to merge after `transitions`).

- **The refusal under the pads is news only while the screen is as the refusal left it.** It stayed until that same shader showed or the service restarted. The engine keeps the player's epoch with a refusal of a whole shader, and the status gives `shader_refused` only while the epoch is still that one: a clip, another shader, a Stop or Vibes takes it away. The Shaders page keeps its `error` as before. A refusal of a live value (the shader stays on) was never a pad's refusal and is not reported there.
- **A tap that Vibes overtakes is said in the journal.** Vibes started by the schedule or a Room scene between a controller's tap and the worker forgot what waited (`changer.clear()`), which is right, and said nothing, which was not: `LiveEngine.drop_waiting` forgets it and writes what it was.
- **`rise` is passed plainly** to `_show_level`; the `except TypeError` around it would have hidden a real fault and written the level twice. No stand-in in the tests defines `_show_level`.
- **`_adopted` is capped and never cleared**, and a comment says why that is enough.

## D74. An effect goes on over a generator shader: the generator first in the player's list, the effect filtering what it drew

2026-10-09. Pull request #113, branch `effect-over-shader`, stacked on #111 (`transitions`). The number: D72 is the area desks (#112) and D73 the shaders on pads (the branch `shader-pads`), both unmerged when this was written; D74 was free on `origin/master`, `transitions`, `area-desks` and `shader-pads`.
Why: the owner asked on 2026-10-09: "please build both, shaders on pads and effects over shaders". Until now an effect came off when a generator or Vibes took the screen, and the panel refused one over a generator with the sentence "A generator shader has the screen. An effect changes a picture that is playing, and a generator is drawn from nothing, so there is no picture to change." (D55). That sentence was a choice, not a fact about the player: a generator's drawing is a picture like any other once it is drawn.

**The spike, before any design** (run 37943294827, the three `effects-gpu` jobs, mpv 0.37, Mesa's software GPU, a 320 x 180 window; `tests/test_pair_gpu.py` at the branch's first commit handed both texts to the player through its socket with nothing in `pvj/` changed). All three ways of drawing agreed on every answer:

1. *Is it the effect of the generator's picture?* Yes. An invert over a gradient generator is the exact negative of the generator alone (largest difference 0 of 255), the right way up, half way at Amount 0.5, and the generator itself at Amount 0, where the effect's pass is not drawn.
2. *Which hook, and what decides the order?* Both hook NATIVE. The order of `glsl-shaders` decides: generator then effect is the negative; effect then generator is the generator alone, since it draws over what the effect made. Of the effect's two hooks the one for RGB runs (the carrier is `rgb0`), the one for LUMA is left out.
3. *Do the carrier and the effect's sampling meet?* The effect meets the generator's drawing: `HOOKED_size` read 321 x 181 for a 320 x 180 generator and 638 x 361 for 640 x 360 (an 8-bit reading, good to 4 pixels). The player's own word about the picture stays the carrier's: 64 x 36, RGB, full range, 30 a second.
4. *Effect detail against Picture detail?* A cap at or above the generator's lines changes nothing: a checkerboard of single pixels came through an effect that changes nothing with a difference of 0, so it is neither scaled twice nor blurred. A cap below them makes the effect draw at the cap and read the generator smoothed (the checkerboard became flat grey; 640 x 360 under 240 lines read about 426 x 241).
5. *GLSL 1.40 and OpenGL ES?* The pair compiles and draws on both and on desktop OpenGL.
6. *Two passes?* Two hand-written hooks, the first saved into its own texture, also at half the size, drew the right pixels. Nothing is built on it: the translator makes no such text.
7. *TIME?* Both run at the clock's pace together (0.99 and 1.00 to 1.01 times the clock). The effect's goes on across a change of generator and stands still while the player is frozen.
8. *Blackout, opacity, the still?* Blackout under the pair: brightest point 0. Opacity 50: the pair less a half. `Player.still()` holds the pair (difference 0).

**What was built on it.**

- **The player** no longer drops the effect when a source comes (`play_source`) and no longer refuses an effect while a source has the screen (`put_effect`). The list was already source, effect, mapping. The buffers' format is not set again when an effect comes or goes under a source or a mapping (it is 8-bit there already, and setting it makes the player set its renderer up anew).
- **Stop takes the pair off**, as it takes an effect off a clip. Found no reason otherwise: Stop means a clean screen, and the next thing played starts clean. **Vibes stopped** (and a rotation that ends by taking its shader off) clears the screen through the same call and so takes the effect too; `last` then says "the shader under it was taken off the screen", not "Stop was pressed". A restart of the player loses both; the module switched off takes both off. None of this is new behaviour of an effect, only its meeting with a generator.
- **A generator chosen while an effect is on, Vibes started, a Vibes step**: the effect stays, as it stays when a clip changes, in the list from the generator's first command on. During Vibes' own dip the effect stays on and dips with the picture: the dip is the brightness, which comes after both.
- **The size the panel shows** is the generator's drawing, from the generators' record (`Effects.picture` asks the player for `path`, and over a carrier reads `shaders.playing["size"]` without the generators' lock, as `Engine.on_screen` reads it). `working.under` says `"shader"` or `"clip"`.
- **A refused pair.** The generator stays on the screen, the effect is off (or the effect before it is back), and the message says both. The pair is remembered in the memory refused values already had (`_bad`), under a key that now says whether a generator is under the effect, and is not sent to the GPU again: 422 "the GPU refused X over a shader before (...); it was not sent again". Over a clip the same file is still tried (another hook runs there), and `_checked` learned the same distinction, so the first meeting of an effect with a generator is listened to. An effect that was on over a clip when a generator came and is then refused comes off, since no text is known to work over that picture.
- **Whose refusal.** The two engines hold different locks and listen to one log. `shaders.about` leaves out what the log says of another shader, told by the name every text carries in a comment and the player prints with a refused text. Without it a refused generator of a Vibes step could be remembered as a refusal of the effect that was being changed in the same moment.
- **The guard.** With an effect on, Vibes marks no shader heavy, notes none as tight and does not move on for dropped frames; the effect's own guard speaks in its card and starts its count over when another shader comes under it. A heavy mark is kept in the settings with the drawing height and decides what rotations show from then on, so a mark made under an effect would blame the shader for the pair for good; and moving on would end the rotation after three shaders ("the box is dropping frames whatever plays") in the middle of a performance.
- **The panel**: the sentence that refused an effect under a generator is gone and nothing takes its place, since nothing is refused there. The card's rule reads "One effect at a time. It stays on when the clip changes, and over a shader and Vibes; Stop takes it off."; the heavy and the working-size lines say "shader" over one; "Now playing" appends the effect to a shader's name; the Shaders page's load line says Vibes is not judging.
- **No lock was added** and no taking is new: the effects' engine reads the generators' record without a lock, and the generators' engine tries, never waits for, the effects' lock when it clears the screen (shaders.engine before effects.engine, as the order has it). The checker ran in every test.
- **Nothing is stored**: no settings key, no schema change.

Cost / rules out: **nothing here ran on a board, and the cost of two shader stages on a Pi 4 is unknown.** Automatic is a table made from effects over clips; it does not know about generators, and nothing takes a heavy pair off by itself. While an effect is on, the guard that protects a rotation from a heavy shader is silent, by choice. That is a suspension, not a guard: what still tells the two apart is the pass time of each, which the player reports per shader (`pass_ms` in both requests), not the dropped frames. **A question left for the owner:** ambience started by staff from the Room screen, by the schedule or by autostart is the same Vibes, so it runs under whatever effect was left on, unattended and with that guard silent; nothing takes the effect off for it today. A wish from a controller (Next, On, a preset) that waits for the worker in the moment a Vibes step lands is dropped by the epoch rule, as it is when a clip is played in that moment. Between a generator's first frame and the worker's look (up to a second) an effect that was on over a video runs with a text made for that video and unheard by anyone; if the GPU refused that text's other hook the screen would show whatever the player shows for a refused hook until the look. The memory of a refused pair lasts until the panel restarts, also for a refusal that had a passing cause. `shaders.about` trusts a comment in the printed text; a player that printed a refused shader without its text would be heard as before, by both engines. A chain of effects, passes and an effect that survives a restart stay unbuilt.

After the independent review (2026-10-09, the same day; nothing above medium, the order of the list, Vibes' ends, the locks, escaping and the notes came back clean):

- **A wish that waits is no longer dropped by a Vibes step, and quick wishes add up.** A controller's Next, On or preset was stamped with the player's epoch, which every step of a rotation moves, so in that moment it came to nothing and the performer heard "ok".
