Since this decision a step does not touch the effect, so dropping was wrong. The player has a count that only a clearing of the screen and the panel's ending of the player move (`Player.clears`), and a wish carries that: a Stop in between still keeps the screen clear, and the wish then says so in `error` ("... was not put on. Ask again.", `kind: "wish"`). A clip, a generator and a Vibes step do not stop a wish: that changes D55's "only if nothing was played in between" for clips too, on purpose, since an effect stays through all three. **A step is kept as a move and resolved by the worker when the job runs**, waiting steps adding up, never computed from a reading taken at the wish: the pattern that lost steps in the shaders' queue on another branch was here too (a second Next made while the worker held the first named the same effect). A wish no longer moves the engine's generation; only Off and an effect put on by hand do, so a job in flight is not taken off again by the wish behind it. Tested for 2, 3 and 5 wishes in five windows (before the worker takes any, while it holds the one before, at its call to the player, during the GPU's look, after it) and against the real worker thread.
- **The suspended guard has a floor.** With Vibes judging nothing while an effect is on, a heavy pair that nobody watches would stutter for ever. The pair is counted by itself across the shaders that come and go under the effect, and after 2 dropped frames a second on average over 20 seconds the box takes the **effect** off, never the shader, with no mark on any shader, says why in `last`, and puts nothing back. The suspension of the mark stays: a mark persists and would blame the shader. **The two numbers are guesses** (`PAIR_LIMIT`, `PAIR_SECONDS` in `pvj/effects.py`), the generators' limit and a little over three of the guard's windows, to be set from rows E1 to E8. It holds for ambience started by the schedule, autostart or the Room screen, which was the case it was asked for. The owner's question above stands in a smaller form: should a start of ambience take an effect off at once, and not after twenty seconds of a stutter.
- **The memory of a refused pair is of the pair**: the effect and its shape, the generator and its shape. Keyed by "a shader is under it" alone, one refusal refused that effect over every generator until the panel restarted.
- **A complaint that names no shader is nobody's while both are in the player.** `about()` kept such a stretch, so a listener that began in the middle of the other shader's printed text took its "line 3: syntax error" for its own. Alone in the player an unnamed complaint is still one's own (a player that printed no text would be heard as before).
- **A generator that comes asks the effect's worker to look at once**, so the GPU's word about the effect over the new picture is listened for from the arrival and not up to a second later. What the screen shows if the old text were refused in that moment is unverified (row E12).
- **The size the panel shows is said also while the GPU still looks at a generator that has just come** (the generators' record is then the one before it).
- **Not done:** a browser test of the panel's words for a refused pair. The browser test's player draws nothing (`--vo=null`), so no GPU refuses anything there; the words are held by the unit tests and by the GPU test.

Cost of this round: a wish for an effect now lands over a clip that was played after it (it did not before). The floor acts on dropped frames, which a hitch of the machine also makes: twenty seconds of two a second is a long hitch, and still a guess. `error` carries a `kind` the panel must read to choose its words; an older panel reads "The GPU refused" for a wish, as it did before for "nothing is playing".

After a second independent review (2026-10-09, another model; nothing high, one medium; the queue, the floor's counting, the order of the list, `about()`, the locks and the notes came back clean, and the change to D55 was judged right):

- **A pair's refusal no longer leaks into the library.** `put` wrote the file's refusal (`_refusals`) for every refusal, also over a generator, so one refused pair made the library's row say "refused" over a clip and made Next pass the effect by everywhere. The file's refusal is now written only when no generator was under it; a pair's refusal lives in `_bad` alone, the row says it of the pair (`refused_pair`) only while that generator is under the effects, and the worker's Next leaves the effect out only then (`order(pair)`).
- **A player that came back by itself is a clearing.** `Player.clears` moved only on `clear` and `quit`; an mpv that crashed and was started again by its service left a wish to land over whatever played next. The player now notes the process it last heard from, and another one moves the count (every play, every status and every wish asks for it).
- **The wish's word says what cleared the screen**: Stop, a shader taken off (Vibes stopped or ended), or a restart.
- **A controller's Off that still waits is carried out before the Next that took its place in the queue**, so it holds when that Next is refused or finds no picture.
- **The floor's words claim no more than is known**: frames dropped with the effect on over the shader, and the effect was taken off to lighten the load. If the shader alone then goes on dropping frames (the generators' own guard), the card says so, so that nobody concludes the effect was the cause.
- **Known, kept:** while the generators' record is stale (the GPU still looks at a generator that has just come), a text checked or refused then is remembered under "some generator" and not under the pair. It is a short moment and errs on the side of asking the GPU again.
- **Ambience: nothing was built to clear an effect at a start.** The floor covers every start. **The open question for the owner**: whether a start by the SCHEDULE alone should take an effect off first, since that is the one unattended start that can inherit a performer's look (a person starts ambience from the Room screen, and autostart follows a restart, which has no effect on).

After the re-check of that round (2026-10-09, the second model, by probes; one high, two medium):

- **High: the floor handed the pair's dropped frames to Vibes' guard.** The moment the floor took the effect off, Vibes' guard was no longer suspended, and the generators' guard still held a window of six seconds full of the pair's dropped frames: in that same second the shader was marked heavy, for good, and skipped. That is the mark the suspension exists to prevent, and it was certain in the unattended case the floor was made for; a plain Off during a heavy pair did the same. **Cured at the root: the generators' guard keeps its window by the shader's name together with the effect over it** (`LiveEngine.watch`), so it starts over whenever an effect comes over a shader, leaves it (Off, the floor, a refusal, a controller's Off) or is exchanged. No word about a shader alone can be made from frames dropped under a pair, and a shader that really is heavy by itself is marked after a whole window of its own (three seconds to settle, six to judge).
- **"Frames are still dropping without it"** is said only on a word of that guard whose window began after the effect went. It was said in the second of the shed, from the pair's own window.
- **An Off that a Next carries does not take off an effect put on by hand since.** The worker looks at the engine's generation before the carried Off, not after.
- **A restart is counted once**: the look at the process number and the count are one step under a small lock of their own (`player.pid`, a leaf in `pvj/locks.py`), since status polls come from several threads. **A wish made after the panel's own ending of the player is for the new player**: that restart was counted at the quit, and the first new process heard of is not counted again.
- **The rows of the library say a pair's refusal by the picture of the same request** (the look at what plays comes before the rows).
- **The worker's look at the picture is made under the engine's lock**, which a by-hand put reads the same fields under.
- **The price of the keyed guard, in plain words** (after a third check, which found nothing above low and judged the branch ready to merge after `transitions`, pending the board's rows): a word about load is made only from frames dropped while one thing was on the screen (a shader alone: Vibes' mark; a pair: the floor), and both counts start again at any change of the pair. So effects exchanged more often than every 20 seconds over a heavy pair are never taken off by the floor, and a heavy shader that has effects put on and off more often than every 9 seconds is never marked. That is what not blaming the wrong one costs. Also from that check: a quit the player did not take no longer leaves the next restart to pass as the panel's own.

**With the shaders on pads (D73) merged in** (2026-10-09, `shader-pads` at 8f54af2 merged into this branch; the order of merging is #111, then #114, then this one). The two branches conflicted in `pvj/api.py`, `pvj/shaderlive.py`, `pvj/shaders.py` and `tests/test_transitions.py`; every conflict was a place both had added to, and both additions stand:

- the status for a shader carries `shader_preset` (D73) and `effect` (D74);
- the worker (`Changer`) has D73's `step` and its `_doing` beside this decision's `merge` and `working`, and marks both while a whole shader is being put on;
- `Engine.off` clears under the player's lock and notes the move for the generators' queue (D73), and then sweeps the text of an effect that went with the picture (D74);
- the stress test has D73's rows r, s and t and this decision's row, now u, and both sets of actions.

**The two were then used together for the first time** (`tests/test_pads_and_effects.py`, nine tests on the real player over the stand-in for mpv, with both engines; a tenth on the real mpv in `tests/test_pair_gpu.py`; four lines in the browser test). What they found:

- **Nothing in either feature had to be changed.** Each of the nine was then made to fail by breaking, one at a time, the piece of D73 or D74 it stands on (eight breakages, each caught).
- **A pad tapped with an effect on** (from the panel and from a controller) keeps the effect, in the list from the pad's shader's first command; the worker's look hands the GPU a text for the shader's picture; the status says the shader, its preset and the effect.
- **A pair refused at a pad's tap** leaves the pad's shader on and playing for the pads; the refusal is the effect's and is said on its card and in its row; nothing is said under the pads, where a refused SHADER is said. The other pad's shader is another pair and the same effect is tried over it.
- **A pad's SHADER refused with an effect on.** With a shader on before, that shader is back under the effect and the line under the pads says the refusal. With a clip on before, the engine stops the screen (it has no clip to go back to: D46 and D73), and the effect comes off with the picture it was over; its card says "the shader under it was taken off the screen" and no refusal of its own. The two places say different things about one event and do not contradict each other. Worth knowing: a refused pad over a clip costs the effect too.
- **The two queues side by side.** A controller's pad and a controller's effect step made together both land, in either order of the wishes and of the two workers. Their rules differ on purpose and both hold: a shader wish carries the player's epoch and is dropped by a clip played after it (D73); an effect wish carries the count of clearings and is not (this decision). A Stop after both drops both, and each says so in its own place (the effect's `error`, the journal's line for the shader).
- **A controller's pad while Vibes changes, with an effect on** (D73's black-screen case, the rotation's own thread held in the GPU's look): the screen is never cleared, so the effect is never taken off; the rotation's shader stays under the effect until the pad's shows, and the effect is on over the pad's shader afterwards, the same effect, never off and on.
- **The floor over a pad's shader** takes the effect off and leaves the pad the one playing (the status and the controller's light), with nothing marked.
- **A pad that changes the shader under an effect** starts the generators' guard and the effect's own guard over for the new shader; the floor's count goes on across it, as across a rotation's step.
- **After a Fade out, a pad's shader comes up from black over half the Mix duration with the effect still on**, from the panel and from a controller.
- **Both workers on their own threads**, pads and effect steps coming together: nothing hangs, the generator is always first in the list, the last wish of each kind is on; the lock checker saw nothing.

After the second model's read of the combination (nothing high; the merge judged right; one medium, four low):

- **A blend that gives up for dropped frames names the effect when one is on.** A crossfade out of a pad's shader keeps the effect on over the incoming clip, and a heavy effect made the first blend give up with "the clip dropped N frames", which blames the clip and names nothing to do; Try again failed again. The frames are still counted with an effect on (a blend that stutters falls back); only the reason changed: "frames were dropping during the change with the effect X on (N in S seconds); taking the effect off may help". It is in `pvj/transitions.py`, which is #111's file: three lines and a small reader of the effects' memory, written on this branch because only here can an effect be on out of a shader.
- **A pad's shader refused while a clip took the screen** answers "What was played meanwhile stays on the screen.", not "The screen is black.".
- **The floor judges by what is under the effect at that look.** With no picture to be seen the pair is not judged; a clip under it is no pair.

## D75. Controller layouts that can be edited: overrides on a built-in profile, never a copy; levels with a centre that sits; one fade button that flashes

2026-10-10. Branch `midi-layouts`, a draft pull request stacked on #113 (`effect-over-shader`), to merge after #111, #114 and #113. The number: D75 was free on `origin/master` (D70 is its last), `transitions` (D71), `area-desks` (D72), `shader-pads` (D73) and `effect-over-shader` (D74).

Why: the owner, 2026-10-10, three messages. (1) "please push for editable midi controller layouts, incorporating the mapping section properly, like in the original live package." (2) "I've tried using all three controllers and found the controlls that were mapped to be sort of, odd, or not helpful, or incomplete. especially, the zoom and x/y position. ps, the fade out and in buttons should be one, that triggers it to fade out, then next press is fade in. when fading out and during black, the button should flash on the control panel and controller." (3) "ps, the launchpad button layouts dont make sense to me." Message 2 is the first report from the real controllers: the three standard layouts were written from documents.

**An assumption about his words, not a fact.** Nothing in his earlier messages, in this repository or in the legacy panel names "the mapping section" or "the original live package". What was looked at (about twenty minutes): the legacy manual (`docs/html/_sources/08_mapping.rst.txt` is ofxPiMapper steered by a USB mouse and keyboard keys; `11_dmxoscmidi.rst.txt` has DMX, OSC and QLC+ and no MIDI at all), the legacy OSC commands (`sync/osc_control.js`: flips, rotate, overlay, soft edge, start and stop; no mapping of controls), the panel's first versions (`git log --follow -- pvj/web/app.js`), `origin/ux-proposal`, `pvj/MIDI.md`, `pvj/MAPPER.md`, `docs/MANUAL.md` and this file. The words "live package" appear nowhere. Three readings stay possible: (a) the panel's Mapping screen and the picture geometry beside it (size, which he calls zoom, position X and Y, rotate, flips); (b) the MIDI page's own "Mappings" section (Learn and the list), which sat apart from the controller cards; (c) Ableton Live's MIDI map mode (tap a control on the screen, move the hardware). **The reading taken: (a) and (b) together.** A controller now reaches every piece of the picture's geometry, and the MIDI page's Learn and list are part of each controller's card. (c) is not built as such, though "tap the control on the card, then press the real one" is its gesture. The question is in the report for him.

**His answer the same night** settled it: he chose the picture controls and the Mapping screen on the controllers, and "the original live package" is the panel as it is on the box (master's), not a legacy thing: "The projection mapping section in the Area Desks layout examples don't look like the section that is in the current live version." He also wrote: "It would also be cool to have a card per controller that could be edited directly, as well as a switch to turn on MIDI/OSC Map Mode to pick any controller, turn a knob or something and it gets mapped. there can be an option for only allowing one controller to map to a control on screen, or allow for multiple physical controllers using a soft pickup version of sharing the digital control." And on what felt odd: "Things were there but in places that make no sense." So: the picture's geometry on the controllers and layouts that explain themselves are Part 1; the card as an editor is Parts 2 and 3; Map Mode is a later phase; the Mapping screen's redesign is another branch's work.

**Part 1, built (2026-10-10). Parts 2 and 3 are not started.**

1. **A level that has a normal value has it at the middle of its control, exactly.** `SHAPES` in `pvj/midi.py` gives each level a range, a centre and a curve; `level_value` is the one transfer function, and nine steps around 64 give the centre (a knob has no notch and 0 to 127 has no middle step). Zoom is 25 to 200 with 100 at the middle and a curve of 1.6, the positions -100 to 100 with the same curve, clip speed and shader speed two straight halves around 1. The table and the reasons are in `pvj/MIDI.md` ("How a level follows a knob"). Chosen by reasoning, not by feel: nobody has turned a knob against it.
2. **Zoom and both positions wait for pickup** on a controller with a layout, and pickup looks for the box's value on the level's own curve backwards (`level_position`), not on a straight line. Two controllers on one level share it this way; that is tested, because it is the model the owner described for Map Mode.
3. **A mapping may carry `min`, `max`, `invert` and `takeover`.** Stored in the mapping, validated in `validate_entry`, ignored by an older version.
4. **New actions**: `position_y`, `fade`, `rotate`, `flip_h`, `flip_v`, `overlay`, `seek_back`, `seek_forward`, `loop`, `mute`, `test_pattern`. Each toggle is decided in the API (`"value": "toggle"`, `{"toggle": true}`, `{"on": "toggle"}`), where the state is, never from a count in the controller's thread.
5. **One fade button.** `POST /api/fade` looks at the fader's label and at Blackout and fades in if the picture is down, else out. The look and the taking of the fader are one step (`Fader.take`), judged again under the level's lock if somebody was quicker. Under Blackout a press fades in and ends the Blackout, as Fade in always did; the button does not flash under Blackout, whose own button shows it. `GET /api/status` gives `mix.fade`. No lock was added and none changed its place.
6. **A fifth light state, flashing.** The Launchpad flashes by itself, from Novation's manual (flash mode `B0h 00h 28h`, a colour less 4); every other light is switched on and off by its own writer thread, four messages a second through the writer's existing limit. A profile says which (`"flash": "device"`).
7. **The three layouts drawn afresh, in zones** (a control's optional `zone`). The Launchpad's grid is the panel's banks, three rows of four each, in three colours; its round buttons are the Room scenes and the screen's four buttons in the panel's order. The drawings and the reasons are in `pvj/MIDI.md`.

**The Mapping screen on a controller is NOT built.** Every change of the mapping needs full access and a controller acts as a presenter. An exception would let whoever stands at the controller change the room's saved mapping. Asked of the owner.

8. **One table of actions for MIDI and OSC** (`pvj/actions.py`), after the owner's "yes, add those to the box's OSC" (2026-10-10; a TouchOSC layout that mimics the panel had shown what OSC could not do). A press and a level in real units become one call into the API from the same entry for both; MIDI's 0 to 127 with its centre and curve stays MIDI's (`midi.level_value`), and OSC gets no dead zone because it sends real values. New over OSC: `/pvj/position/y` (and `/x`), `/pvj/fade`, `/pvj/fadein`, `/pvj/clip/next` and `/prev`, `/pvj/transition` and its duration, `/pvj/flip/h` and `/v`, `/pvj/overlay` and its file, the effect (on or off, next, previous, amount, control n), the shader (by name, next, previous, speed, hue, brightness, control n, preset n) and `/pvj/vibes/dwell`. Freeze is pause, so no `/pvj/freeze`. Three things in the API made room: `POST /api/mix` takes one of its two parts, `POST /api/effects` takes `{"on": true or false}`, and `POST /api/shaders/play` from a controller is queued as a pad's shader is (D73), so OSC's thread never waits for the GPU. `pvj/osc.py` gained a table and one function (`_reach`) and one call of it at the top of `translate`; the receiver was not touched, because the branch `osc-lockdown` is changing who may send.
9. **OSC feedback is NOT built**, on purpose. `pvj/OSC.md` promises "No replies are ever sent" as a safety property. The next step, for the owner to decide: state sent back only when switched on, only to addresses on the allow-list, limited in rate, designed on top of `osc-lockdown`.

**Rollback, shown with master's own code** (`git archive origin/master pvj` in a scratch folder): a stored mapping with `min`, `max`, `invert` and `takeover` loads there and behaves as that release's own (zoom 1 to 200, straight), the four keys ignored; a mapping whose action that release does not know (`position_y`, `fade`) is skipped when a message comes, with no error; when that release saves the map (another mapping added) it writes the unknown mappings and keys back unchanged. One thing does not go back: master's settings IMPORT refuses a file that holds a mapping with an action it does not know ("unknown action"). No settings schema change; nothing new is stored by Part 1 except those keys in a mapping.

**After the independent review** (another model, at b6e1dec; its text is on pull request #115: the feel's arithmetic, the pickup's way back, the profiles, the Launchpad's flash numbers and every OSC address were found right, and its six deliberate breakages were caught by the tests). Three faults of medium weight, all mended with a test:

- *A press in the middle of a fade started from the wrong end* (black first, or full first). The fader now keeps the level its ramp wrote last (`Fader.at`), and the button's new ramp starts there.
- *Two presses that met while the screen was black*: the first to act found a fader the other had just taken, judged the picture lit and faded OUT from the mix's level, a flash of the whole picture. `Fader.take` now keeps what the fader was doing (`Fader.was`) until a ramp or another wish for a level replaces it, and the button decides inside the level's lock, so presses are judged one after another.
- *`/pvj/fadein` and `/pvj/fadeout` read a TouchOSC button's 1 and 0 as seconds.* A 0 is a release now, exactly 1 a press with 2 seconds, any other number still the seconds; `/pvj/fadein/seconds` and `/pvj/fadeout/seconds` take a real time. This changes one old meaning: `/pvj/fadeout 1` was a fade of one second.

And smaller: a stored mapping that fails the check is named in the log once; Rotate moved away from Fade and Blackout (M 7 on the nanoKONTROL2 with R 6 and M 8 spare, Rec Arm 8 on the MIDI Mix, whose Solo row has its previous shader back); the reviewer's other drawing of the Launchpad (banks stacked in pairs of rows) is in `pvj/MIDI.md` beside the one built, for the owner to compare.

**The owner's answers of the same day, built in the same round** (his words: "1. rows, 2. the controller can handle corner or point nudges once in that mode. 3. flashing when active, same with blackout"; and for the nanoKONTROL2: "all eight knobs for shaders, zoom x y on faders, if they make sense. otherwise, use a button to switch to the geometry controls and flash the button when in that state"):

- **The Launchpad's banks in rows**: one bank per pair of rows, six and six (every bank the same shape, pad 7 under pad 1, and the two columns left over one strip beside all three: the presets). The blocks of three rows of four are gone; `pvj/MIDI.md` says in a line that they were tried.
- **Blackout flashes for as long as it is on**, on the panel and on the lights, as the fade button does for its own state. Each shows its own: under Blackout the fade button is steady. A press of fade under Blackout still fades in and ends the Blackout: that does not contradict "each shows its own state", and a fade button that did nothing over a black screen would look broken.
- **Layers on a controller, one mechanism for two things** (`LAYERS` in `pvj/midi.py`; a control's `"layers"` in a profile; `MidiMapper.active_layer`): a state in which some controls have another self, a button that flashes while it lasts, one layer at a time.
  - *Geometry* on the nanoKONTROL2. The faders were weighed and do not make sense for zoom and position (all in use, no middle to feel, a fader left low is a picture left small), so it is his "otherwise": all eight knobs on the shader, M 6 a plain press into Geometry, knobs 1 to 3 zoom and the positions with pickup, knobs 4 to 8 resting, out by M 6 or after two minutes without a touch. On the way back a shader knob waits until it is where it stood when it last set the shader: the box has no cheap way to know a shader input's value as a knob position (it would have to read the engine's values, under its lock, for every type of input), and the knob's own last place is exact for what the knob did.
  - *Mapping mode.* The permission is a switch of the owner's, `control.mapping` in the settings (a key an older version ignores and keeps; not taken from an import; off after a reset), set through `POST /api/mapper/remote` by full access only. The mode lives in the mapper's memory (`Engine.remote`), is entered by an action pressed twice, shows the panel's own "Edit on the display", and ends by the button, the switch, or three minutes after the last thing done in it; not by Blackout, Stop, a fade or a pad. A nudge is `Engine.handle`'s own `move`, so the whole mapping is checked each time; the route `POST /api/mapper/nudge` is for the presenter role but takes only the MIDI and the OSC device, so a presenter's phone gets no way round the Mapping screen. No lock was added: the mode's few fields are under the mapper's existing `mapper.state`.
  - Not built: a larger step while a button is held (the step button cycles 1, 10, 50), lights for the controls' other selves, a mode button on the MIDI Mix's standard layout.

Cost / rules out: the hand that had learned the first layouts starts again (MIDI.md lists what moved); every stored mapping of zoom, the speeds and the shader's speed changes its feel, because the centre moved to the middle; the nanoKONTROL2 has four shader knobs where it had eight; the effect's controls as presses are gone from the Launchpad; the Launchpad is put into its flash mode at every plug-in, which no test on hardware has seen.

Open, for the owner: whether a controller may change the mapping; whether the Launchpad's blocks of three rows of four are right, or rows of six, or one large bank; whether the fade button should end a Blackout; whether 25 percent is the right bottom for zoom.

**Amended after the independent review of #115 at `80dcb67` (2026-10-10, the same branch).** Two medium findings and four low ones, each with a test that failed first. Not on a controller, a projector or the Pi, like the rest.

- **A layer that changes resets pickup however it changed.** Geometry's button parked the shader knobs and cleared the levels' pickup; mapping mode, which comes and goes in the mapper's memory and re-purposes knobs 2 and 3 in the same way, did not, so a knob used as a nudge set the shader to wherever it had been turned as soon as the mode ended. Now `MidiMapper.matching` remembers the layer that was on at each controller's last message and does the same clearing (`_afresh`) on any change, including none to mapping and back and mapping over Geometry. The same clearing forgets where a nudging knob stood: found by the second read of this fix, the other direction of the same change (into the mode a second time, the knob nudged by all it had been turned as the shader's knob meanwhile, 89 steps in the test). Decided: met at the controller's next message, not pushed from the mapper, so there is no hook to forget for a new road into or out of the mode (a controller, OSC, the owner's switch, the three minutes).
- **Nudges are taken at once and saved a little later.** Each nudge was two files forced to the card and a new picture; a knob gives 20 a second and OSC lets 200 through. Now a nudge is checked and taken into the mapping in memory as before, the settings are written at most twice a second (`REMOTE_SAVE_GAP`) and the picture made at most 25 times a second (`REMOTE_SHOW_GAP`), each with one last time when the nudges stop, and leaving the mode saves at once. Cost: a service that stops within half a second of a nudge loses the nudges since the last save, and nothing else. The panel's own changes are saved and shown at once, as they were.
- **One step of undo is one run of nudges**: the nudges of one corner with less than a second between one and the next (`REMOTE_RUN_GAP`); another surface, corner or step size ends a run. Undo puts the corner back where it stood before the run by the panel's own "place", which is exact where a reversed move is not (a point of the picture held at its edge) and cannot exceed a move's limit. Still one step, not a history. Rules out: taking back a single click of a knob, or one of several quick taps.
- **The panel's editor is borrowed and given back.** The mode uses "Edit on the display" and the card's selection. The state at entry is remembered and put back when the mode ends by any road; the card says a controller is adjusting and that the selection moves meanwhile. Not done: a selection of the mode's own, separate from the panel's (it would need a second set of marks on the display).
- **A 0 on `/pvj/mapping/mode` is a button's release**, as on the fade addresses: 1 enters, no argument switches over. Leaving is `/pvj/mapping/mode/off`; `/pvj/mapping/mode/on` and `/pvj/mapping/mode/toggle` (one momentary button for both) came with it. Breaks: a sender that left the mode with `/pvj/mapping/mode 0` (one day old, no layout uses it).
- **The Launchpad's presenter code is on the round button Top 3**, not on pad 6.8 beside bank C's pad 12. Top 3 rather than Top 4: Top 4 stays spare between it and Rotate.
- **Master merged in (#117, #118 and D78's three locks on who may send OSC).** The locks are about the sender and stand before any address is read, so nothing in `pvj/osc.py` had to change for D75's addresses or mapping mode's; tests now hold each lock against one of each, with the key's prefix in front.
- Not in this round, as before: Parts 2 and 3 (editable layouts, a card per controller, Map Mode) and OSC replies.

**Where its panel pieces live after the merge with the four areas (D65, D72; master at `0b5faeb`, merged 2026-10-10, late).** The old Live screen is gone, so: the one fade button is the strip's `b('fade')` in `transport()`, drawn by `fadeButton()` with the strip's own classes (`btn tpb tpx fold f-fades`: on the line from 1280 px, behind More under that, as the two were), and `fadeShow()` is called from `patchLive()`, which also sets Blackout's `flash` on the strip's Blackout; on a phone's open strip Fade and Loop are each two wide on one row. The Signal look restates the flash after its block (`blackflash` keeps Blackout in its problem colour). The MIDI page and its controller cards are a Setup page (`pagePart('midi')`), unchanged in substance. The Mapping screen is a desk of its own (D72, changed 2026-10-10): the "a controller is adjusting" message stands in the stage under the display, and the owner's switch "Controllers may adjust the mapping" (`#mapremote`) in the stage with what acts on the whole mapping. `tests/ui/shell.js` holds the strip with one fade button and lists the old layouts' fifteen labels as replaced, each with what stands for it. Decided here: Fade is not on a phone's closed strip (Freeze, Stop, Blackout, More; five at 320 px would be 62 px each where Blackout needs 69), and the fold widths are unchanged.

## D77. A full-access device can read the owner PIN from the panel, and every device can log out

2026-10-10. The owner's words: "i need to be ab to see the Owner pin from the control panel, if Im logged in as an Owner. allow for logouts." Until now the PIN was readable only at the box (`sudo pvj-pin`), on the display while nothing was paired, or by making a new one with New PIN; and "Leave this panel" only forgot the device in the browser, the token stayed good on the box for a year.

What was decided:

- **`POST /api/pin/show`, full access only, answers `{"known": true, "pin": "1234"}`.** A POST, not a GET: it needs the request header like every change, so no other website can ask, and the PIN is never in an address, a browser history or a cache (`Cache-Control: no-store` as on every API answer). A presenter or a guest gets 403. A remote support login gets 403 whatever its role, twice over: the route is in `support.REMOTE_DENY` (the guard runs before the handler) and the handler refuses a `remote` device itself. A device removed between the token check and the handler gets 401 (`_still_paired`). The PIN is in no other answer: a test walks every GET the API has, the diagnostics file and a settings export and finds the PIN only in this one route.
- **The box knows the PIN in clear only when this run made it.** `Auth.current_pin` is set by `_new_pin` (at every start on the box, and by New PIN); a run that started on a kept settings file knows only the scrypt hash and answers `{"known": false, "pin": null}`. The panel then says so and offers New PIN, which makes one and shows it. No settings field was added and no secret is stored in clear: this is what `pvj-pin` already read from the run folder, now readable by the owner's device too.
- **Rate limited per device, and one journal line per answer, never with the PIN.** `auth.PIN_SHOWS` (10) in `PIN_SHOW_WINDOW` (5 minutes) per device, counted whether the PIN is known or not, so the timing of a refusal says nothing; after that 429 with `Retry-After`. Each answer writes "owner PIN shown to device <id> (<name>) from <address>" (or "asked for by", with "not known to this run of the panel"). The journal line is the one new cost worth watching: a full-access device could already rotate the PIN and read the new one, invite devices and draw the PIN on the room screen, so Show gives it no power it did not have; but a stolen full-access cookie can now learn the PIN quietly, where before it had to rotate it and the owner would have noticed the old PIN failing. The line in the journal is what remains of that notice.
- **In the panel the PIN is behind Show, never on the screen by default.** A laptop may be on a projector or a stream. Show puts it in a read-only field for 12 seconds (`PIN_SHOW_MS`), then it clears itself; it is gone when the page is left, since the card is rebuilt. Copy asks the box again (counted and in the journal like a Show), selects the field and copies it, with "Select the PIN and copy it" as the fallback on plain http where the clipboard API is missing. New PIN sits beside them and shows the new PIN the same way.
- **`POST /api/logout`, any role, idempotent.** The device's token is removed from the box (`auth.revoke` of its own id) and the server answers with the cookie expired, the same `Path=/; HttpOnly; SameSite=Strict` as the cookie it set, `Max-Age=0`. A second press, or a device the owner removed meanwhile, gets the same 200 and the same expired cookie (`ended: false`), so a panel ends up logged out either way; without the request header nothing happens (403). The route needs no role in the table (`None`) for that reason, and the body is ignored. A support login's logout forgets that one token (`SupportManager.logout`); the session goes on until Stop or its deadline, and the login still counts toward `MAX_LOGINS`, so logging out buys no second login. No "log out every device": the WIP had one, nobody asked for it, and it would not have ended a support login; the owner removes devices one by one as before.
- **The last full-access device may log out.** The box then has no device with full access, and nobody can read the PIN from a panel; the way back in is what it always was: the box draws the PIN on its display while nothing is paired and nothing plays (`pinscreen.auto_wanted`, checked in a test), and `sudo pvj-pin` prints it. The confirm says so, and shows the PIN it will need (asked from the box like a Show), or when the panel does not know it, offers New PIN first or logging out anyway.
- **Where the controls are.** Owner: System > People and codes, under Paired devices the own device is marked "this device" with Log out in place of Remove, and further down "The Owner (everything) PIN" with Show, Copy and New PIN. Presenter and guest: System > About and power, the "This phone" card, Log out (it was "Leave this panel"). Each asks in place like the page's other destructive buttons.

Looked at and left: the session cookie lasts a year for every role (a guest code's device is forgotten by the box after 7 days unused, so its cookie dies with it; a link's or the PIN's device lasts until removed, which is what "a link that does not expire" promises); an open panel whose device was logged out elsewhere or removed falls back to the pairing screen at its next answer, since `api()` drops the device on any 401; the Bearer header path for scripts is untouched (a script's logout removes its token, the Set-Cookie it gets is harmless). The panel prints the PIN in its start line in the journal, as before; the journal is root's. **Nothing here has run on the box**: the Mac's tests and CI are the proof so far; two rows in `tools/DEVICE-TESTING.md` say what to try there.

## D78. Three locks on who may send OSC, each off by default; "secure" is said with its limit

2026-10-10. The owner asked to make the connection from TouchOSC to the box secure. (D71 to D77 are taken or reserved by branches open at the same time.) OSC here is receive-only UDP: it cannot be encrypted by the box, anyone who can listen reads it, and a device on the same network can forge a source address. So the decision is what can honestly be given: far fewer devices may send, a sender must know a secret, and the owner sees who sent. Wherever the docs or the page say what this does, the limit is said beside it.

- **Three independent locks on System > OSC, all off until switched on**, so a box that updates behaves as before: "Only these devices may send" (up to 16 single addresses), "A sender must be a paired device", "A key in the address" (`/k/<key>/pvj/stop`). A packet must pass every one that is on; the old names without `/pvj` follow the same rules.
- **The private ranges and the extra networks stay the outer wall, also for a listed address.** The brief left this open. The list narrows who may send; it never lets in an address the wall keeps out. An address outside the wall is refused when the list is saved (the message says to add its network first), and the wall is checked first for every packet.
- **The list is filled by pressing "Allow this one" beside a sender that was seen**, not by typing. The risk that comes with it is said in the docs: check the address is the tablet's own before pressing.
- **The paired-device lock uses the address a paired device last made a panel request from, kept in memory only** (`Auth._addresses`; never in the settings file or an export; gone after a restart). Whether an address counts is worked out for each packet from the devices paired at that moment, with their role at that moment, so removing a device, a factory reset and a guest's expiry need no hook. A guest never counts; the owner chooses owner only or owner and presenter; hours 1 to 72, 12 unless changed. A request through the support tunnel is never remembered. Sending OSC never makes an address count. Logging out (D77, merged while this was in review) removes the device through `auth.revoke`, which also forgets its address, so a logged-out device stops counting at once (tested). A device that reached the panel over IPv6 is named apart and never shown as counting, because the receiver is IPv4 only.
- **The key is made by the box only** (80 random bits, hex, so it is safe in an OSC address), never accepted from a request or a file. It has its own route (`POST /api/osc/key`, full access, refused through the support tunnel), so it is never in what the page polls; showing or renewing it is limited like the owner PIN (D77) and leaves a journal line without the key. A key is never in a shown or logged address (`<key>` stands for it) and an address with the prefix twice is refused. The comparison is `hmac.compare_digest` on bytes. If any message of a packet lacks the key the whole packet is refused. With the lock off a `/k/<anything>` prefix is dropped, so a layout with the key goes on working when the owner switches the lock off to find a fault.
- **No limit on wrong keys by sender (changed after the review of #118).** The first build refused every message from a sender after 20 wrong keys. The reviewer showed it was a lever, not a defence: 20 forged packets with the tablet's address and its right key was refused, and 1024 forged addresses reset the table. 80 bits cannot be guessed at 200 packets a second, so the limit bought nothing. Wrong keys are counted per sender and shown ("N wrong keys from this address"). The rule kept from it: never refuse by source address alone.
- **What a refused packet leaves: its sender's address, a count and the reason.** Never its OSC address, its arguments or the key it tried (a typo is close to the real key), in the senders list, in "Last messages" or in the journal. The brief asked for the address in "Last messages" and for nothing of a refused packet's body to be kept; the strict reading was taken for both lists. A message that passed every lock is shown with its address (64 printable characters at most), whether or not it then meant anything, which is what the owner needs to find a typo in a layout.
- **The lists are in memory, bounded (64 senders, 50 messages) and for a full-access device only.** They live on `OscManager`, because every save makes a new receiver. A flood of the same refusal is one line with a count; when the senders list is full, refused senders go before one that was let in.
- **One save at a time, and the socket stays open when only the locks change** (`OscManager.lock`, and `apply` changes the rules of the running receiver in place when the port is the same). The page saves on every tap, so two saves can be close together; before, each reopened the port, the slower one found it in use and took the other's change back. Keeping the socket open also means no message is lost while the owner allows a device in the middle of a show. A change of port still reopens it.
- **An export never carries the key; an import never takes one.** A settings file gets mailed and kept; the key is a secret of one network, like the PIN. On import the box keeps its own key, or makes one if the file switches the lock on and the box has none (never a blank key). What a file from before this change does not say about the locks stays as the box has it, so an old file cannot switch a lock off. A factory reset clears the keys, the remembered addresses and both lists.
- **No settings schema change, and the key in a section of its own (changed after the review of #118).** The locks are optional settings under `osc`, read through `osc.layers` with "off" for a missing one and fail-closed for a damaged one. The key is in `settings["osc_secret"]`: every release exports only the sections it names, refuses an import that holds a section it does not know, and leaves an unknown section alone when it loads and saves, so no release writes the key into a file or loses it. The reviewer suggested `auth`; that section is written anew at every new PIN (`Auth._new_pin`, also in older releases), which would lose the key, so it has its own. Tried against master's own code (2026-10-10): it loads the file, keeps the lock settings and the key through a save of its OSC page and two new PINs, its export does not hold the key, and its import leaves the section alone; it does not enforce the locks and its import drops the lock settings. A key found under `osc` (the first build kept it there) is moved when the receiver is set up (`OscManager._move_key`), with no schema bump. That is said in `pvj/OSC.md`.

Not done: an IPv6 socket (the receiver is IPv4 only as before, so an IPv6 address in the list matches nothing); any reply or handshake (the receiver still never sends). **Nothing here has met a real TouchOSC or a box.** The browser test was written without being run on the development Mac (no Playwright there); CI runs it.

## D79. A secure connection to the box: the owner's own root certificate signs one certificate per box; HTTPS beside HTTP, never instead of it

2026-10-10. The owner: "I would like to make this a secure connection, please", and after reading the five options in `docs/WEBAPP.md` (branch `webapp-install`): "build the 'own certificate authority on your Mac' route". His facts: he owns no domain, the box has internet at the venue only sometimes, and "Keep in mind that I may expand this to multiple boxes". What he was told and what this keeps true: ONE root certificate of his own signs a certificate for each box; each phone, tablet or laptop installs the root once and then trusts every box he ever adds; the root's private key never sits on a box; the key is a file plus a passphrase, not tied to one computer, and he backs it up; guests and devices without the root stay on plain HTTP; a switch keeps the PIN and owner sessions off the clear; each box's certificate is renewed about once a year; every device pairs again once, because https:// is a different origin. `docs/HTTPS.md` has the verified facts with their sources and the steps for a person. **Nothing of this has run on the box or met a real phone**; the proof so far is the tests on the development Mac and in CI.

**The threat model, in plain words.**

- *Someone on the same Wi-Fi who listens.* Today the PIN at pairing, every session cookie and everything the panel shows travel in the clear. With HTTPS and the switch "Owner access only over the secure connection" on, the PIN and the owner's cookie never cross the air in the clear; a listener sees only encrypted bytes to port 443. A listener still sees guests' codes and their cookies over HTTP (a guest's code gives view access for a while, which is what it gave before), and still sees that a box exists and who talks to it.
- *Someone who can answer for the box's name* (a laptop that says it is `nxlx-mastercontrol.local`, a rogue hotspot). Over HTTPS a device with the root refuses the impostor: it has no certificate signed by the owner's root for that name. Over HTTP nothing changes: the impostor can show a fake pairing page and collect a PIN typed into it. That is why the switch matters: with it on, the real box never accepts the PIN over HTTP, so a PIN typed into a fake HTTP page is a PIN the attacker cannot use against the real box over HTTP either, only over HTTPS, where the stolen PIN still works. The PIN is four digits and rotates at every start; the switch does not make it long.
- *Someone who steals a box or its SD card.* They get the box's own key and certificate, and can impersonate that one box to devices that trust the root, until the owner signs a new certificate for a new key and the old certificate runs out (there is no revocation list: `docs/HTTPS.md` says what to do, and that the old certificate stays good for the devices until its end date). They do NOT get the root's key: it is not on the box, so they cannot sign for any other box or any other name. They get everything else the card holds, as before (settings, the token hashes, the clips).
- *Someone who steals the root's key file.* With the passphrase they can sign a certificate for any name the root allows. The root carries name constraints (below), so on a platform that enforces them that is `.local` names and private addresses, not `bank.example`. Without the passphrase the file is encrypted (AES-256). The cure is in `docs/HTTPS.md`: a new root, re-sign every box, remove the old root from every device.
- *Someone with a sniffed plain-http owner token, once the certificate has run out* (the third review of #119). The box then opens three routes over http to a paired owner: the status, the request download and the certificate upload; the PIN, pairing and everything else stay shut. The box pins the root it was first given and accepts only certificates that root issued (checked by `openssl verify`), and the root is replaced only over https by a device paired there, with its fingerprint confirmed. So what remains to him is to renew the box with a certificate the owner's root issued, which he cannot make, and to read the request, which holds the box's public key and names and no secret.
- *A guest with a code.* Unchanged: a code gives view or live access, over HTTP, as before. Guests cannot reach any of the certificate routes (full access only) and cannot read the root from the box. A guest who happens to have the root installed can open https:// and use their code there too; nothing is lost by that.
- *What it does not stop.* A device the owner paired that is itself compromised; a camera on the owner's screen; anybody who can run commands on the box; the four-digit PIN being guessed over HTTPS (the same throttle as before); and a box whose clock is wrong is not a weakness here (the visiting device judges the dates, see below).

**What was decided.**

- **The root is made and kept on the owner's computer by `tools/boxcert.py`, with Python 3 and the system's `openssl` and nothing else.** `make-root` asks for a passphrase twice, writes `root.key` (an encrypted ECDSA P-384 key) and `root.pem` (the public root, valid 20 years), refuses to overwrite, prints where they are and says to back the key up and never to put it on a box. The folder is `~/nxlx-root-ca` unless told otherwise. It works on macOS (LibreSSL 3.3 in `/usr/bin/openssl` and OpenSSL 3 both), Linux and Windows where `openssl` is on the path: every extension goes through a configuration file, not `-addext`, because LibreSSL lacks the flag. `sign` takes the box's request file, reads the names out of it, lets him add or drop names and addresses, and writes `<box>-cert.pem` holding the box's certificate followed by the root, so he uploads one file. `show`, `verify`, `list` and `renew` (which is `sign` again with the same request) complete it. The tool keeps `signed.json` beside the root: box, names, serial, start, end, so he sees what is due.
- **The certificate's shape follows Apple's rules, which are the strictest found** (`docs/HTTPS.md`, "Facts"): names in the Subject Alternative Name, `serverAuth` extended key usage, SHA-256, ECDSA P-256 for the box's key, a random 128-bit serial, and **397 days** by default (`--days` up to 825). Apple says its 398-day limit does not apply to certificates from user-added roots and its 825-day limit applies to all; 397 keeps the yearly rhythm the owner was told, with a month of slack, and is under both.
- **The root carries name constraints**: permitted DNS `local` (which covers `nxlx-mastercontrol.local` and every other `.local` name) plus the names given at `make-root`, and the private address ranges 10/8, 172.16/12, 192.168/16 and 169.254/16. Apple (iOS 9 and macOS 10.12 onward), Chrome (on every platform since Chrome 112 for user-added roots), Firefox and Android enforce them (`docs/HTTPS.md` has what each source says and how firm it is). The cost is that a certificate for a public address or a name outside the list is refused by those platforms; the tool says so when asked to sign one. The gain is that a stolen root key cannot be turned against the owner's own devices for any site on the internet. Nothing found says a current platform rejects a constrained root outright; if a device of his does, `make-root --no-constraints` makes one without.
- **The box's key is made on the box and never leaves it; the owner carries a request to the tool and a certificate back.** The simpler way (the tool makes key and certificate, he uploads both) would send the box's private key over plain HTTP once, to a box he has not yet verified, which is the one moment the whole exercise is about; and with several boxes it would put every box's key on his computer. The request route costs him one download before the signing and one upload after, both from the same page. The box makes the key and the request with the operating system's own `openssl` (the panel runs it with fixed arguments as its own user; Debian installs `openssl` with `ca-certificates`); if a box has no `openssl` the page says so, and nothing else is offered in this version.
- **The panel itself terminates TLS, on port 443 beside port 80, same panel, same API.** The key is read by the process that needs it and by nothing else: `/var/lib/pvj/tls/` is made by the panel with mode 0700 and `key.pem` 0600, both pvj-web's; the player (group pvj, a different user) cannot enter the folder, and the root helpers run as root and do not look. A root helper that terminated TLS and relayed to the panel was considered and rejected: it would double the server, hide the client's address from the panel, and gain nothing, because whoever owns the panel process already owns everything the key protects. Port 443 needs no new privilege: `pvj-web.service` already has `CAP_NET_BIND_SERVICE` for port 80. `PVJ_HTTPS_PORT=443` in the unit; unset, there is no second listener. The listener is always there when the port is set; with no certificate every handshake is refused at once, so "HTTPS off" and "no certificate" are the same thing and need no restart to change. TLS 1.2 is the floor, the ciphers are the library's defaults, and no client certificate is asked for.
- **An uploaded certificate is checked before it is taken into use, and each failure has its own message**: it must parse (PEM, first certificate is the box's, a second one may be the root and is kept for download), it must not be a CA certificate (that is the root, uploaded by mistake), it must carry `serverAuth`, its names must include the name or address the uploading device reached the box by, it must match the box's key (the standard library's `load_cert_chain` refuses a mismatch), and it must not have run out, judged ONLY when the box's clock is set from the network; otherwise the box takes it and says the end date cannot be judged here. The one before is kept as `previous.pem` for Undo. Remove returns the box to HTTP only; the key stays for the next request, and "new key" on the request makes a new key, which drops the certificate in use because it no longer matches.
- **A wrong or run-out certificate never locks the owner out.** HTTP keeps working as the way back in; the switch below is effective only while a certificate is loaded; and the box never refuses to serve a certificate because of its own clock. The visiting device judges validity. The page says what it knows: "ends on", a warning from 30 days before, a clear line after, and when the box's clock is not from the network that the date is as the box counts it.
- **Cookies.** Over HTTPS the session cookie is `__Host-pvj_token` with `Secure` (the prefix forces `Secure`, `Path=/` and no `Domain`); over HTTP it stays `pvj_token`. Two names, because a cookie's identity is name, domain and path, not scheme: with one name, pairing over HTTP after pairing over HTTPS would be silently refused by browsers that leave Secure cookies alone, and the HTTP page would seem paired and get 401 on every call. The server reads the secure name only over TLS. Logout over HTTPS expires both names, over HTTP the plain one. Each device record remembers whether its token was issued over the secure connection (`secure: true`; an older release ignores the key).
- **The switch "Owner access only over the secure connection"** (`https.owner_only`, off by default) can be turned on and off only from an HTTPS request by a full-access device whose token was issued over HTTPS, so the owner cannot lock himself out, and the box's own console is the other way back (`sudo rm -r /var/lib/pvj/tls` and a restart of `pvj-web` take HTTPS away, and with it the switch's effect). While it is on and a certificate is loaded: over HTTP the PIN is not accepted at pairing, a full-access device gets 403 with the https:// address on every route but hello, logout and the secure-connection status itself (so a panel can explain and log out), the PIN reveal of D77 is among them; and a full-access token issued over HTTP is refused the same way over HTTPS too, because it may have been listened to: the owner pairs again over https://. Guests and presenters keep working over HTTP: a presenter's code is shown on the room screen for people who will not install a root, and a presenter can neither read the PIN nor change settings, so the clear costs what it cost before.
- **No HSTS, no redirect for everybody.** HSTS would make every browser that saw it refuse http:// for the name, and guests have only http://. The page over HTTP tries one small fetch of `https://<same host>/api/https/probe` and says whether this device trusts the box; if yes it offers the link to https://; it never redirects by itself. *Amended 2026-10-10, the trust check's policy.* The first version relied on `connect-src 'self'` matching the https form of the same host: that is a CSP level 3 rule for the default ports only, unverified for Safari on iOS (the owner's main device), and the browser test in CI, whose ports are not the default ones, refused the fetch outright once an earlier failing step stopped hiding it. Three ways were weighed. (a) The server names the box's own `https://` origin in the policy, built from the Host header after strict checks. (b) No cross-origin fetch at all: the check becomes the link to https:// that the user follows, with the result shown there. (c) A different mechanism (an image or a frame): these need the same policy change for `img-src` or `frame-src`, and the panel's `frame-ancestors 'none'` would refuse a frame anyway. Chosen: (a), as narrow as it can be made, because (b) gives up the in-page answer the page is for ("self-contained pages" is a design rule here) and sends the owner into the browser's certificate warning to learn "no", which the link beside the button already does for whoever wants it. How narrow: the origin is added to the HTML document only (the policy on JSON and script answers governs nothing), over plain http only (over TLS `'self'` already is that origin), while a certificate is in use only, and only when the Host header is a clean DNS name of ASCII labels, a dotted IPv4 address or a bracketed IPv6 address (a tight pattern; a port is allowed and dropped) that the certificate in use carries (`name_matches`, the browser's rule); what is written is the matched name in canonical lower case and the TLS listener's own port, never a byte copied from the header; and the header builder refuses anything but that one shape a second time. What an attacker can try and what stops it: a header with `;`, spaces, a second origin or a newline is refused by the pattern (and a newline never reaches it); a foreign name is refused with 421 before any page is served (DNS rebinding, `host_allowed`) and would not be in the certificate anyway; a name the box answers to but the certificate lacks (`localhost`, another box's name, an IPv6 literal) gets the plain policy; and the one origin that can be added is the box itself, so an injected script gains no third host to send to. The probe route stays as it was: `{"https": true|false}`, no `Access-Control-Allow-Origin`, so an opaque answer and nothing a page elsewhere can read. The panel asks only in the case the policy allows (a certificate in use whose names include this host) and otherwise answers by itself: "no certificate in use yet", or "the certificate does not carry this address, open the box as one of its names". Rejected along the way: a `https:` scheme source or a wildcard (an injected script could then post anywhere over https), the origin on every answer (no gain), and taking the port from the Host header (the http port is not the https port, and it is the one part of the header that is not checked against the certificate). Nothing of this was tried on a real phone or on the box; the tests send the headers with the standard library's client, and CI's Chromium exercises the no-certificate branch only.
- **Settings and files.** No schema change (master is 14; another branch holds 15): one key `https` (`owner_only`, and the extra names he typed for the request) that an older release ignores. The certificate, the key, the request and the root are files in `/var/lib/pvj/tls/`, in no export, no import and no diagnostics file, and a test reads every GET's body for them. A factory reset removes the folder and the box returns to HTTP only; the reset's confirm says so. An update through the Updates card does not touch `/var/lib/pvj`, so they stay.
- **The page**, System > Secure connection, full access only, in small functions of its own in `pvj/web/app.js`: the state, the request to download, the certificate to upload, the root to download with install steps for the device being held (chosen by its user agent, in each platform's own words from `docs/HTTPS.md`), the trust check, the link to https://, and the switch. Reached over HTTP it invites; reached over HTTPS it confirms.

Cost: a second listener and a second cookie name in the server; a certificate to renew once a year per box, by hand, with the page warning a month ahead; and the per-device install of the root, which is four to eight taps with frightening words in them, for the owner's own devices only. What is not built: a box without `openssl` gets no key; no revocation; no automatic renewal (the root is not on the box, by design); no certificate for a public address; nothing was tried on a box or on a real phone.

## D80. Three levels: Owner, Operator, Guest; guest controls with one lock, open by default; controllers and support keep the reach they had

The owner, 2026-10-10: "let's revisit the role permissions. Owners/Admins have ultimate authority. Normal daily users can take over the Presenter role position, but may get a new name, and have almost all the same controls as the Owner/Admin, except for system critical options. Guests can still exist but mostly to play around, like powering projectors, choosing scenes, videos, shaders, etc." Then: the daily role is called Operator; "3 levels, not locked by default. owners and operators can lock. an operator may delete clips and remove guests. they run the room day to day."; a guest may power projectors off "yes with confirm, but only when guest controls are unlocked"; an Operator's code is made by an "Owner only, room screen allowed".

The stored role ids do not change: `view` is the Guest, `live` the Operator, `full` the Owner. No settings schema change and no change to a device record. The panel and the manual say Owner, Operator, Guest; "Presenter" is gone from both.

**How it is enforced.** One gate, `Api.gate`, that every request passes: the route table's `handle()` and the five paths the server answers outside it (the snapshot, the QR code, the two uploads, the settings import). In order: (1) the support tunnel's own rules (D7, unchanged); (2) a controller identity (MIDI, OSC, DMX, a Room scene or the schedule), a support session that is not `full`, and a `live` device that was paired with a controller-drawn code before this change (decided while building: anyone at the controller could make that code with no owner there, which is the very reason such a code now pairs a Guest) may only use a route in `policy.LEGACY_LIVE`, a list written out by hand of what a presenter could reach before this change, so raising the Operator raises none of them; (3) the route's minimum role; (4) for a Guest on a route above his role, the guest controls: the lock must be open, the request's body must have one of the forms in `policy.GUEST`, the rate limits must have room, a power-off must carry a confirm token, and the action is logged with the device's name.

**The lock.** One state for the box, "Guest controls: open or locked", open by default by the owner's choice. An Operator or an Owner changes it (`POST /api/guests {"locked": true}`); it is in `GET /api/status` for every device; it is kept under the settings key `guest_controls`, which is optional (missing means open; a damaged value means locked), not exported and not imported (it is in `boxcare.NEVER`, with the other things that belong to one box; a file that carries it is refused by this version, and master's import leaves a section it does not know as it is), and gone after a factory reset. A settings file with the key loads and is saved unchanged by master's own `settings.py` (tried, see the journal). Locking also ends every power-off confirm that was waiting; the lock is looked at a second time under the controls' own lock, so a request that was on its way when the lock was set leaves no confirm behind. One request that had already passed the gate when the lock was set still runs. A scene a guest started before the lock runs to its end.

**Because the lock is open by default, every guest device and every guest link that exists on a box today gains the guest controls at the update.** The manual and the hand-off say so first under "What changes when you update", and the lock is one tap on Room and on People and codes.

**What a Guest may do while the lock is open** (the forms in `policy.GUEST`; anything else on these routes is refused as before):

- Play a pad, or a clip that is on the box (`{"pad": [bank, index]}` or `{"file": name}`, with `loop` if wanted). Not a stream, a USB drive, a slideshow, a capture input or a mix preset.
- Next, Previous and Stop. Blackout on and off. (Chosen: whoever can start a picture can stop it and darken it. Not pause, seek, speed or loop: nobody asked for them.)
- Ambience (Vibes) on with a set, off, next and previous. Not the dwell time (it is saved).
- A shader by its id, one of its saved presets, and the next or previous shader. Never live values, never saving.
- An effect on by its id or one of its presets, off, next and previous. Never its values or controls. (Chosen yes: an effect is undone with one tap, it is what the room sees, and the owner said "shaders, etc.". Its cost on a Pi 4 is bounded by Effect detail, which a guest cannot change.)
- A projector or a group: power on, choose an input, and power off with the confirm below. Not the mutes, not Refresh details (it saves what the projector answered).
- A scene, every one of them, with two exceptions: a scene that plays a stream is refused (the box would connect to an outside address), and a scene that switches a group off needs the same confirm as a direct power-off. (Chosen over a "guests may use" mark on each scene: it stores nothing and needs no new control. The mark is an open question for the owner.)
- Not for a guest, open or locked: size, position, rotate, flips, opacity, fades, volume, mute, the sound output, the overlay, the mapping, test pictures and tones, uploads, deletes, renames, editing anything stored, codes, devices, any setting.

**Power-off needs two requests.** A guest's power-off (one projector, a group, or a scene with a group switched off) is answered with 409 and `{"confirm": {"token", "seconds": 30}}`; the same request sent again with `"confirm": token` within 30 seconds does it. A token is random, works once, belongs to that device and that exact request, and is checked after the lock, the form and the limits. Operators and Owners are not asked by the server (the panel asks them as before).

**Limits on guests.** Each guest device: 10 actions in 10 seconds. All guests together: 30 in 10 seconds. Every request on a guest route counts, a refused form and a scene that is not there too (review of #120). Play, blackout and a scene, the three that change the whole picture at once: one of them for all guests together in 2 seconds (`policy.COOL_SECONDS`; chosen as slower than any flicker that harms and still one tap after another for a person; next, stop, ambience, shaders, effects and projectors are not in it, and an Operator or Owner never is). Power-offs (direct, a group, or in a scene): one per device and two for the box in 5 minutes. A power-off and the cooldown are held from the moment the request is let in and given back if the handler did not answer 200, so only what happened is counted, and only what happened is written in the journal (review of #120: a switch-off on a box with no projector had been logged and counted). Over a limit: 429 with `retry_after`. A device that went quiet leaves no entry in the counters. A projector therefore cannot be strobed by guests: switching on again and again does nothing to a projector that is on, and off is limited.

**Nothing a guest does is saved.** The forms above were chosen so that none writes the settings. A test counts the saves around every form whose handler runs to its end on the test rig (play, control, blackout, scene, ambience off); for shaders, effects and projectors, which the rig cannot run to the end, a second test reads from the source which functions save in those modules and holds that list. What the box saves by itself stays as it was: a guest device's last-used day, at most once a day, and a projector's details when the monitor first reads them from a projector that came on (whoever switched it on).

**Codes and devices.**

- An Operator's code, link and QR code are made by an Owner only, as a presenter's were (D48). They may be drawn on the room screen; the panel says once, beside that choice, that anyone who sees the screen can then run the room.
- An Operator makes guest codes (the bounds of D48 stay) and now also guest links (`POST /api/devices/invite` with `view` only), sees the guest devices (`GET /api/devices` gives an Operator the guests and nobody else) and removes a guest device (`POST /api/devices/revoke`; an Operator or Owner device answers 403 to him).
- A code a MIDI controller draws on the display (D61): its "join" kind now pairs a **Guest**, not an Operator. It needs no owner present and anyone at the controller can make it, and the Operator is now close to an owner. The "owner" kind stays behind its own switch. This reverses the owner's answer of 2026-10-08 (D63: "keeps pairing a presenter"), which was given when a presenter could only play and mix; it is an open question for him. Such a guest goes after 7 unused days like a guest from a code.

**Controllers.** MIDI, OSC, DMX, a Room scene and the schedule keep exactly the reach of before (`LEGACY_LIVE`), whatever the Operator gains. OSC still switches a projector off with no confirm, as before: the confirm is for guests. The lock is not an OSC or MIDI action: locking would be harmless, unlocking would give a controller with no login a say over who controls the room, so neither is built.

**Remote support.** A `full` session is an Owner's minus `REMOTE_DENY`, as before. A `live` session keeps the presenter's reach of before (`LEGACY_LIVE` minus `REMOTE_DENY`) and gains none of the Operator's new powers. A `view` session stays read-only: it never has guest controls, open or locked. The lock itself (`POST /api/guests`) is in `REMOTE_DENY` (review of #120): who may play with the room is decided by an Operator or an Owner who is in it; a support session of any role neither opens the room to guests nor shuts them out.

**The two questions the brief left to this decision.** The mapper (`POST /api/mapper`: surfaces, their corners, on and off, saved mappings): **Operator**. It is adjusted during a show, holds no secret and reaches nothing outside the box. MIDI layout, Learn and lights: **Owner**, for now. A mapping is shared by everyone at the controller, a mapping can put "Show a full access code" on a pad, and the layouts are being rebuilt in #115. An open question for the owner.

**The table.** One row for every route and every path handled outside the route table. "Before" is the lowest role that could use it on master (G guest, P presenter, O owner, none: no login). Then who may after: Guest with the lock open, Guest locked, Operator, Owner. "part" means some forms only, as written above or in the last column.

| Route | What it does | Before | Guest, open | Guest, locked | Operator | Owner | Why |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| GET /api/hello | Says what the box is | none | yes | yes | yes | yes | Needed before pairing |
| POST /api/pair | Pairs with the PIN or a code | none | yes | yes | yes | yes | The way in |
| POST /api/session | Starts a session from a link | none | yes | yes | yes | yes | The way in |
| POST /api/logout | Logs this device out | none | yes | yes | yes | yes | Everyone may leave (D77) |
| POST /api/support/login | Support's own login through the tunnel | none | yes | yes | yes | yes | Unchanged (D7) |
| GET /api/status | What plays, the mix, the lock | G | yes | yes | yes | yes | Read only |
| GET /api/media | The clips | G | yes | yes | yes | yes | Read only |
| GET /api/pads | The pads | G | yes | yes | yes | yes | Read only |
| GET /api/modules | Which features are on | G | yes | yes | yes | yes | Read only |
| GET /api/theme | The look in use | G | yes | yes | yes | yes | Read only; the Look page's details now also for an Operator |
| GET /api/osc | OSC state, without senders for less than Owner | G | yes | yes | yes | yes | Read only |
| GET /api/media/import | Progress of a copy from USB | G | yes | yes | yes | yes | Read only |
| POST /api/media/info | What is in a clip | G | yes | yes | yes | yes | Read only, as before |
| GET /api/system | About the box | G | yes | yes | yes | yes | Read only |
| GET /api/inputs | Capture inputs | G | yes | yes | yes | yes | Read only |
| GET /api/overlay | The overlay | G | yes | yes | yes | yes | Read only |
| GET /api/health | Power, temperature, helpers | G | yes | yes | yes | yes | Read only |
| GET /api/support | Is a support session open | G | yes | yes | yes | yes | Everyone may see it |
| GET /api/sync | Boxes in step, state | G | yes | yes | yes | yes | Read only |
| GET /api/mapper | The mapping | G | yes | yes | yes | yes | Read only |
| GET /api/shaders | Shaders and sets | G | yes | yes | yes | yes | Read only |
| GET /api/effects | Effects | G | yes | yes | yes | yes | Read only |
| GET /api/projectors | Projectors, no address or password below Owner | G | yes | yes | yes | yes | Read only |
| GET /api/room | Groups, scenes, how a scene is going | G | yes | yes | yes | yes | Read only |
| GET /api/audio | Sound outputs | G | yes | yes | yes | yes | Read only |
| GET /api/autostart | Autostart | G | yes | yes | yes | yes | Read only |
| GET /api/streams | Saved streams, no address below Owner | G | yes | yes | yes | yes | Read only |
| GET /api/schedule | The schedule | G | yes | yes | yes | yes | Read only |
| GET /api/preview.jpg | A snapshot of the screen (outside the table) | G | yes | yes | yes | yes | Read only; codes on the display are left out below Owner, as before |
| POST /api/play | Plays something | P | part | no | yes | yes | Guest: a pad or a clip on the box only |
| POST /api/control | Transport, geometry, volume | P | part | no | yes | yes | Guest: next, prev, stop only |
| POST /api/blackout | Blackout on or off | P | yes | no | yes | yes | Who can start can darken |
| POST /api/fadeout | Fades out | P | no | no | yes | yes | A fade is the operator's |
| POST /api/fadein | Fades in | P | no | no | yes | yes | A fade is the operator's |
| POST /api/fade | The one fade button: out, or in if the picture is down (D75) | P | no | no | yes | yes | A fade is the operator's; the controllers keep it |
| POST /api/testpattern | Colour bars | P | no | no | yes | yes | A set-up tool |
| POST /api/testtone | A test tone | P | no | no | yes | yes | It makes noise |
| POST /api/mix | The transition, saved | P | no | no | yes | yes | It is saved |
| POST /api/overlay | The overlay picture, saved | P | no | no | yes | yes | It is saved |
| POST /api/vibes | Ambience | P | part | no | yes | yes | Guest: on with a set, off, next, previous |
| POST /api/shaders/play | Shows one shader | P | part | no | yes | yes | Guest: by id, no values |
| POST /api/shaders/values | Live values of a shader | P | no | no | yes | yes | Performing |
| POST /api/shaders/step | Next or previous shader | P | yes | no | yes | yes | Choosing |
| POST /api/shaders/preset | Applies a saved preset | P | yes | no | yes | yes | Choosing; nothing saved |
| POST /api/effects | An effect on or off | P | part | no | yes | yes | Guest: by id or preset, or off |
| POST /api/effects/values | Live values of an effect | P | no | no | yes | yes | Performing |
| POST /api/effects/step | Next or previous effect | P | yes | no | yes | yes | Choosing |
| POST /api/effects/preset | Applies a saved preset | P | yes | no | yes | yes | Choosing; nothing saved |
| POST /api/projector | Power, input, mutes, details of a projector | P | part | no | yes | yes | Guest: on, input, off with confirm |
| POST /api/room/scene | Applies a scene | P | part | no | yes | yes | Guest: not a scene with a stream; confirm for one that powers off |
| POST /api/room/group | Power, input, mutes of a group | P | part | no | yes | yes | Guest: on, input, off with confirm |

| GET /api/https/probe | Says whether this request came over the secure connection (outside the table, `policy.NO_GATE`) | none | yes | yes | yes | yes | Nothing secret; a page over plain http asks it to learn whether its device trusts the box (D79) |

Controllers (MIDI, OSC, DMX, a Room scene, the schedule) and a support session below `full`: every row whose "Before" is none, G or P, and no other. A support session of any role is refused the routes in `REMOTE_DENY` as before.

D75 (#115) merged into master while this was a draft and brought three routes, with their rows above. The controllers' list `policy.LEGACY_LIVE` was worked out again from master's route table by a script (every route whose minimum there is none, view or live): it gained `/api/fade` and `/api/mapper/nudge` and nothing else, so a controller does what it could on master and no more. A Guest has no Fade: D80 gives him Stop and Blackout and says "not ... fades", and the strip's one Fade button is disabled for him.
