- **The delay is the player's.** Two senders share no clock, and the helper stamps what arrives when it arrives. The owner sets the delay by ear; the panel is to apply it as the player's `audio-delay` for the NDI load only, taken back like the load's other options. The helper is told the number and does nothing with it. (When this was written the number was stored and sent and nothing applied it yet; the journal says when that changed.)
- **After the independent review of #132 (the coordinator's decisions).** The delay belongs to the two-sender mode alone: it is applied only when the sound comes from the chosen other sender, and where mpv takes options with a load it goes with the load. The "sound only" label is advice and never refuses a press; it expires after ten minutes. The sound source is one sender of the name at the machine the Owner allowed, compared by the helper (which is told that one row) and again by the panel, at every status. A sound source that keeps changing the form of its sound is held back with a pause that doubles (3 seconds to a minute) while pictures play without it: it ends a picture's stream once per change and never in a loop.
- **Who.** The Owner, on `POST /api/ndi` (`sound_from`, `sound_delay`), under the same rule for remote support as the other NDI settings. A chosen source must be on the helper's list at that moment and not announced twice under one id; it is added to the allowed list. It cannot be removed from that list while it is the sound source.

Why one for the box, and why this way. One source is what his rig has, and one more connection is what the helper's bounds were measured to hold (below). The stream the player reads is made by the same writer under the same narrowing: nothing new reaches mpv. Each picture's own sound stays the default for whoever has one sender.

Rejected or left for later: a sound source per pad; sound under library clips, or alone with no NDI picture (both need a stream of the helper's with no picture in it, which the player's side does not have); a second pipe of raw sound (D62 says why); playing the picture's own sound when the sound source is away (two answers to "where does the sound come from" on one screen); Dante and AES67 (another module, D30).

Cost and what is not known. One more peer that may be hostile, on ports the helper's sandbox already allows; every one of its blocks passes the same checks before a byte is read. The helper's unit allows 64 tasks: by hand on the test Pi, one process with a finder, a picture connection and a sound-only connection had 38 to 46 tasks at most (35 with the picture alone), and the helper has about five threads of its own besides. That fitted with about ten to spare, so the owner raised the bound to 96 the same day (`TasksMax=96`); a box that is set up for NDI gets the new unit at its next install or update. On the Pi's network on 2026-10-12, with the real library, by hand and not through the box's own services: a picture from Scan Converter with the sound of Test Patterns on an audio-only connection played in mpv 0.40 with two tracks and the sound's clock at real time, and the picture was opened again on the same sound connection. The owner's sound-only sender ("macOS AV Output") connected and sent no sound while it was looked at, so the case he asked for has not been heard or seen with sound in it. Nobody has heard any of it. See [pvj/NDI.md](../pvj/NDI.md).

## D88. Remote support: in a settings file, "on the box's own networks" means another device there (2026-10-15)

D84 holds a settings file brought through the support tunnel to the box's own networks for NDI: no address and no discovery server that is not on one of them, and no `own_discovery`. The review of #137 found a way round the last: the box's own LAN address counted as "on its networks", so a file could name this box itself as a discovery server, which is what holding `own_discovery` back means to prevent. Now the file's rule is the rule a state target is held to (D82, `oscfeedback.not_local`): another device on one of the networks the box is directly connected to now, never the box's own address and never a network's own or broadcast address; the tunnel's network never counts, by its interface's name and by the support session's own network, should a studio network overlap it. `own_discovery` is read as `is True`, as its other readers do, and the box's networks are read once per file. The method is `Api.ndi_on_own_net`.

**What this does not change, the owner's decision (2026-10-15).** The route `POST /api/ndi` stays exactly as Streams for a full-access support session, the owner's answer of 2026-10-08, kept when he was asked again: "Keep my rule". A first version of this branch held the route to the file's rule as well (the Low queued from the review of #125); it reversed his answer, CI's `TunnelSameAsStreamsTest` caught it, and it was taken out. So D84's paragraph "One thing is not symmetric" stands as written. Tests: `NdiTest` in `tests/test_support_reach.py`. Not run on a box.

## D89. Where mpv draws an overlay at a size of its own, a crossfade's still is smaller: the clip's own picture when that is the screen, a large still at half its size, and every step scaled up by the player (2026-10-15)

Why: on the test Pi 4 (2560 x 1440 screen, a 1920 x 1080 clip, mpv 0.40.0) the old picture stands still for about a second before a blend, and the owner called that "not acceptable" and suggested to "work at a lower size and scale up". The coordinator's probe of the same day measured: a screenshot of the window 0.63 to 0.68 s (11.1 MB PNG; 0.58 s as a JPEG, so the cost is reading the screen back, not the PNG writer); a screenshot of the clip alone ("video", 1920 x 1080) 0.38 to 0.44 s (6.2 MB); one `overlay-add` step at full size (2560 x 1440 BGRA) a median of 57 to 71 ms, and one of 1280 x 720 drawn at 2560 x 1440 with mpv's `dw` and `dh` 26 to 38 ms, which worked on mpv 0.40.0; each screenshot made the player drop 17 to 24 frames; `read_still` about 0.13 s at that size, and `faded()` rewrites 14.7 MB per step on top. Branch `transitions-fast`, pull request #138. **Nothing of it has run on the Pi.**

**What is built** (`pvj/transitions.py`, `pvj/player.py`).

1. **Asked once per player process** (`Player.overlay_scales`, `Transitions._scales`): one transparent pixel under overlay 63 with `dw` and `dh` of 1, taken off at once. It is asked by a hold, under `_io`, before anything is frozen, and only while nothing of ours is on the screen (a play during a transition gets the full-size way that once and asks next time). An mpv that refuses (0.37 answers "invalid parameter"; any answer that is an error counts) gets **D71's commands exactly as before** for the life of that process (`pid`), and the panel's journal says so once per process. An mpv that does not answer the question is not taken to refuse: the full-size way this once, and asked again.
2. **The kind of still.** Where the player scales, a still of the clip at its own size (`screenshot-to-file <path> video`) when `Player.video_place` says the clip's own picture is what the screen shows and is smaller than the screen: the mapping is off (`_mapping_mode`), no generator plays (`_source`), the picture is not turned (`video-rotate`, the params' `rotate`), not moved (`video-pan-x`, `-y`) and not enlarged past fitting (`video-zoom` above 0), `osd-dimensions` is of this screen with no margin below 0, the clip's display size (`video-out-params` `dw`, `dh`, else `video-params`) has fewer pixels than the screen and the shape of its place within 1 percent; and the picture over the video (the logo, overlay 10) is off. It is laid at (`ml`, `mt`) at the size of the clip's place. Otherwise a still of the window, as before.
3. **What a still of the clip has and lacks, looked at in the code.** It has the effect and the brightness (D71's spike, round 1, and now `ScaledCase.test_an_effect_and_the_brightness_are_in_a_still_of_the_clip` on 0.37 and 0.40) and a flip (a video filter, in the frame). It lacks everything drawn over the picture. The panel's overlays are 10 (the picture over the video, `pvj/overlay.py`), the pairing QR codes (`pinscreen.QR_IDS`) and 63 (the still); the player's own on-screen text is off (`--osd-level=0`, `--no-osc`), and the only `show-text` is the pinscreen's (codes, the recovery stick, a controller's notice). Every one of those but the logo makes `access_on_screen` true, and a transition then dips before any still is taken (D71). So during a play only the logo can be up, and with it on the still is of the window. It also lacks **the bars beside a clip of another shape**: there the new clip shows at once while the old picture fades in its own place (X27 asks the owner whether that is acceptable). And the player draws it with the overlay's scaler, not the video's: a still drawn at another size may look softer at the moment of the tap than the frame it replaces (X25).
4. **Half size where the still is large** (`read_still(..., half=)`: every other pixel of every other row, by slices of whole rows, no loop over pixels): a still of the window above `HALF_WINDOW` (1280 x 720) pixels, any still above `HALF_ANY` (1920 x 1080). On the owner's rig a still of the window is read at 1280 x 720 and drawn at 2560 x 1440: 3.7 MB a step for `faded()` and the file instead of 14.7. A 1920 x 1080 clip's own still is not halved (8.3 MB a step, drawn at 2560 x 1440); halving it too is the constant `HALF_ANY` if the Pi's steps say so. The window's floor of 1280 x 720 was added on this branch, not asked for: on a small screen a step is cheap (D71: 5 to 14 ms at 1280 x 720) and the detail is worth more.
5. **Wipes and slides** draw a part of the still's file as before, and the part is placed and sized on the screen through `Transitions.on_screen` (both edges rounded, so parts meet, never under one pixel). On a halved still the edge moves in steps of two screen pixels.
6. **`GET /api/status` gives `mix.last`** (what the last transition did, from memory): with `still` ("window" or "video"), `still_size` (the pixels sent, after halving) and `drawn` (where on the screen), beside `still_ms`, `hold_ms`, `steps`, `seconds`, `dropped` and `ended`.
7. **Two rules the stress test asked for.** It ran most rounds on a stand-in that scales and found within seconds that a still of the clip can be of the wrong size: a generator chosen while the still is taken loads its carrier (64 x 36 in the stand-in) before the player counts the change (`Player.play_source` moves `source_epoch` after the load), the screenshot is of the carrier, and the box gave up on transitions. Now (a) **a still of the clip that is not of the size or shape expected is a cut and no reason to give up**; `MISSES` (2) in a row and that player process gets stills of the window from then on, with a line in the journal; and (b) **a still that fails after the player's count of changes of hands moved is no reason to give up** either, as an `end()` already was not. Neither loosens the give-up for a slow or failing box.

**Unchanged, on purpose.** No lock was added, none moved. `_scaling`, `_misses` and `_no_clip` are fields under `_mark`; the question is under `_io` while `_holding` is held, which is the order `pvj/locks.py` already gives (7, then 9, then 10), and the checker held it in every test. `end()` and every caller of it, newest wins, the tickets, `abandon`, the cleanup and `tidy` (the question's pixel goes through `Player.overlay`, with its `O_NOFOLLOW` and 0640, into `overlay-63.bgra`, which `overlay_remove` and `tidy` already remove). **The give-up thresholds stay** (`SLOW` 1.0 s, `SLOW_IN_A_ROW`, `HOPELESS`, `MIN_RATE`, `MAX_DROPS`, `MAX_PIXELS`): the still of the clip (0.38 to 0.44 s measured, plus its reading) and of the window (0.63 to 0.68 s plus a reading of a quarter of the rows' copies) are both under `SLOW` already, and nothing measured says a step rate or a drop count should move; a screen above 2560 x 1440 still dips, since nobody has measured a step there. No settings change, so rollback is as D71's; an older panel ignores `mix.last`.

**What this does not do.** The freeze is shorter, not gone: at a 1920 x 1080 clip on the 2560 x 1440 screen the screenshot goes from about 0.65 s to about 0.4 s, and the reading from about 0.13 s to an estimate of 0.07 (not measured). With the logo on, a mapping or a generator it stays the window's 0.65 s; the steps after it are cheaper. The screenshot still makes the player drop frames.

**Tested** (`tests/test_transitions.py`: `ReadHalf`, `Scaled`, `ScaledRealPlayer`, and the stress test with the stand-in scaling in 80 of 100 rounds and a clip smaller than the screen in 55; `tests/fakempv.py` learnt both kinds of mpv, `osd-dimensions` and a still of the clip; `tests/test_transitions_gpu.py`: `ScaledCase` on a real mpv in a 320 x 180 window, run by the three effects-gpu jobs on 0.37, which hold the full-size way, and by the job ndi-mpv on Debian 13's 0.40 with `PVJ_SCALE_WANT=yes`, a skip failing the step). Each piece of D89 was broken on purpose on a scratch copy, one at a time; what caught it:

| what was broken | the stress test, three runs | by hand |
| --- | --- | --- |
| v the question's pixel left on the screen | 3 of 3 | `ScaledRealPlayer` |
| w the player asked with a still of ours on the screen | 0 of 3 | `Scaled` |
| x the answer kept for the next player process | 0 of 3 | `Scaled` |
| y a scaled crossfade step sent without its display size | 0 of 3 | `Scaled` |
| z a part of a halved still placed by the still's own pixels | 0 of 3 | `Scaled` |
| aa a still of the clip that misses makes the box give up (the fault the stress test found) | 2 of 3 | `Scaled` |
| ab a still of the clip taken with the logo on | 0 of 3 | `Scaled` |
| ac misses of the still of the clip never turn to the window | 0 of 3 | `Scaled` |

The stress test holds the order of things, not what is drawn where, so most of these are the hand tests' to catch; its docstring has the same rows (v to ac).

**Not tested:** anything on the Pi or on a real screen: the times, the steps a second, the frames dropped, how the scaled still looks, the bars. The check list is X24 to X27 in `tools/DEVICE-TESTING.md`.

**After the review of #138** (an independent read at 52f8a01; nothing high, two medium, five low; each fixed with a test that fails without it, by breaking the fix on a scratch copy).

- **A wipe or a slide over a clip with bars took a still of the clip** (medium) and moved it inside the clip's place: a slide off left over a 4:3 clip stopped at the bar's edge with the new clip already beside it. Wipes and slides now take the window's still unless the clip's place is the whole screen; the crossfade keeps the clip's own still (it fades where the clip was). `Scaled.test_a_wipe_or_a_slide_over_a_clip_with_bars_takes_the_window`, all eight, pillarboxed and letterboxed.
- **A refused screenshot of the clip gave up on transitions** (medium): only a `StillError` counted as a miss, so "mpv: error running command" went to the give-up. Now any failure of a still of the clip but no answer and a player that went away is a miss: a cut, and the window's still after `MISSES` in a row, never a give-up. Taking the window's still at once in the same play was not chosen: it would add a second screenshot to the freeze.
- **Lows.** The still of the clip is held to the size the player said (`video-out-params`, halved as `read_still` halves) as well as to the shape, as point 7 said. An `OSError` writing the question's pixel is "cannot tell" (the full size this once, asked again), not a failed still. X24 asks for an effect and a lowered opacity in a still of the clip on the Pi; X25 for the softness of a halved still on a 1920 x 1080 screen too (a window there is above `HALF_WINDOW` and is halved to 960 x 540; left as it is); X26 says an unhalved still of a 1920 x 1080 clip drawn at 2560 x 1440 moves its edge in steps of one or two pixels, not two; X27 asks for the wipe and the slide over bars.

## D90. The screen's mode: Automatic up to 1920 x 1200 at 60 Hz on every box; a mode by hand is tried and goes back by itself unless it is kept (2026-10-15)

**The owner's decision, in his words**, after reading the research on outputs and resolutions: "I want you to proceed with whatever is going to provide the most economic output for performance and clarity and detail in the output." The coordinator read that as the research's recommendation, which is what is built here: the screen's own mode where it is at most 1920 x 1200 at 60 Hz, else the largest mode up to that size at 60 Hz, and a manual choice that cannot leave the room with a black screen.

**Why.** Until now the player took the screen's own preferred mode, whatever it is (`--vo=gpu --gpu-context=drm --profile=fast`, no `--drm-mode`, `pvj/hardware.py`). On the owner's monitor that is 2560 x 1440 at 75 Hz: the costliest size for a Pi 4 (1080p is 56 percent of its pixels, XGA 21) and an uneven beat for his 29.97 fps clips (30 shows evenly on 60 Hz, as 2:2, and unevenly on 50 and 75; 25 shows evenly on 50 and 75). `pvj/SHADERS.md` measured 8.4 to 12.8 ms on the Pi 4 just to scale to 1440p, and D28, D51, D56 and D58 were all measured at 2560 x 1440 at 75 Hz. A projector is the other half: the Epson X27 and X39 take 1024 x 768 and the other computer formats at 60 Hz only, 720p at 50 and 60, and 1080p at 24, 30, 50 and 60 ([X39](https://files.support.epson.com/docid/cpd5/cpd54237/source/specifications/reference/pls39_x39/spex_video_display_format_pls39_x39.html), [X27](https://files.support.epson.com/docid/cpd4/cpd42080/source/specifications/reference/pls27_965h/spex_video_display_format_pls27_965h.html)). Fed 1080p, an XGA panel shows 1024 x 576 with bars: the top and bottom of the panel cannot be mapped and the picture is scaled twice. At its own 1024 x 768 it is scaled once, by the box, and every panel pixel can be mapped.

**Automatic** (`screenmode.automatic`), the default on every box, existing ones at their next update:

1. The screen's own mode (the first in its list), if it is at most 1920 x 1200 and drawn at about 60 Hz (59.94, 59.95, 60.00: within 0.2 Hz).
2. Else, among the modes at most 1920 x 1200 at about 60 Hz (at about 50 only if there is none at 60): those of the same shape as the screen's own mode, the largest; else 1920 x 1080; else the largest. Of one size, the rate the screen lists first (the kernel lists 60.00 before 59.94).
3. Else nothing is passed: the screen's own mode, as before.

So an XGA projector gets 1024 x 768 at 60, a 1080p one 1080p60, a WUXGA one 1920 x 1200 at 59.95, the 1440p monitor 1080p60, a 4K TV 1080p60. Judgements made here, the owner's to change: a 16:10 screen without 1920 x 1200 keeps its shape at 1680 x 1050 before 1920 x 1080; between 60.00 and 59.94 at one size the screen's own order wins (60.00), although 59.94 is the even beat for a 29.97 clip (mpv's default `video-sync=audio` then repeats one frame about every 33 seconds; `display-resample`, which bends speed by up to 1 percent, is not built and is a device test, S4); and a projector whose EDID names 1080p as its own mode gets 1080p, because the box cannot know the panel's real size.

**How the mode is chosen and passed.** `/sys/class/drm/*/modes` has the sizes only, never the rates, so it cannot say "at 60". The one list that has the rate exactly as mpv compares it is mpv's own: `mpv --no-config --drm-mode=help` prints each connected screen's modes in the kernel's order as `Mode N: name (WxH@R.RRHz)`, from the same connector data mpv will use. mpv 0.40's `--drm-mode` takes `preferred`, `highest`, an index, or `WxH[@R]` ([vo.rst](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/mpv-player/mpv/v0.40.0/DOCS/man/vo.rst)); in [drm_common.c](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/mpv-player/mpv/v0.40.0/video/out/drm_common.c) a `WxH@R` that matches no mode is an error, not a fallback (`mode_match`: `(int)round(R*100) == (int)round(rate*100)`, the rate being `clock * 1000 / htotal / vtotal`), and a disconnected or missing connector is fatal. So the player's service (`pvj-player serve`, the display account, just before it becomes mpv: `screenmode.prepare`) asks for that list, reads what is wanted, and passes one `--drm-mode` that is in the list, R written as mpv prints it (`%.2f`); where an interlaced mode of the same numbers comes first, its index instead. It writes what it chose and why to `/run/pvj/player/screen.json` (0640, the panel reads it, and reads it as data from a less trusted account: every field checked, `screenmode.clean_report`). `hardware.drm_connectors` still says whether a screen is connected at all, so a box with none asks mpv nothing. Only where mpv draws through DRM itself (`--gpu-context=drm`); a `--drm-mode` given by hand (`pvj-player --mpv-arg`) wins.

**What is wanted, and who may read it.** The setting is `screen.mode` in the settings ("auto" or a mode's text), read with defaults everywhere: no schema change. The player may not read `settings.json` (0600, the panel's; it holds the PIN's hash), so the panel writes the wanted mode alone to `/var/lib/pvj/screen-mode.json` (0640, group pvj, in the panel's 2750 folder) at its start, on a change and after an import or a reset. The player takes a missing or damaged file for Automatic. **Not in an export and not imported** (`boxcare.BOX_OWN`, a new list beside `NEVER`; the diagnostics file shows it): the mode is chosen for the screen on this box, and an import would set it with nobody in the room to keep it or let it go back. A file that holds it is imported without it, with a note.

**By hand: Setup > Screen output** (the Owner's; `GET` and `POST /api/screen`, `/api/screen/confirm`, `/api/screen/revert`). The connected screen's own modes (size and rate, interlaced ones left out), "Automatic (recommended)" first with what it chose and why, and the note that 60 Hz suits clips at 30 and 29.97 and 50 Hz suits clips at 25. A change asks in place first (D42, D43): the player restarts and the screen goes dark for a few seconds, then there are 15 seconds to keep it. Then "Keep this mode? Going back in 15 s", counting down from the moment the new player is up (the restart takes a few seconds, and nobody can judge a picture that is not there yet); a player that is not back 45 seconds after the tap goes back too. Nothing is saved until Keep. This is the Network page's pattern (apply, then confirm or revert, and back by itself: `networkCard`, `pvj/netcfg.py`), with the try held in the wanted file as `trial` with the boot's id: a box that restarts during the try never uses it (the player takes a try only in the boot it was made in), and a panel that restarts during it takes it back.

**Who.** The Owner, as the network: a wrong mode blacks out the room's screen, it is set once per screen and not during a show, and the owner's words in D80 keep "system critical options" for the Owner. (The sound output and Effect detail are an Operator's because they are daily work; this is not.) **Remote support (D84):** a session of the Owner's role may read, try and take back a mode; **the keep is refused through the tunnel** (`support.REMOTE_DENY`), because the keep is the proof that someone sees the picture in the room, and a helper sees only what the player draws (a snapshot), never what the projector shows. A mode kept outlives the session; so the keep is the room's, exactly as a network change's confirm is. The rows are in `tests/test_roles.py`.

**Fail safe, never a black screen loop.** The research expected a bad mode to make the player's service restart once a second for ever. Reading mpv's code showed something else: with `--idle` and `--force-window` a VO that fails to open (a mode it cannot set) leaves mpv running with no screen at all (`handle_force_window`'s error path in `player/playloop.c` turns force-window off and goes on), so the service never restarts and the screen stays black. Both are covered:

- Before every start the wanted mode is looked up in the screen's live list; one that is not there (another screen, a hot-plug, a file from another box) gives Automatic, and the page, the player's journal and the panel's journal (once per reason) say so: "1920 x 1080 at 50 Hz is not offered by this screen now, so Automatic is used: 1024 x 768 at 60 Hz".
- After a start with a mode, the panel asks the new player (every two seconds, from autostart's watch) for `current-vo`. A player that has none six seconds on is started once more with no `--drm-mode`, the screen's own mode, by a `skip` of that mode in the wanted file for this boot only, and the page says "... did not start on this screen; the player was started with the screen's own mode". A mode being tried that does so goes back at once. A player with no `--drm-mode` is never restarted for this, so there is no loop.
- **No screen connected**: as before. Nothing is passed, mpv takes the first screen that comes, and the mode is chosen at the next start of the player. Under KMS the kernel reads the EDID and HDMI is off when no display is detected at boot ([config.txt](https://www.raspberrypi.com/documentation/computers/config_txt.html): "HDMI output is disabled when no HDMI display is detected"). **Not built, the owner's later choice:** forcing a mode at boot with `video=HDMI-A-1:1920x1080M@60D` in `cmdline.txt` ([configuration](https://www.raspberrypi.com/documentation/computers/configuration.html); `D` forces the output on with a digital signal, `M` computes CVT timings: [modedb](https://www.kernel.org/doc/html/latest/fb/modedb.html)). It would give a box that boots before its projector a picture without a restart of the player, at the cost of driving a mode the screen never offered (a projector switched on later might not take it), of a root change to the boot files that an update would have to own, and of the EDID's own list no longer being what the box sees.

**What else is in screen pixels** (grepped for osd-width, osd-dimensions, 2560, 1440, 1920, 1080 and MAX_PIXELS). Everything reads the player's size when it acts, so it follows the mode with nothing stored: the crossfade's still and MAX_PIXELS (`pvj/transitions.py`, not touched: #138 changes it), a shader's carrier (`shaders.py`), the overlay and logo (converted to the screen's size when put on, and again on a new player), the QR codes and PIN screen (scaled from the height), snapshots (the player's own window), and a video wall's crop (a share of the picture, D31). Boxes in step do not care about each other's modes, but a wall of boxes looks best with the same mode on every screen (an even beat on each); nothing enforces that. **The mapping (D28, D58)** already stored the size it was made at (`mapper.screen`) and moved its corners by each axis on its own (`mapper.scaled`), and a new player gets it again with the new size (autostart's watch). What was missing is the warning: from 16:9 to 4:3 that squeezes every shape, so the mapping page says so ("This mapping was made on a 2560 x 1440 screen, another shape than this one ...") until the mapping is next changed, which stores the new size (`state()["reshaped"]`). Effect detail (D58) is in lines of the picture, not the screen. Nothing new is stored for it: an older release reads the same `mapper.screen`.

**The test pattern** (the box's own colour bars, `POST /api/testpattern`) is now drawn at the player's size, with a grid of squares a twelfth of the height, a red border one pixel wide at the very edge, and the mode in words over it ("Screen output: 1024 x 768 at 60 Hz (Automatic)", drawn again every two seconds while it is on, never over a code someone asked to have on the display). It is on the Screen output page as well as on Mapping. A pattern an older panel put up is still recognised as one.

**Status.** `GET /api/status` has `screen`: `asked` (the setting, or the mode being tried), `used` (the text passed, or null for the screen's own), and `why` they differ. `GET /api/screen` has the whole list, what Automatic chose and why, what mpv says it draws at now (`display-width`, `display-height`, `display-fps`) and the try.

**Rollback.** The release before (master at 33e7eb4) loads a settings file with `screen`, keeps it through its own save, and maps as it did; tested with that release's own code (`tests/test_screenmode.py`, `RollbackTest`; CI fetches the commit). An older release ignores the wanted file and plays at the screen's own mode, as before D90.

**Cost and what is not known.** A start of the player costs one more short mpv run (the list: a fraction of a second, measured nowhere yet). A mode change interrupts what plays: the player restarts, and autostart then plays what it would play after any restart. Nothing follows a hot-plug while the player runs (mpv keeps its mode until it restarts). The shape warning goes with the next change of the mapping, whether or not anyone looked. The lists in the tests were written from the kernel's usual order and the Epson spec sheets, not taken from a real screen. **Nothing of this has run on a Pi or in front of a screen or a projector**: no mode was set by this code on any display. The rows are S1 to S14 in `tools/DEVICE-TESTING.md`: the list per screen, dropped frames and the shader, scaling, mapping and crossfade times at 1080p60 and 1024 x 768 at 60, judder with display-resample on and off, boot with the screen off and hot-plug, and the X27 or X39's picture at 1024 x 768 against 1080p. Reads devices and takes a file from another account, so it needs the independent review before it is merged.

**After the independent security review of #139 (2026-10-16, the default model; one Medium, five Low, each fixed with a test seen to fail without it).** M1: the panel read the player's report with a plain open, so the player's account could put a pipe there and every status poll would hang; it is now opened without following a link and without waiting (O_NOFOLLOW, O_NONBLOCK), and read only if it is a regular file, at most 64 KB (`read_json`, and `Screen.report` looks at the name itself first). L1: Keep and the timer's going back are one step each under one lock, so the page can no longer say Kept while the player goes back. L2: the look at a new player's screen is claimed under that lock, so two threads never both restart it; testing it showed that the wanted file's temp name was one per process, so two threads' writes collided: each write now has a name of its own (`tempfile.mkstemp`). L3: only mpv's own answer (`property unavailable`, or an empty name) counts as no screen; a failed word with the player is "not known yet" and is asked again two seconds later, so a hiccup no longer costs the box its mode for the boot. L4: a `screen` setting of the wrong kind is Automatic, not a 500 on every status. L5: a board with two cards (a Pi 5) lists screens of each; the HDMI screen is chosen (else the first listed) and passed with its card as `--drm-connector=CARD.NAME` beside `--drm-mode`, so the mode is always set on the screen it was read from; a `--drm-device` or `--drm-connector` given by hand wins. Not run on a Pi 5 or any two-card board (S1).

## D91. The screen's card comes from the kernel: `--drm-device=/dev/dri/cardN` and a bare `--drm-connector=NAME`, never mpv's "N.NAME" (2026-10-16)

Supersedes the part of D90's last paragraph (the review's L5) that passed `--drm-connector=CARD.NAME`.

**What happened.** #139 was merged (master 3ad61a7) and put on the test Pi 4. Automatic chose 1920 x 1080 at 60 for the 1440p monitor and the player was started with `--drm-connector=0.HDMI-A-1 --drm-mode=1920x1080@60.00`. The panel then saw no screen ("the player was given 1920 x 1080 at 60 Hz and has no screen"), started the player once more with no mode, and the monitor came up at its own 2560 x 1440. So the safety net of D90 held and the room was never dark, but Automatic did nothing on the one box that matters.

**The cause, as mpv's own source states it** ([drm_common.c of 0.40](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/mpv-player/mpv/v0.40.0/video/out/drm_common.c); 0.37 is the same). `--drm-connector=<name>` ([vo.rst](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/mpv-player/mpv/v0.40.0/DOCS/man/vo.rst)) is compared whole with each connector's own name (`get_connector_by_name`: `strcmp` against "HDMI-A-1", made from the type and its number); nothing reads a card number out of it, so "0.HDMI-A-1" matches no connector, `setup_connector` fails ("No connector with name ... found") and the player has no video output. The card is chosen in `get_primary_device_path`: by `--drm-device=<path>` if given, else the first device in libdrm's list (`drmGetDevices2`) that has a primary node, supports KMS and has a connected output. The "N." that `--drm-mode=help` prints ("Available modes for drm-connector=0.HDMI-A-1") is only that device's place in libdrm's list, not /dev/dri/cardN: on the Pi it printed 0 for the HDMI of card1 (card0 is v3d, which renders and has no outputs). The CI lists had been written with that number and the tests checked only that mpv took the option text, which mpv does for any string.

**Now.** The card is the kernel's: `/sys/class/drm/cardN-NAME` is output NAME of `/dev/dri/cardN` (`hardware.drm_connectors` gives `card`). The screen is chosen from mpv's list as before (an HDMI one first), its card is the one card whose connected output has that name, and the player gets `--drm-device=/dev/dri/cardN --drm-connector=NAME --drm-mode=WxH@R`. With a device given, mpv uses it as is. On the test Pi that is `--drm-device=/dev/dri/card1 --drm-connector=HDMI-A-1 --drm-mode=1920x1080@60.00`; a PC with one card gets card0; a board with HDMI on card1 and composite on card2 gets card1 and its HDMI. If two cards have a connected output of the same name, or the kernel's list names no card, only `--drm-mode` is passed and mpv picks the card itself, as D90 did before L5. The report checks both texts (`/dev/dri/cardN` only, a connector name only). Tests: `CardTest` in `tests/test_screenmode.py` (the Pi 4 layout, a one-card PC, a two-card board, one name on two cards; each seen to fail with the old text). **Not run on the Pi yet**; S1 in tools/DEVICE-TESTING.md says what to look at.

Cost: one more argument for mpv, and a box whose display card the kernel renumbers between the player's start and mpv's open would fail once and fall back by D90's net.

## D92. A skip bars the arguments that gave no screen, not the mode text alone (2026-10-16)

Adds to D90's net and D91; neither is changed.

**What happened.** On the test Pi the first start with a mode had `--drm-connector=0.HDMI-A-1`, gave no screen, and the panel wrote `skip: {"mode": "1920x1080@60.00", "boot": ...}`. After #140 was deployed the player would have passed `--drm-device=/dev/dri/card1 --drm-connector=HDMI-A-1` with the same mode text in the same boot, and `decide` dropped it for that skip: the corrected arguments were never tried until the Pi was restarted.

**Now.** The skip is `{"mode", "boot", "args"}`, `args` the list the player was given for the screen (`--drm-device`, `--drm-connector`, `--drm-mode`, those there were; `screen_args`), as the panel read it back from the report. `decide` bars the mode only when this boot's skip has exactly the arguments it would pass now. Other arguments (another card, another connector, a mode passed alone) are tried; the same ones stay barred for the boot, so there is still no loop. The player emits a device or connector argument only in the form the panel's report reader keeps, so the skip the panel writes always matches what the player would pass again.

**A skip of the old shape** (`mode` and `boot` only, written by #139 or #140) **is ignored**: `clean_wanted` drops it. It does not say which device and connector earned it, and the release that wrote the first ones is the one that passed a wrong connector, so honouring it would repeat this fault after the update. Ignoring it costs at most one more try of the same arguments in that boot (six seconds without a picture, then the screen's own mode), which writes a skip of the new shape. A skip whose arguments are not of the known forms is dropped the same way, so `--drm-connector=0.HDMI-A-1` can never be one.

**Every failed set is kept for the boot (the review of #141, Low).** With one skip, two sets that alternate between starts (a second output that flaps and makes the card ambiguous, or an EDID read two ways) would each overwrite the other, and each round would cost about six seconds without a picture. So `skip` is a list: each set that gave no screen is added (`with_skip`), at most MAX_SKIPS (8, the newest kept), and entries of another boot are dropped when one is added or the panel starts. A single skip object of the first D92 shape is read as a list of one; the shape before D92 (no `args`) is still ignored, as above. More than eight sets alternating in one boot would again cost one dark start each per round; none is known.

Tests (`tests/test_screenmode.py`): a skip with other arguments does not bar the new ones, in `decide` and through the panel with a new release in the same boot; the same arguments stay barred and the player is not restarted again; an old-shape skip is tried once more and then barred; a skip's arguments are checked; two sets that alternate cost one dark start each and then stay barred; the list stays at eight; a new boot clears it. Comparing by mode text again fails two of them, and keeping one skip again fails three. **Not run on the Pi.**

## D93. A shader is drawn at the most lines it held on a Pi 4 at the box's output: Automatic picture detail, from the run at 1920 x 1080 (2026-10-16)

Pull request #142. The owner asked for "improving the shader rendering and overall performance", then "improving shader variety and detail", and for outputs "the most economic output for performance and clarity and detail" (2026-10-15, D90).

**The measurement.** Since D90 and D91 a Pi 4 drives a 1440p monitor at 1920 x 1080 at 60 Hz. All 47 bundled shaders were played again on the test Pi 4 at that output (master 0648db6, 20 seconds at 540 and at 720 lines). At 540 lines all 47 hold (fewer than 0.5 dropped a second; in fact none dropped any); at 720 lines 41 hold, and nxlx-drift (10.07 a second), nxlx-nebula (9.67), isf-ridgelines (7.32), isf-simplex-noise (6.43), nxlx-aurora (2.59) and nxlx-lantern (1.05) do not. At 2560 x 1440 at 75 Hz (D51) drift, nebula and ridgelines dropped frames at 540 too. The shaders' own passes are the same in both runs; the scale to the screen is cheaper at 1080p and a 60 Hz screen gives each of the carrier's 30 pictures 33.3 ms.

**Decided.**
1. `PI4` in `pvj/shaderlive.py` is the run at 1920 x 1080, and says so (`PI4_OUTPUT`). Its first number is **the sum of all of a frame's passes** (that run kept the shader's own pass only where frames dropped), and `measured` names it `all_passes_ms`. The run at 2560 x 1440 stays, unchanged, as `PI4_1440` with the shader's own pass as `pass_ms`, and its numbers stay in `pvj/SHADERS.md` as the first run. `table_for(screen)` takes PI4 for an output of at most 1920 x 1080 pixels and PI4_1440 above it, so a box set to 1440p by hand keeps the old classes and numbers. The class of a shader (`weight` in the library) follows the table for the box's output: at 1080p none is heavy. **The computed sets Ambient and Show judge each shader at the height it will be drawn at** (`too_heavy`, after the review of #142): on a Pi 4 a shader is left out where it did not hold at that height in the table for the output (Automatic: its own height; a kept or chosen height: that one). So under Automatic at 1080p drift and nebula are in Ambient at 540, and with 720 lines kept they, aurora and lantern are out. A shader nobody measured, and every board but a Pi 4, go by the class as before.
2. **Automatic** is a value of Picture detail on a Pi 4 (`AUTO_BOARDS`), the way Effect detail's Automatic chooses per filter (D58): each shader at 720 lines where it held at 720 in the table for the output, else at 540; an upload (nobody measured it) at 540 (`AUTO_OTHER`). At 1080p: 720 for 41, 540 for the six above. At 1440p: 720 for the light ones, 540 for the rest. The engine draws each shader at `height_for(sid)`, a heavy mark is made at and compared with that shader's own height and records the output whose table counted (`output`; a mark made for another output does not count, one from before D93 has none and counts as it did), and `render.auto` and each row's `lines` say what it does.
3. **The default.** Automatic is what a Pi 4 has when no height was saved. The 540 default was not the owner's: an agent set it on 2026-10-04 after the first Pi run (commit 931663b), before D51, which kept it. The ground for Automatic is the owner's own words: he asked for "improving the shader rendering and overall performance" and later "improving shader variety and detail", and on 2026-10-15, asked how to proceed on outputs: "I want you to proceed with whatever is going to provide the most economic output for performance and clarity and detail in the output." (D90). **Reading those words as "Automatic is the default for shaders" is the coordinator's reading, not a choice he confirmed**; it is on HANDOFF's list of what he may want to change. D58 (his words about Effect detail over clips, "whichever performs better overall") is background only.
4. **A choice by hand wins.** `{"action": "config", "height": 540}` (or 360, 720) draws every shader at it and is remembered as chosen; `"auto"` goes back. A Pi 5, x86 and the other boards are not offered Automatic (400): nothing was measured on them.
5. **A height an earlier release saved is kept.** Every save of the section by an earlier release wrote `height`, whether anyone chose it or not, so a saved number cannot be told from an old default (the test box has the 720 the first version wrote). Taking it as Automatic would override a choice the owner may have made by hand; so it stays, `config.height_kept` says so, and the page offers **Use Automatic** in one tap (beside the load, and on Room's ambience card).
6. **Settings and rollback, as D71 did with `mix.style`.** `height` stays a number every release reads; under Automatic it is 540, so an earlier release draws every shader at 540, its old default. Beside it, `height_auto`: true for Automatic, false for a height chosen by hand, absent where an earlier release saved the height. An earlier release ignores the key and drops it at its next save of the section; back on this one the box then has 540 kept, and is offered Automatic. An import takes `height_auto` (a boolean). No schema change. The rollback test runs master's code (0648db6) on a file written here.

**Cost / rules out.** A box set up before this one does not get Automatic until someone presses Use Automatic once. The class of a bundled shader in the library now depends on the screen size, so the same box shows different words at 1080p and at 1440p. Some shaders that hold have little room: at 540 drift and nebula held with the GPU 96 and 97 percent busy, at 720 tide (90) and bloom (87); an effect or the mapping on top may tip them, and the guard is what catches that (D58 stepped two effects down for room by the owner's choice; that was not done here, nobody has asked him). Whether 720 lines look better than 540 on the projector is not known (`tools/DEVICE-TESTING.md`, "Automatic picture detail"). One run of 20 seconds per height on one board and one screen; a class near the line can change with another minute. A 1024 x 768 output is taken by the 1080p table without having been measured. The table is chosen when a shader is shown: a change of screen mode does not redraw the shader on screen. With 720 lines kept, a Pi 4's computed Ambient is four shaders smaller at 1080p (21) and Show at 1440p loses nxlx-beam and nxlx-scope: they drop frames there. Under Automatic a lower picture detail is one number for every shader; a choice per shader would need a new setting, so the load line says that instead (after the review). While a new generator comes under an effect, the effect's guess of its size is the box's one number for a few seconds (`effects.py`).

## D94. The render bridge: the box cues a render node over OSC and shows its NDI picture, and goes back to what played when the picture goes (2026-10-16)

The owner's words (2026-10-16), after the scope review that put the practice in three tiers (Control: Mastercontrol, always on; Playback: the Pi boxes; Render: an optional laptop or GPU box): "Yes, the three-tier picture fits. Build the bridge first: Mastercontrol cues a render node over OSC and plays its picture back over NDI. I will say which render node to start with." His render tools: VDMX on a MacBook Air (personal shows), MadMapper, Max/Jitter/Vsynth, Resolume for contract work, TouchDesigner possible. The review's rule stands over all of it: nothing the room depends on lives only on a render node.

**Design note (written before the code, so the owner can read it first; the "As built" part below replaces it where they differ).**

- **What is built now is only what every tool shares.** No tool's OSC addresses are written anywhere in the code or the docs: a cue is an address and arguments the person types. A tool's profile (address templates such as "go to scene N") has a documented place and is empty until the owner names the first tool.
- **Render nodes**, a list the Owner keeps under Setup (at most 8): a name, a host (an IPv4 address or a `.local` name), an OSC port (1024 to 65535), UDP, and the NDI source the node sends, chosen from the NDI input's list the way an NDI pad's source is (D62): by the id of a source that is allowed already or one on the network now, which this makes allowed. Stored under a new settings key `render` (`{"nodes": [...]}`), read with defaults, no schema change.
- **Where the box may send**: the rule of an OSC state target (D82), so one rule and one function: a plain private or link-local IPv4 address on a subnet of one of the box's own real ports at the moment of sending, never its network or broadcast address, never the box itself, never loopback or multicast, never the support tunnel's network (by interface name and by the session's own network, D88), and a port from 1024. A `.local` name is checked for its form when saved and resolved when a cue is sent (with a deadline, at most one lookup at a time per node); the address it resolves to is held to the same rule. The socket's time to live is 1. No packet from anywhere decides where the box sends.
- **A cue** is one OSC message (an address and up to 8 typed arguments: int, float, string, bool) to one node, optionally followed by "show this node's NDI picture". It is sent by the panel (`POST /api/render/cue`, the Operator's) and is a fourth kind of pad (`{"label", "file": "", "render": {...}}`, beside the clip, the shader and the NDI source; an older release sees an empty pad). Because Room scenes, the schedule (through a scene) and the controllers (MIDI, OSC, TouchOSC, DMX) already press pads, a render cue is attachable everywhere a pad is, through the one existing road (`/api/play {"pad"}`), with no new action names in `pvj/actions.py`.
- **Reuse**: the encoder, the address checks, the networks and the buckets of `pvj/oscfeedback.py` (D82); the NDI input, its allowed list and `play_ndi` (D62, D85 to D87) for the picture. No new listening port: the sending socket is opened for one datagram and closed; it is never read.
- **Caps**: a datagram is at most 1200 bytes; at most 20 cues in any second to one node and 60 to all nodes together; over the cap a cue is refused in words (429), never queued.
- **Who** (D80, rows in `tests/test_roles.py`): the list of nodes is the Owner's (`POST /api/render`); seeing the nodes and their health is the Operator's (`GET /api/render`, without host and port below the Owner, as projectors and streams); firing a cue by hand is the Operator's; putting a cue on a pad is the Operator's, as every pad is (D80). A Guest is refused a pad or a scene that holds a render cue, as he is refused an NDI pad and a stream (it reaches another machine on the network). The controllers, a Room scene and the schedule reach a cue only through a pad, which they could already press; they gain no route.
- **Remote support (D84)**: treated as the OSC state targets are, the nearest setting (the box sending to a device in the room): a session of the Owner's role may change the list and fire cues, with every address held to the box's own networks, the tunnel's never; a settings file brought by a helper keeps the box's own list when it names a host that is not on one of those networks now. Below the Owner's role: nothing new, as always (a pad press stays what it was).
- **Health**, per node: whether its NDI source is on the network now, when a cue was last sent and any error. A state chip on the page. OSC over UDP has no reply: the page says "sent", never "received", until a profile adds a reply.
- **Fallback**: when a node's picture is on the screen and goes (the helper says the connection dropped, the source is no longer announced, or the picture ended by itself), the box goes back to what played before the cue (a clip or a playlist from the media folder, a shader, Vibes, another NDI source or a saved stream) within a bounded time (5 seconds), says it once in the journal, and the page says why. If nothing played before, the box stops as Stop does. Anything chosen after the cue (a tap, a scene, a Stop) is a newer wish and stands: no fallback over it. This is the HANDOFF's open item "a source that drops leaves the player idle", closed for this path only.
- **Profiles (placeholder)**: `pvj/render.py` documents the shape (a tool id, a default port, named cues each with an address template and typed arguments) and holds none. Facts needed from the owner or the tool's own documentation for the first one are listed in `pvj/RENDERNODES.md`.

**The first render node, the owner's words of 2026-10-16:** "Try to start with Max and Jitter as the first render node." Max has no OSC address space of its own, so the first profile is this project's own scheme, `max`, under `/nxlx/`: `/nxlx/scene <int>`, `/nxlx/param <name> <float>`, `/nxlx/color <name> <r> <g> <b>`, `/nxlx/go`, `/nxlx/hello`, default port 7400. Add, never rename. A companion patch, `tools/max/nxlx-render-node.maxpat`, receives exactly it with `[udpreceive 7400]` and `[route]`, drives a placeholder Jitter look (`jit.world`, `jit.gl.gridshape`) and sends the picture with `jit.ndi.send~` from 2bbb's jit.ndi (github.com/2bbb/jit.ndi), a third-party package the owner installs in Max 9 himself; it is not bundled. **The patch is untested in Max**: it is checked as JSON, and its addresses against the profile, by the tests. `/nxlx/hello` is defined for a patch that answers; the box reads no answer, since reading one would need a listening port (`reply: False`). Still no other tool's addresses anywhere.

**Packages, the owner's word of 2026-10-16** ("pull packages and libraries as needed, and reverse engineer when not possible"): none was needed. The bridge is the standard library (a UDP socket, `struct`, `getaddrinfo`), and the OSC encoder is the one of D82. jit.ndi stays the owner's to install.

**As built (2026-10-16, #143), where it differs from the note above or adds to it.**

- `pvj/render.py`: the forms (`host_form`, `node_form`, `cue_form`, `arg_form`), the encoder (`i`, `f`, `s`, and `T`/`F` with no data), `Bridge` (the sender, per-node health, the record of a shown picture), `PROFILES` and `profile_cue`. Api: `get_render`, `set_render`, `render_cue`, `render_fire`, `pad_render`, `_render_before`, `_render_back`, `render_tick` (called beside `ndi_tick` once a second, in its own `try`). `Transitions.generation()` gives the wishes' count, which tells a picture that ended by itself from one that something newer took. `Player.playlist_files()` reads mpv's list for the way back.
- **A cue that shows a picture is all or nothing up front**: the picture is checked (`_ndi_pad_check`) before the OSC leaves; if it fails afterwards (the connection), the answer says the cue was sent and the picture was not.
- **What counts as gone** adds two to the note: the NDI helper not answering, and a picture that stood still longer than the node's still time (`still`, 10 seconds by default, 0 for never, for a tool that sends only on change). Seen gone for `GONE_SECONDS` (3), the box goes back; the tick is once a second.
- **What is put back**: clips from the media folder only (a USB drive's clip is not), from the one that played, looping if it looped; a shader with its preset; Vibes with its set; another NDI source; a saved stream; else Stop. A capture input and the test pattern are not put back (Stop). The position inside a clip is not kept.
- **Remote support**: `POST /api/render` is in `support.REMOTE_OWNER_OPENED` and asks `_remote_owner` itself; its change is a major line in the session's log. A settings file through the tunnel: `boxcare._remote_parts` keeps the box's own nodes if the file adds an IPv4 host that is not on the box's networks now.
- **The diagnostics file** holds the nodes without their hosts. An export holds them whole (the Owner's, and a session of his role reads them anyway).
- **Not done**: a reply from a tool; a per-node choice of where to fall back to; a cue list that spans boxes and nodes; the position in a clip; per-tool profiles other than `max`.
- Tests: `tests/test_render.py` (forms, the exact bytes on a loopback socket, where the box may send, the caps, lookups, roles and both walls a Guest meets, a cue from every identity, the way back with the fake NDI library behind the real helper for each way of going, a later choice standing, export and a helper's file, the older release at 1ed58bc reading the file), new rows in `tests/test_roles.py`, the opened route in `tests/test_support_reach.py`, bad values in `tests/test_boxcare.py`. Each part was broken on purpose and a test failed. Nothing ran with a real render node, a real Max or on a Pi: rows RN1 to RN8 in `tools/DEVICE-TESTING.md`.

## D95. A renderer of the box's own, `pvj-render`: a native helper beside the Python panel, built in phases; phase 1 takes the one mpv's place behind the same socket (2026-10-16)

(D94 is taken by the render bridge, #143.)

**The owner's decision, in his words** (2026-10-16). Asked whether a native helper beside the Python panel is allowed: "Yes, a native helper". And: "Determine the best course of action for the Pi renderer and proceed. I do like the idea of rebuilding it now that we have a more thoughtout scope. Let's not forget that the Pi version of Mastercontrol can be it's own All-in-one VJ rig." Target: a Pi 4 (maybe a Pi 3) first, then bigger Linux, Mac and PC boxes. **Licence, his working choice:** the helper is GPL-3.0-or-later in a folder of its own (`render/`), because it links libmpv, which is GPLv2 or later as Debian builds it; everything else stays Apache-2.0. The panel only starts the helper and talks to it over a socket, as it does with mpv today: two programs, never linked together.

**Why a renderer of our own, and why now.** One mpv draws one picture with one chain of user shaders. Everything a VJ rig needs beyond that has been worked around inside it: a crossfade is a still of the screen laid over the next clip as an overlay (D71, D89), a generator is a shader over a blank carrier (D73), an effect is a filter in the same chain (D74), the mapping is the last shader (D28, D58), and a value of a generator changes by writing a new shader text that mpv compiles again. Two clips at once, a real crossfade, feedback, per-frame uniforms and sound-driven values are not reachable that way. A program that owns the screen and uses libmpv only as a decoder into textures reaches all of them, and it is the ground the all-in-one rig stands on.

**The evidence** (a spike on the test Pi 4, 2026-10-16, run by the coordinator; the source and logs stayed outside the repository). Two 1080p30 H.264 clips through two libmpv render contexts into GL ES textures, crossfaded with a feedback pass, on EGL, GBM and KMS with page flips paced by vblank:

- **Software decode, 960 x 540 inside:** 10 minutes with 9 missed vblanks after a 3 second warm-up, flip interval p99 16.676 ms, GPU 46 percent busy, the CPU about 80 percent on each core.
- **1280 x 720 inside:** 3 missed vblanks in 2 minutes. **1920 x 1080 inside fails:** compositing two 1080 textures alone took 10.4 ms.
- **v4l2m2m hardware decode fails at every size:** importing its DRM PRIME frames cost 15 to 19 ms of GPU a frame. **60 fps clips fail.**
- Lessons kept: draw the next frame while the last flip is still pending; convert at most one clip per vblank; do not hand the DRM display to mpv (it renders into our context only); give a clip 3 seconds before judging it.

So on a Pi 4 the layered work is drawn at 960 x 540 and scaled once, at the end, to the output. One clip alone at 720 or at the output's size may hold; that is a device test, not a claim.

**The phases.** Only phase 1 is built now; the others are written down so that phase 1 does not close a door.

1. **A drop-in for the one mpv** (this pull request). One video layer, the mapping, the overlays and on-screen text, the final scale, behind the same JSON socket. A setting chooses it; classic mpv stays the default and the fallback.
2. **Two video layers and real crossfades**: a second libmpv context, the spike's pipelined loop with one conversion per vblank, the crossfade drawn by the renderer instead of D71's still.
3. **The shader engine as real passes**: ISF inputs as uniforms set every frame (no recompile when a value moves), persistent and feedback buffers, an effects chain over any layer, generators as layers.
4. **Sound values** from a separate sound-analysis service (level, beat, bands published to the renderer as uniforms), soak tests, a Pi 5, and the desktop path (a GLFW or SDL window on Linux and macOS for the bigger boxes).

**What phase 1 is.** `render/pvj-render`, C11, a small Makefile, GPL-3.0-or-later (SPDX header in every file, `render/LICENSE`, `LICENSES/GPL-3.0-or-later.txt`, THIRD_PARTY_LICENSES.md). It links libmpv (the render API), EGL, OpenGL ES 3, GBM and libdrm, and nothing else. **It needs OpenGL ES 3.0**: a Pi 4 (V3D) has it; a Pi 3 (VC4) has OpenGL ES 2 only, so there pvj-render does not start and the box falls back to classic by itself (a GLES 2 path is a later choice, if the Pi 3 matters).

**Libraries** (the owner, 2026-10-16: "pull packages and libraries as needed", within licences that fit, pinned, from official sources, never vendored binaries). Phase 1 needs none beyond the four above, all from the distribution's own packages (Debian 13 on the Pi: mpv 0.40.0's libmpv, Mesa, libdrm), named with their licences and the versions CI builds against in THIRD_PARTY_LICENSES.md. Nothing is vendored, and no code of mpv, Mesa or kmscube is copied: mpv's connector and mode rules and its user-shader format were read in its source and are followed as behaviour.

- **It owns the screen on a box without a desktop:** EGL on GBM on KMS, page flips paced by vblank, the next frame drawn while the flip is pending. The card, connector and mode are not chosen again: the player's service passes exactly the arguments `screenmode.prepare` chose for mpv (`--drm-device=/dev/dri/cardN --drm-connector=NAME --drm-mode=WxH@R` or an index; D90 to D92), and pvj-render reads them as mpv 0.40 does (a bare connector name compared whole; the rate compared as `round(R * 100)` against the mode's own `clock * 1000 / htotal / vtotal`, doubled for an interlaced mode; an index into the connector's list). Without them it takes the first connected HDMI screen at its own first mode. A mode that matches nothing is an error and the program ends, so the fallback below takes over.
- **The internal size is a setting** (`renderer.detail`): `auto` (a Pi 3 or 4: 540 lines; any other box: the output's size), 540, 720 or `output`. The width follows the output's shape (1920 x 1200 at 540 lines is 864 x 540). The video layer is drawn at that size and the final pass scales it to the output.
- **A headless mode** (`--headless=WxH`, EGL surfaceless, paced by a clock) is the same program for the tests and for CI. It is not a way to run a show.

**Changed from the brief, and why.** The brief asked phase 1 to draw brightness and opacity itself, to draw the overlays through a command like `overlay-add`, and to port the mapping's geometry. Reading the code that drives mpv today changed three things:

1. **The video layer's own controls stay libmpv's in phase 1.** Brightness (the panel's opacity is mpv's `brightness`), zoom, pan, rotation, `keepaspect`, flips, a video wall's crop, and the effects and generators (user shaders on the MAIN stage) are passed to libmpv, which draws them into the layer's texture exactly as classic mpv does. Why: the picture stays the same as classic down to the colour maths, there is no second implementation of each control to keep equal, and the spike showed the cost is in decoding and in the final composite, not here. Phase 2 draws opacity in the renderer's own compositor, where it becomes real opacity over another layer.
2. **Overlays and on-screen text go to a second libmpv instance that plays nothing** and draws only its on-screen layer, at the output's size, which is laid over the final picture in screen space (only while something is on it). The PIN screen is not only an overlay: it writes its text with mpv's `show-text` (`pvj/pinscreen.py`), as the test pattern's words do (`screenmode.Screen.tick`). A text renderer of our own would be a second, poorer one; this way `overlay-add`, `overlay-remove` and `show-text` keep mpv's exact meaning (the BGRA file, its offset and stride, a drawn size with `dw` and `dh`, the durations), and the QR codes, the logo, the masks and the PIN screen work unchanged. Cost: a second, idle mpv core (a few megabytes) and one blended full-screen pass while text or an overlay is shown.
3. **The mapping is the same generated shader, run by the renderer at the output's pixels.** `mapper.warp_shader` (the show table) and `mapper.shader` (editing, with the outlines) are mpv user shaders on the OUTPUT stage. pvj-render reads such a file itself (the `//!HOOK OUTPUT` block, `//!BIND`, and `//!TEXTURE` blocks with SIZE, FORMAT, FILTER, BORDER and their hex data), gives it `HOOKED_tex`, `HOOKED_pos` and `HOOKED_size` (the output's size, as mpv's OUTPUT stage has it), and draws it as the final pass that scales the layer to the screen. So the stored mapping (D28, D58), its validation and its generation are untouched, and mapping and scaling cost one pass. A shader file with any other stage (an effect, a generator) is handed to libmpv in the same list order. A file of more than 16 MiB, a texture larger than 4096 x 4096 or with the wrong amount of data, or a format other than rgba16f, rgba32f or rgba8 is refused with mpv's "invalid parameter".

**The socket: the same JSON IPC, a subset.** pvj-render listens where mpv listened (the path classic mpv's `--input-ipc-server` named, `/run/pvj/player/player.sock`), speaks mpv's protocol (one JSON object a line, `{"command": [...], "request_id": n}`, answered with `{"request_id": n, "error": "success", "data": ...}`), and answers anything outside the subset with mpv's own words: "invalid parameter" for a command, "property not found" for a property it does not let through. So `pvj/player.py` and everything that uses its socket are unchanged; the switch is the program the service starts.

| Command | Phase 1 | Where it goes |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `loadfile URL [replace\|append [index [options]]]` | yes | video layer; the options are only the pipe and NDI keys the player sets (below), anything else is refused |
| `stop`, `seek T relative\|absolute`, `playlist-next\|prev [force]`, `playlist-shuffle` | yes | video layer |
| `quit` | yes | ends pvj-render; the service starts it again |
| `get_property NAME` | yes | the renderer's own answer for the names below, else the video layer's |
| `set_property NAME VALUE` | only the names below | as below |
| `add volume D` | yes (volume only) | video layer |
| `vf add\|remove` | only `@pvjfliph:hflip`, `@pvjflipv:vflip` and those labels | video layer |
| `screenshot-to-file PATH video\|window` | yes | video layer (phase 1: "window" is the video layer at the internal size, without the mapping and the text) |
| `overlay-add ...`, `overlay-remove ID`, `show-text TEXT [MS]` | yes | the text layer, in output pixels |
| `pvj-capture PATH` | new | the final picture as it goes to the screen, as a PPM file (tests; later the panel's snapshot) |
| anything else (`run`, `subprocess`, `load-script`, `script-message`, `keypress`, ...) | no | "invalid parameter" |

| Property | Answered by |
| --- | --- |
| `pid` | pvj-render (its own pid) |
| `current-vo` | pvj-render: "pvj-render" once the screen is open (D90's look for a screen works unchanged) |
| `osd-width`, `osd-height` | the output's size |
| `osd-dimensions` | the output's size, with the video layer's margins scaled to it |
| `display-width`, `display-height`, `display-fps` | the mode on the screen |
| `glsl-shaders` (get and set) | the list as set: OUTPUT-stage files run by pvj-render, the others by the video layer |
| `pvj-render` | the renderer's state: internal and output size, frames, missed vblanks, the mapping's state |
| read only, from the video layer | `path`, `idle-active`, `time-pos`, `duration`, `width`, `height`, `video-params`, `video-out-params`, `track-list/count`, `seeking`, `playlist-pos`, `playlist-count`, `frame-drop-count`, `decoder-frame-drop-count`, `hwdec-current`, `container-fps`, `estimated-vf-fps`, `video-codec`, `vo-passes`, and any other name (a read changes nothing) |
| set, on the video layer | `pause`, `speed`, `volume`, `mute`, `loop-file`, `loop-playlist`, `keep-open`, `image-display-duration`, `audio-device`, `brightness`, `video-zoom`, `video-pan-x`, `video-pan-y`, `video-rotate`, `video-crop`, `keepaspect`, `fbo-format`, the screenshot settings (`screenshot-format`, `-jpeg-quality`, `-png-compression`, `-png-filter`, `-high-bit-depth`), and the live inputs' keys (`demuxer`, `demuxer-rawvideo-w`, `-h`, `-mp-format`, `-fps`, `cache`, `cache-pause`, `initial-audio-sync`, `demuxer-readahead-secs`, `demuxer-max-bytes`, `vd`, `ad`, `sid`, `ordered-chapters`, `cover-art-auto`, `embeddedfonts`, `audio-delay`) |

Phase 1 is judged on one clip: load, stop, pause, seek, loop, volume, and what the status, the panel, the screen's mode and the mapping read. The live inputs, streams, the crossfade's still, effects and generators go through the same commands and are expected to work, but no test here runs them on pvj-render, and "window" stills lack the mapping and the text (a crossfade's still over a mapping would show unmapped for its length: phase 2 replaces that still anyway).

**The rules the socket keeps** (it reads control input on the box that owns the screen). The socket is opened 0660 to group pvj, as mpv's is (the same detached opener); pvj-render runs as the player's account in the player's unit, never as root. A line is at most 64 KiB, a JSON value at most 16 deep, at most 8 clients at once; a client that sends a longer line is dropped, and bad JSON is answered with an error. The command list and the settable names are fixed lists in the code (above): there is no way through the socket to run a program, load a script, send a script a message or set an option the box does not use. `loadfile`'s fourth argument is split and every key checked. `pvj-capture` writes only to an absolute path, never through a link (O_NOFOLLOW), 0640. The libmpv instances get the same safety options as classic (`load-unsafe-playlists=no`, `access-references=no`, `sub-auto=no`, `audio-file-auto=no`, `ytdl=no`, `load-scripts=no`, `load-auto-profiles=no`), and `config=no`, `input-default-bindings=no`, `input-vo-keyboard=no`. What a URL may name is what classic mpv takes today (the panel checks what it sends); narrowing it is not part of phase 1.

**Choosing it: Setup > Screen output, "Renderer"** (`GET` and `POST /api/renderer`, the Owner's). Two values: **Classic (mpv)**, the default on every box, and **New (pvj-render)**, with the picture detail beside it. The setting is `renderer` in the settings (`{"use": "classic"|"new", "detail": "auto"|540|720|"output"}`), read with defaults everywhere, no schema change. As for the screen's mode, the player may not read `settings.json`, so the panel writes the wish alone to `/var/lib/pvj/renderer.json` (0640, group pvj) and restarts the player for a change; the player's service reads it just before it starts. **Not in an export and not imported** (`boxcare.BOX_OWN`): it is chosen for this box and its screen, like the mode. **Remote support (D84):** switching the renderer outlives the session, and unlike the mode there is no try that goes back by itself; so the switch is refused through the tunnel (`support.REMOTE_DENY`), and a support session of the Owner's role may read it. The rows are in `tests/test_roles.py`.

**Falling back by itself.** With New chosen, `pvj-player serve` stays a small supervisor instead of becoming mpv (`renderer.Supervisor`): it starts pvj-render with the screen's arguments and waits up to 10 seconds for its socket to answer with its pid. **A failed start falls back at once**: pvj-render is not built, cannot be run, ends before it answers (no screen, a mode it cannot set, no OpenGL ES 3) or does not answer in time. **An end after it has drawn** is started once more; **a second end in a row** (less than 10 minutes after its start) falls back. Falling back is the same `exec` of classic mpv as before, with the same screen arguments, so the box shows a picture; the supervisor writes why to `/run/pvj/player/renderer.json` and its journal. One end after a long run is started again at once. A **quit** asked over the socket (the panel restarting the player, for a screen mode or this switch) makes pvj-render end with 0; the supervisor then ends with 0 too and systemd starts the service again, which reads the wish and the screen's mode again, so a restart of the player from the panel is also when a box that fell back tries pvj-render again. systemd's stop goes to the supervisor, which passes it on and waits up to 5 seconds. The panel reads the report as data from a less trusted account (opened without following a link or waiting, at most 64 KB, every field checked), counts it only when it is from the run that answers on the socket now (the supervisor's pid, pvj-render's parent), and the page and `GET /api/status` say which renderer draws and why ("New was chosen; classic mpv is drawing: pvj-render ended twice in a row (exit 3)"). There is no watchdog for a pvj-render that hangs while running, as there is none for classic mpv.

**Built on the box, from source.** The installer builds `render/` where the build tools and headers are there (`gcc`, `make`, `pkg-config`, `libmpv-dev`, `libegl-dev`, `libgles-dev`, `libgbm-dev`, `libdrm-dev`): it installs them as optional packages when it may use the network, as it does for remote support, and the image has them. A box where the build fails or the tools are missing installs everything else as before and the page says New is not available. An update (offline) builds again with what the box has. **Why on the box and not a binary from CI:** no binary is ever in the repository or in a release bundle, a release stays one signed archive of sources whose signature pvj-update checks, the program is built against the libmpv the box really has (Debian's ABI on that box), and nothing new has to be trusted (a CI artifact would need its own signing and provenance). The cost: about 120 MB of build packages on the image and a minute or two of compiling on a Pi 4 at each install and update. CI builds the same source on Ubuntu and on Debian 13 (the Pi's distribution, x86_64) on every push, so a break is seen before a release.

**Tests.** The job `pvj-render` (in `.github/workflows/pvj.yml`, two runs: Ubuntu, and Debian 13 whose libmpv must be mpv 0.40.x or the job fails) builds the helper with `-Wall -Wextra -Werror`, and runs `tests/test_pvj_render.py` against the real program in headless mode on Mesa's software GPU (llvmpipe, EGL surfaceless): a picture drawn the right way up at the output's size and at a smaller inside, a clip made in the job (pause, seek, loop, volume, stop, its end), brightness and an effect handed to libmpv, the mapping's show table and its editing shader with the right screen pixels, damaged or too large mapping files refused, a shader that does not compile leaving the picture unmapped, an overlay and show-text over the picture and over a mapping, the answers the panel reads, the refusals (`run`, `subprocess`, scripts, unlisted properties, loadfile options, filters, an overlay from a descriptor), bad and too long lines, and a clean end on quit and on SIGTERM; every picture is judged by its pixels (`pvj-capture`). A skip there fails the job. `tests/test_renderer.py` drives the player's side against a stand-in helper (a Python program that answers as pvj-render does and fails in each way on request): the choice, the arguments, the start, a failed start, a silent one, one end and two, a long run, systemd's stop, `Player.serve` falling back to the exec of classic mpv with the screen's mode, the report as untrusted data, and the routes, the restart, the status, an import and a reset through the real Api. Each part was broken on purpose and a test failed (`ipc.c`'s lists and the overlay path, the texture's size, the picture's and the mapping's orientation, the text layer, the inside size, the mapping's screen size, the fallback counts, the report's cleaning, the remote refusal, the box's own setting, the supervisor in `serve`); two of the breaks were caught only by a second line of defence that stays (a line longer than 64 KiB is also dropped when the buffer is full; a link or a pipe at the report's name is also refused by `read_json`). Locally (WSL, mpv 0.41, Mesa 26.0.8 llvmpipe) all of it passes. **Adding `pvj-render (Ubuntu)` and `pvj-render (Debian 13, the Pi's mpv)` to master's required checks is the owner's ruleset; it is proposed, not done.**

**Cost, and what is not known.** A second program in C to keep; a GPL folder in an Apache tree (the line is the folder: `render/` is GPL, nothing outside it links it); build packages on every box. Phase 1 alone gains a Pi nothing a show can see: it is the same picture through another program, at 540 lines inside where classic draws the clip at the output's size (a softer picture of one clip, for the room the later phases need; detail `output` gives classic's sharpness back). **Nothing of pvj-render has run on a Pi or in front of a screen**: only the spike did. The rows in `tools/DEVICE-TESTING.md` (R1 to R8): one 1080p30 clip at 540, 720 and 1080 lines, the mapping, the PIN screen, the switch and the fallback to classic. It reads control input on the box that owns the screen, so it needs the independent review before it is merged.

## D96. Sound-reactivity of the box's own: `pvj-sound` reads levels from a capture device, publishes them on the box, and the panel sends them on and moves a shader input (2026-10-18)

The owner's words (2026-10-16): "Yes, it's worthwhile to add sound-reactivity to the Pi and Mastercontrol overall." D95 put sound values in pvj-render's phase 4, "from a separate sound-analysis service". This is that service, and what can use it before phase 4. RNBO (Max's export for the Pi) stays an optional later source: it would write the same layout below.

**Design (written before the code; "As built" below says where it differs).**

- **What it is, and what it is not.** It analyses levels only. No audio is stored, recorded, played or sent anywhere, ever: the samples of one block live in memory for that block and are gone. What leaves the analysis is a handful of numbers per block (below). Off by default, with no device chosen.
- **A service of its own: `pvj-sound`** (`install/pvj-sound.service`, `bin/pvj-sound`, `pvj/sound.py`), installed on every box and idle until the Owner switches it on and chooses a device; with no device it idles and says so. Its own account, `pvj-sound`, whose only group is `audio` (who is in `audio` can already open the microphone, so the levels add nothing to what that group reads; the panel and the player are in it). The sandbox: **no network at all** (`PrivateNetwork=yes`, `RestrictAddressFamilies=AF_UNIX`, `IPAddressDeny=any`): the one process that touches sound cannot send anything anywhere; the devices closed but ALSA's (`DeviceAllow=char-alsa rw`: alsa-lib opens a capture device read-write), nothing writable but its own `/run/pvj-sound`, the box's settings and other helpers' folders out of sight, no capabilities, `CPUQuota=25%`, `MemoryMax=64M`, `TasksMax=8`. A release without the program skips the unit (`ConditionPathExists`), as the NDI helper's does.
- **Reading the device: `arecord` as a child, from Debian's `alsa-utils`.** `arecord -q -D plughw:CARD=<id>,DEV=<n> -t raw -f S16_LE -r 24000 -c 1`. Why: it is ALSA's own tool, in Raspberry Pi OS and one package away on Debian (the installer adds it where the network allows), so no third-party Python and no binding of our own to libasound through ctypes (an ABI we would have to keep). Why 24,000 samples a second in one channel: ALSA's plug layer mixes down and converts the rate in C inside arecord, so the Python side reads a quarter of the 48,000 x 2 a device gives; 12 kHz is still above the hats that matter to a picture. `/proc/asound` gives the list of capture devices and nothing else. **The device is never a free ALSA string**: only `<card id>,<device number>` of a capture device that `/proc/asound` lists now, made into `plughw:CARD=...,DEV=...` by the service; ALSA's `file` and `tee` plugins write audio to a file, and a free string could name them.
- **The analysis, per block of 20 ms (480 samples), in pure Python:** the RMS level in dB (and a 0 to 1 level from -60 to 0 dB), the peak and a clip flag, a smoothed envelope (10 ms up, 300 ms down), six band levels from a 512-point FFT of the last 512 samples with a Hann window (sub 40 to 120 Hz, bass 120 to 250, low mid 250 to 500, mid 500 to 2,000, high mid 2,000 to 5,000, high 5,000 to 12,000), onsets as spectral flux of the compressed magnitudes against the mean of the last second, a beat as an onset at least 0.18 s after the last one and above a floor of loudness, a beat pulse (1 at the beat, down to 0 in 0.15 s), and a tempo from the median of the last beats' intervals folded into 75 to 150 BPM, with a confidence. The gain trim (-24 to +24 dB) multiplies the levels, never the samples. The budget: under about 10 percent of one Pi 4 core; measured with a bounded benchmark (the numbers are below, once taken).
- **Where the values go, (a): one file of 128 bytes in `/run/pvj-sound/levels`**, written in place at every block (50 a second) and once a second while idle, readable by group `audio` (0640). Its layout is fixed and documented in `pvj/SOUND.md` (little-endian; a magic `NXSD`, a version, a sequence number at both ends and a CRC-32, so a reader that met a write half done reads again). pvj-render's phase 4 reads it with `pread` every frame; the panel reads it for the meter, OSC and the shader input. The service's socket (`/run/pvj-sound/sound.sock`) answers only the panel's account: the panel tells it what the Owner chose (on, device, gain), again after a restart of either; the service keeps no settings of its own.
