- **(b): OSC out, sent by the panel, never by the service.** Under `/nxlx/sound/...` (`pvj/SOUND.md` has the table: level, dB, envelope, pulse, a message at each beat with its count, BPM, and the six bands one by one and together), to the targets the Owner already lists for "Send state back" (D82; only while OSC is on and that switch is on, and each target held at every send to the same rule and the same address locks as the state) and to the render nodes (D94; held to the same rule at every send). Two switches, both off by default and the Owner's: "Send sound to the state targets" and "Send sound to the render nodes". At most 20 bundles a second to a target, a beat at once; caps per target (25 datagrams, 10,000 bytes in any second) and for all together (150, 60,000), with D82's buckets; nothing queues.
- **(c): the classic renderer's shaders: a slow envelope only.** A value change there is a new shader text that mpv compiles again (at most 5 a second, 0.34 to 0.61 s to the screen on a Pi 4, `pvj/SHADERS.md`). So an Operator may map one number input of the shader on the screen to the level, the beat (as a pulse smoothed into how busy the beat is) or a band, with where it rests (0 to 1 of the input's range), a depth (-1 to 1) and a smoothing (0.5 to 10 s), sent at most twice a second and only when it moved by 2 percent of the input's range. The page says plainly that this is slow and that real per-frame reaction comes with the new renderer (D95 phase 4), which reads the file above every frame.
- **The panel: Setup > Show tools > "Sound reactivity"** (the name "Sound" is taken by the sound output's page). The Owner chooses the device from the list, the gain trim, on and off, and the two OSC switches; an Operator sees the meter (bars from the file, through a light route the page asks about eight times a second while it is open) and sets the shader mapping. Routes, each a row in `tests/test_roles.py`: `GET /api/sound` and `GET /api/sound/levels` (the Operator's), `POST /api/sound` (the Owner's), `POST /api/sound/map` (the Operator's). A Guest gets nothing. No new listening port.
- **Remote support (D84).** A helper must not start listening to the room's microphone. A session of the Owner's role may switch capture off, change the gain, read the page and the meter, and map a shader input; it is refused switching capture on, choosing or changing the device while it is on, and switching either OSC sending on (each a 403 that says why; switching them off is allowed). Below the Owner's role: nothing new.
- **Settings.** A key `sound`, read with defaults (no schema change); **it is the box's own** (`boxcare.BOX_OWN`, beside the screen's mode and the renderer): it names this box's devices and is never exported or imported, so no settings file can switch a microphone on. An older release keeps the key and does nothing with it (tested with the release before this, 9ca9cbf, D71's pattern).
- **Privacy, said plainly.** The values are not sound. But six band levels fifty times a second are close to what a channel vocoder carries, and someone determined could make speech in the room partly understandable from them. Hence: the file is for group `audio` only (who can already record the microphone); OSC is off by default, at most 20 a second, and goes only to devices the Owner listed on the box's own network; the diagnostics file carries the switches and never a value. Every buffer is bounded: one block, the 512-sample window, a second of flux, the last 16 beats, at most 4 KB of arecord's words.

**As built (2026-10-18, #145), where it differs from the design above or adds to it.**

- **16,000 samples a second, not 24,000; a 256-point FFT, not 512.** The bounded benchmark (`pvj/SOUND.md`, "What it costs"): on the development laptop (Ryzen 7 PRO 6850U, Python 3.14) the first build at 24 kHz with a 512-point FFT took 0.548 ms a block, 2.7 percent of one core; as a half-size real FFT 0.390 ms, 2.0 percent; at 16 kHz with 256 points **0.21 ms a block, about 1.0 to 1.3 percent**. A Pi 4 core runs pure Python roughly 5 to 8 times slower, so the estimate there is **6 to 10 percent of one core**, inside the budget; 24 kHz would have been 10 to 16. Not measured on a Pi (SR6). The top band is 5,000 to 8,000 Hz; the bins are 62.5 Hz apart, so the sub band is one or two bins. The FFT sees the last 16 ms of each 20 ms block (a kick lasts longer); the window's mean is taken out in bins 0 and 1 only, which is exact with a Hann window.
- **The caps** are 25 datagrams and 12,000 bytes in any second to one target (D82's per-target figures), 150 and 72,000 for all together: one send of the values is about 450 bytes, so the byte cap binds first for them and the count cap for the beats. A beat is a message of its own, sent within about 10 ms (the panel looks at the file every 10 ms while sound goes out, every second otherwise).
- **The tempo** is the mean of the intervals near the median, not the median: a beat is seen at the end of a block, so intervals alternate a block short and a block long (140 BPM read as 142.9 with the median).
- **The shader input starts from where it rests**, never from the level at the moment it is switched on (it would jump).
- **The benchmark found a fault the tests had not**: an even number of beat intervals whose middle two are far apart left nothing near the median and divided by zero, which would have ended the capture thread while the socket still answered. Fixed, with a test, and the capture thread now survives any fault of its own (said on the page, started again after the pause).
- **The service** runs in group `audio` only; the installer makes `pvj-sound` with `--gid audio`, writes and enables the unit on every box and starts it even on an update with `--no-start` (it is idle until the Owner chooses a device); it installs `alsa-utils` where the network allows, and the image lists it. Uninstall removes the unit and leaves the account. `MemoryDenyWriteExecute` and `SystemCallFilter=@system-service` are set; whether arecord and ALSA's plug layer run under them is a device test (SR1).
- **What was tested** (`tests/test_sound.py`, 46 tests; every one of 31 deliberate breaks of the code made a test fail): sines at four levels read their level, a sine in each band reads there and at least 24 dB down two bands away, a DC offset stays out of the lowest band, click tracks at 90, 120 and 140 BPM give one beat per click within two blocks and the tempo within 2 BPM, 200 BPM folds to 100, hiss and a steady tone give no beats, the pulse, the gain trim, the envelope, the clip flag, the real FFT against a direct one; the layout at its documented offsets, a torn record and a changed byte refused, the reader never following a link or waiting on a pipe; `/proc/asound` parsing, free ALSA strings refused; the service idling with no device and saying so, capturing from a stand-in arecord, following the gain without a restart, ending arecord when switched off, an arecord that ends (its words on the page) and one that stalls; the socket answering only the allowed uid; the exact OSC bundle on a loopback socket, time to live 1, the caps, where it may go (the network rule, the address locks, a name not yet found, both switches); the beat at once; the thread only while needed; the shader input at most every half second and only when it moves; the routes and roles; remote support refused switching on, the device while on and sending on, allowed off, the gain and the mapping; no settings file carrying the section; the unit's sandbox; the release before (9ca9cbf) loading and keeping the key. Rows in `tests/test_roles.py`; `tests/test_units.py` knows the new folder and why this unit may open ALSA devices read-write.
- **Not tested, said plainly:** nothing has run on a Pi or with a real audio input; whether arecord starts inside this sandbox; the CPU on a Pi; whether the beat follows real music (a click track is the easy case); the meter in a real browser (CI's browser test runs the page with no service behind it). DEVICE-TESTING SR1 to SR8.

**After the independent security review of #145 at 7ffce1c (2026-10-18): two Mediums and three Lows, each fixed with a test that fails without it.**

- *M1: the fast bands left the box.* `GET /api/sound/levels` gave an Operator, and a support session of the Operator's role through WireGuard, the six bands of every block, polled 8 or more times a second: the "vocoder" concern above, carried off the box. **The coordinator's decision, for every reader off the box** (HTTP to any device, remote support, OSC to the D82 targets and the D94 nodes): the bands leave only averaged over 200 ms and at most 5 times a second (modulation under 2.5 Hz is below the syllable rate of about 4 to 5 a second, so only whether someone speaks remains; precision barely matters, the rate does). The level, the envelope and the beat may stay at 20 a second. Through the support tunnel: level, envelope and beat, no band. The levels file on the box keeps 50 a second for pvj-render. As built: the service computes the slow bands itself and writes them in the file's reserved bytes (104 to 115, six u16; no version change, as the layout's rule allows); `soundout.public` is the one view every device is given; OSC sends the values bundle at 20 a second without bands and a bands bundle of the slow ones at 5. The page shows the Owner one plain line above the sending switches, and its hint that said the levels leave the box "only to the devices you list" (untrue: the meter goes to every paired Operator and Owner who opens the page) now says where they go.
- *M2: no core limit.* A crash of arecord or of the service could have written capture buffers to `/var/lib/systemd/coredump`. The unit has `LimitCORE=0` (inherited by arecord), and the service sets its own core limit to 0 and marks itself not dumpable (`prctl(PR_SET_DUMPABLE, 0)`) before anything is captured. `CoredumpFilter` was not needed with no core at all.
- *L3: the cap was below the schedule.* 20 bundles a second plus the beats was more than 20 datagrams a second, so bundles dropped steadily. The cap per target is now 32 a second with a burst of 6 (38 in any second, the bytes unchanged at 12,000), all together 384 and 72 (456 in any second, 86,400 bytes); a test runs six seconds of the real schedule with beats as fast as the analysis gives them and nothing drops.
- *L4: where it may go was worked out every 10 ms.* It is worked out once a second, or at once after the setting changes; the setting itself is read on the same terms.
- *L5: late values looked fresh.* A record carried the time it was written. It now carries when its block's last sample was heard (the bytes still waiting in arecord's pipe, `FIONREAD`, said in seconds), so a block analysed late looks stale to every reader, and a backlog of more than 2 s is thrown away.


## D97. Sound sources: a channel of a capture device, the box's own sound, the NDI sound it plays, and levels sent in over OSC (Max/RNBO); the Owner allows, the Owner and Operators switch (2026-10-18)

The owner's words (2026-10-18), on the list of sources proposed after D96: "All of the above please :). You can put the more 'advanced' options into an advanced subsection if it makes sense." And on who switches: "Owner and Operator".

**Design (written before the code; "As built" below says where it differs).**

- **One publish channel stays.** Every source ends in the same 128-byte levels file of D96, written by `pvj-sound` alone, so the page, OSC out, the shader input and pvj-render's phase 4 do not change with the source. The privacy rule of D96's review stays for every source: the bands leave the box only averaged over 200 ms, at most 5 times a second, none through the support tunnel; no audio is stored or sent.
- **1. A channel of a capture device.** The device's capture channel count is read from `/proc/asound/card<N>/stream0` (USB audio, "Capture:" then "Channels: N"; 1 to 32), and 2 where the card does not say. The Owner chooses the mix (as now: ALSA's plug layer mixes down, `-c 1`), or input 1 to N (left and right for a pair). For a chosen input arecord is run with `-c N` and the service takes that channel out of each block itself (one strided slice of the samples, in C). arecord stays argv only, `plughw:CARD=<id>,DEV=<n>` only, and `-c` is a number the service checked against the count it read.
- **2. The box's own sound** (what the clip or the NDI stream plays): **measured inside the player, never copied.** The panel adds one audio filter to classic mpv, `@pvjsound:lavfi=[astats=metadata=1:reset=1:measure_perchannel=none:measure_overall=RMS_level+Peak_level]`, which passes the sound through unchanged and leaves its RMS and peak level per audio frame in the player's property `af-metadata/pvjsound`; the panel reads that over the player's socket it already uses, 25 times a second, and hands the numbers to `pvj-sound`, which turns them into the level, the envelope, onsets, beats and a tempo as for a device. Why this way: no new privilege, no new device, no kernel module, no second sink, and no audio leaves the player at all (only two numbers per frame). Rejected: an ALSA loopback (`snd-aloop`) with a `multi` device in `/etc/asound.conf` (the player's own path to the HDMI sound would change, two clocks in lockstep drift and xrun under a show); PipeWire's monitor (not on Raspberry Pi OS Lite, and it would run as the player); a second mpv output (mpv has one). Cost: astats in C on 48 kHz stereo, small (to be measured on the Pi); latency: the filter runs before the player's output buffer, so the meter leads the loudspeakers by that buffer (about 0.1 to 0.2 s). **What it cannot give: bands** (lavfi in mpv passes only the main chain's metadata), so the bands of this source are 0, and the page says so. pvj-render (D95) does not take `af` over its socket: with the new renderer drawing, this source says it is not available until phase 4.
- **3. Advanced: the NDI sound.** The NDI helper already receives the one NDI sound source and the player plays it (D87). So the NDI source is the same measurement in the player, counted only while the player shows NDI; no second NDI connection, and no copy of the helper's PCM (that would be a new path for sound to leave the helper's sandbox).
- **4. Advanced: levels sent in over OSC (Max/RNBO).** Messages under `/nxlx/sound/in/` on the box's OSC port: `level` (f 0 to 1), `db` (f), `envelope` (f 0 to 1), `beat` (no argument or i), `bpm` (f), `bands` (6 x f 0 to 1). Taken only from the IPv4 addresses of the render nodes (D94) and of an "Accept from" list the Owner keeps (at most 8, each a private address on one of the box's own networks at that moment, never the support tunnel's); every other sender's `/nxlx/sound/in/` is nothing, and these addresses do nothing else. Checked before OSC's locks (D78), because the list is narrower than any of them and the messages can only feed numbers into the levels file; with OSC switched off nothing is received. Bounded: at most 100 messages a second from one sender and 200 from all, values clamped to their ranges, at most 50 feeds a second to the service; nothing is ever sent in answer.
- **5. Who.** The Owner keeps the list of allowed sources (`sound.allowed`, the device alone by default), the device and its channel, the gain, the "Accept from" list and listening on and off. **The Owner and Operators switch between the allowed sources** (`POST /api/sound/source`, the Operator's). Remote support as D96: a session never switches listening on; it may switch to an allowed source that is not a microphone (the box's sound, NDI, OSC in) and is refused switching to the capture device, a channel change while listening, and adding an address to "Accept from". D80 rows; D84 in the handler.
- **Settings.** New keys in `sound` (`source`, `allowed`, `channel`, `in_hosts`), still the box's own and in no settings file. An older release (D96 at 5a3a1c6) meets keys it does not know and reads the whole section as off (its `stored` fails closed): going back switches listening off, never on.

**As built (2026-10-18, #147), where it differs from the design above or adds to it.**

- **The input of a card:** `sound.capture_channels` reads the largest "Channels:" under "Capture:" in the card's stream file (never Playback's), 1 to 32, else 2. The service restarts arecord when the device, the input, the count or the source changes. A two-input card shows Left and Right, a larger one Input 1 to N, and the mix stays the default.
- **The box's own sound:** `soundout.METER_FILTER` is put into the player's audio filters while that source (or NDI) is chosen and taken out when it is not; whether it is still there is looked at every 2 s (a player that restarted lost it). The panel reads `af-metadata/pvjsound` 25 times a second; only the RMS and peak in dB ever leave the player, and they go to `pvj-sound` as a feed (`{"cmd": "feed", "rms_db", "peak_db"}`). The service's `FeedAnalyzer` makes the envelope, onsets (a rise of at least 3 dB against the last second), beats, the pulse and a tempo from them. Measured on a real mpv (0.41, `--ao=null`) in `tests/test_sound_sources.py`: a 0.25 sine in one of two channels reads -18 dB RMS and -12 dB peak, as it should. **Not measured: its CPU on a Pi**, expected small (astats is a few additions per sample in C); latency is the player's audio buffer, about 0.1 to 0.2 s ahead of the loudspeakers. pvj-render refuses `af`, and the page then says so.
- **The NDI sound** is the same meter, fed only while the NDI input shows a source (`Input.current`); otherwise the page says no NDI picture is playing.
- **OSC in** is handled in `OscServer.handle_packet` after the outer wall and the rate limit of D78 and before its address and key locks, only for a packet whose every message is under `/nxlx/sound/in/` and only from an address `Sound.osc_in_host` allows (worked out once a second: the render nodes' IPv4 hosts and the "Accept from" list, each passing the render bridge's rule, so never the box itself or the tunnel). Anything else in the packet sends it the normal way, through every lock. `level` 0 to 1 is taken as -60 to 0 dB. A beat is fed once. Caps: 100 messages in any second from one sender, 200 from all, 50 feeds a second; the service itself takes at most 120 feeds in any second.
- **The page:** a Source chooser for the Owner and Operators (shown when more than one is allowed; a source without bands says so); for the Owner the Input of the device and, folded under "Advanced: sources and levels sent in", the allowed sources and the "Accept from" addresses (an Operator does not see the addresses).
- **Tests:** `tests/test_sound_sources.py` (20), the changed rows of `tests/test_sound.py` and a row in `tests/test_roles.py`; the release before (5a3a1c6) reading the section as off and keeping it. CI fetches that commit.
- **Not run:** anything on a Pi or with a real multi-input card, Max/RNBO sending in, the meter on the Pi's player with a real clip and NDI sound. DEVICE-TESTING SS1 to SS6.

**Addendum (review, 2026-10-18):** a change that would leave the microphone as the source while listening is on is refused through remote support, whatever field causes it (choosing it, or taking the source in use off the list so the microphone is the first left). The rule is checked on the result, not on the request.

## D98. pvj-render phase 2: two video layers, and the crossfade drawn by the renderer itself (2026-10-18)

**The owner's words** (2026-10-18): "yes, start phase 2 of the renderer. let's give it real crossfades across two layers." D95 named phase 2: "a second libmpv context, the spike's pipelined loop with one conversion per vblank, the crossfade drawn by the renderer instead of D71's still", and "Phase 2 draws opacity in the renderer's own compositor".

**What it is.**

- **Two video layers.** pvj-render starts two libmpv cores with a render context each, both drawing into a texture at the internal size (`renderer.detail`). One is the **front**: the socket speaks to it and the screen shows it. The other is idle: no file, nothing decoded, never drawn. Its cost while idle is a core's threads asleep and a few megabytes.
- **A crossfade** is one new command, in mpv's style: `["pvj-crossfade", URL, SECONDS, "keep-open=no,image-display-duration=inf"]`. The clip is loaded into the idle layer, and that layer is the front **from the command on**: `path`, `pause`, `time-pos`, the loops, `playlist`, a seek, a screenshot and `osd-dimensions` are the new clip's at once, exactly as after a `loadfile`. So `pvj/player.py` and everything above it still see one player. The outgoing clip plays on under it (it is not frozen, as D71's had to be), silent, and the render thread mixes the two.
- **The mix, once a vblank.** The render loop waits for the incoming clip's first frame (libmpv's playback restart for that file), then the incoming share goes from 0 to 1 **in a straight line by the clock** over SECONDS, as D71's still was made more transparent in a straight line. Each vblank: each layer converts at most one new clip frame (one conversion per layer per vblank, as in phase 1 and the spike), one mix pass at the internal size makes `outgoing * (1 - share) + incoming * share`, and the existing final pass (the mapping or the plain scale) takes the mix as it took the one layer. At the end the outgoing layer is stopped and is idle again. While no fade runs there is no mix pass at all.
- **Pacing: better than the box today.** Classic's crossfade is a still stepped by the panel at 20 steps a second (`transitions.STEPS`; about 40 steps for 2 seconds on the Pi), and the outgoing picture stands still. pvj-render's mix moves at every vblank (60 a second at 60 Hz), both pictures move, there is no still to take (no freeze before the fade, which was 0.6 s at 2560 x 1440 on a Pi 4), and nothing goes through Python per step.
- **The duration and the controls are the panel's own.** The Mix's transition (Crossfade) and its duration (0.1 to 10 s) are used unchanged; nothing new for the operator to learn. The status says no fallback for a crossfade on the new renderer (nothing of a still can give it up).

**How the look reaches both layers.** The names that are how the picture looks and sounds (`brightness`, which is the panel's opacity, `video-zoom`, `video-pan-x`, `video-pan-y`, `video-rotate`, `video-crop`, `keepaspect`, `fbo-format`, `speed`, `volume`, `mute`, `audio-device`, the screenshot settings), the flips (`vf`) and the user shaders that libmpv runs (effects, generators: `glsl-shaders`) are set on **both** layers whenever they are set. So the incoming clip has the look the panel set from its first frame, an effect stays on through a crossfade as D74 wants, and a **Blackout or a fade of the level during a crossfade darkens both pictures** (with D71 these ended the still at once: that was a rule of the still, not an owner's rule, and the screen still goes black at once). The file's own names (`pause`, `loop-file`, `loop-playlist`, `keep-open`, `image-display-duration`, a live input's keys) go to the front only.

**What ends a crossfade early.** A `loadfile` that replaces, `stop`, `playlist-next` or `-prev`, and a new `pvj-crossfade`: the fade that runs ends at once (its incoming clip is the picture, its outgoing layer is stopped), then the command goes to the front. A crossfade into a file that does not play fades to that layer's empty picture (black) over its seconds, where a cut to it would be black at once, as on classic. If the clip's first frame does not come within 10 seconds the fade runs anyway, as D71's did.

**Sound.** The outgoing clip's sound stops at the tap, as D71's freeze stopped it. One sound device, one core on it at a time: a raw ALSA device may take only one opener. So the incoming clip is loaded with no sound track, the outgoing layer's sound is taken off, the renderer waits (at most 1 second) until that core has closed its sound output, and only then gives the incoming clip its sound (after the review, see the addendum). A crossfade of the sound itself is not built.

**The panel's side** (`pvj/player.py`, `pvj/api.py`). `Player.crossfades()` asks the player once per process for `pvj-render`'s `layers`; classic mpv answers "property not found", which is remembered for that process. `Api.play` with the Mix on Crossfade, something lit to blend from, a plain clip on the screen (`Player.fades_from_clip`: no generator, no live input) and a player that has layers, calls `Player.play(..., fade=duration)` and takes no still. Everything else is untouched: a cut, a dip, a wipe, a slide, a play from black, and every play on classic mpv go exactly the way they went (a test checks that a cut and a dip never even ask about layers, and that classic still takes D71's still).

**Falls back to a cut, never a black screen**, and the panel's journal says why ("no crossfade on the new renderer, a cut instead: ..."):

- the renderer refuses or fails `pvj-crossfade` (its answer is mpv's words), or does not answer;
- **into a list**, a preset, a slideshow, a stream, NDI, the capture input or a generator: these never blended through `Api.play`, on classic either;
- a live input's narrowing that could not be taken back (`_pipe_globals`): only a load into that same layer takes it back;
- nothing on the screen (after a Stop, at power-up): the renderer loads the clip into the front layer as a cut, as D71 starts the clip "as with a cut" there.

**Out of a generator or a live input the crossfade is D71's still, on the new renderer too** (corrected after the review; the first version cut there, which was a step back from classic and from phase 1). The generator's shader is in the shader list both layers share and comes off at the tap, so the renderer's two layers cannot fade out of it (that needs a shader list per layer: phase 3, generators as layers); and a live input's pipe must be let go of by the one reader before the next source (D62's hand-over), which a second layer still reading it would break. D71's still works on pvj-render as on classic, so those fades are as they were. Wipes and slides keep D71's still on the new renderer too (its "window" still is the front layer without the mapping and the text, as D95 said); they are the next thing to draw in the compositor.

**The socket's rules are kept** (#146): at most 32 clients, a line at most 64 KiB, a client that does not read dropped after 2 seconds. `pvj-crossfade` is on the fixed list: its URL is a string of at most 4096 bytes loaded by the same hardened libmpv as any `loadfile`, SECONDS a number from 0 to 60, the options only `keep-open` and `image-display-duration` with plain values; anything else is "invalid parameter", and `aid` may not be set through the socket ("property not found"). The render thread never waits on libmpv: the stop at a fade's end is sent with `mpv_command_async`, under the same lock the socket's thread takes before it loads the next crossfade into that layer, so the load always comes after the stop in that core's queue. libmpv's GL fences are still deleted after each render, on both layers; and libmpv's textures, framebuffers, renderbuffers and buffers are now counted the same way (`gl-objects`).

**What the `pvj-render` property says now:** `layers` (2), `front` (0 or 1), `fade` (`off`, `waiting`, `running`), `fade-progress`, `fade-seconds`, `fades`, `fades-done`, `fades-cut`, `back-drawn` (frames the other layer converted), `back-idle`, `gl-objects`, `fades-from-nothing`, `sound-held`, beside phase 1's keys. Version 0.2.0.

**Tests.** `tests/test_pvj_render.py` (pvj-render for real, headless, on llvmpipe): the mix of two pictures half way, by pixel; the swap of roles and the answers after it (path, pause, size, playlist, a seek, a paused clip left behind); the straight line by the clock; 0 seconds; swaps again and again; a Blackout and a flip reaching both layers; a load, a stop and a new crossfade during a fade; a file that does not play; the refusals; the idle layer drawing nothing and stopped after a fade; 60 flips a second during a fade of two 30 fps clips with each layer converting each of its frames once; **220 fades in a row** (200 of two pictures, 20 of two clips, bounded at 300 s) with the file descriptors (9 then 9 here), the GL fences (0 held, over 1000 deleted) and libmpv's GL objects (no growth over the last fades); and `Player.play(fade=...)` through the real program. `tests/test_transitions.py` (`LayeredCrossfade`, the real Player and Api over the stand-in mpv): the renderer's crossfade with no still and the Mix's own duration, asked once per process, classic unchanged, cut and dip never asking, a refusal giving a cut, out of a generator and out of a live input giving D71's still (after the review), a play from Blackout, and the status. Each important part was broken on purpose and its test failed (16 breaks: the mirror of brightness, the mirror of a flip, the swap, the idle layer's drawing, the stop at the end, the end at a load, the option keys, the straight line, a failed load, the fence deletion, a file descriptor per fade, the cut on a refusal, classic's still, the cut out of a generator, the probe once per process, the fade in `Player.play`).

**Cost.** One more libmpv core for the life of the program (idle: threads asleep, a few megabytes). During a fade: a second clip decoded (the spike's software decode of two 1080p30 clips at 540 lines inside held 10 minutes with 9 missed vblanks, the CPU about 80 percent on each core, the GPU 46 percent busy), one more conversion a frame and one mix pass at the internal size. At 720 lines the spike missed 3 vblanks in 2 minutes; **at the output's size (1080 lines) two layers did not hold in the spike**, so a crossfade at detail `output` may drop frames on a Pi 4 (a device test says).

**Limits, said plainly.** Not run on a Pi or in front of a screen: everything above ran on a software GPU in a 320 x 180 headless window (WSL, mpv 0.41, Mesa llvmpipe). Not known: flips and missed vblanks during a fade on the Pi at each detail, the GPU's share, whether the outgoing core lets go of the sound device in time for the incoming one on the box's HDMI device, and the sync-file count on V3D after many fades. Those are rows CF1 to CF10 in `tools/DEVICE-TESTING.md`. The sound cuts at the tap; out of a generator or a live input, and for wipes and slides, the crossfade is D71's still.

**Addendum (review of #148, 2026-10-18).** One Medium and four Lows, all fixed, each with a test that fails without its fix:

- **Medium: out of a generator or a live input the first version cut**, where classic and phase 1 fade from D71's still (D89: the window still is used with a generator on). `Api._layered_fade` now also asks `Player.fades_from_clip()` (no generator, no carrier, no live input's pipe; asks the player nothing and takes no lock), and otherwise the play goes D71's way (still, dip or cut by its rules). The status shows the still's reason for giving up whenever the still is what a crossfade would use. Tests: a crossfade out of a generator and out of a live input on the new renderer take the still and run to "done"; the status row with a generator on.
- **The outgoing clip could go black in the middle of a fade**: with its own `keep-open=no` a clip that reached its end went idle and was drawn black. At the tap the outgoing layer gets `keep-open=yes` (it is stopped at the end anyway), and the incoming layer always starts from `keep-open=no` before its options. Test: a 3 second clip fading out over 8 seconds is not idle half way, and its picture is still there.
- **After a Stop a crossfade rose from black**: with nothing loaded on the front (`idle-active`) the renderer now loads the clip into the front layer as a cut (`fades-from-nothing` counts it). Tests: after a Stop and at power-up.
- **The render thread read the fade's clock apart from its phase**: a crossfade that began between the two could mix one wrong picture and write a wrong `fade-progress`. The layer, the phase, the start and the length are now read in one look under the lock, and nothing is mixed or written unless the front is still the layer looked at (not break-checked: the window is a few microseconds and no test can hit it on purpose).
- **The leak test was loose** (+4 descriptors, +64 GL objects). Now, after a warm-up of pictures and clips, the counts at rest are the same (give or take 2) after 200 more fades and after 30 fades each cut short by the next one (which runs `end_fade`), and the thread count (`/proc/PID/task`) is checked as well. Here: 8 descriptors, 81 threads and 38 GL objects at the start, after 200 and after the 30 cut short (40). Breaks caught: a descriptor leaked in `end_fade`, a libmpv core leaked per fade (threads 101 to 301).
- **The sound order** is now as described under Sound above: the outgoing `aid=no` is checked and logged, `current-ao` is waited for (bounded, 1 s), a core that still holds the device is logged and counted (`sound-held`). Test: a clip with a tone into another, and back; the incoming clip has its sound track each time, nothing held. Not known: what the box's HDMI device does in that second (CF1).
- **CI: PaceTest measured 63 to 64 flips a second on a clock of 60** (all four pvj-render jobs, mpv 0.37 and 0.40; master passed). The first version asked the idle layer's libmpv core for `idle-active` inside the `pvj-render` property, after the counters had been taken. 127 flips in a 2 second window is about 7 frames too many, which fits one answer leaving about 110 ms after its counters, at the start of the window: that question is the first one ever put to the idle core in that process. Nothing presented more often; the clock was read late. Now nothing in that property asks libmpv: each layer's event thread keeps `idle-active` by `mpv_observe_property`, read with an atomic load beside the counters. **Not reproduced here** (mpv 0.41: the answer takes 0.3 ms, and PaceTest passed 9 times, 3 of them on 2 pinned cores), so this cause is a reading of the numbers, not a measurement; CI on the next push says.
- **The sound test** opens a real output (ALSA's `null` device, `--audio-device=alsa/null`), so the order is tested with a device open: with the outgoing `aid=no` left out, the outgoing output stays open and `sound-held` counts it (caught); with the incoming sound left off, the incoming clip has no track (caught). WSLg's PulseAudio, this machine's own server, once held a core for over 10 seconds, which is why the test does not use it.
- **An older fault met on the way, fixed: the text layer sometimes drew nothing for the life of the process** (`TextLayerTest`: an overlay never showed; master's build 1 start in 30, this branch 4 in 30 before the fix). Every libmpv core was started with `force-window=yes` before its render context existed, so the core tried to make its window at once ("No render context set", "Error opening/initializing the VO window") and raced the render context's creation. The cores now start with `force-window=no`, and it is turned on right after their render context is made. After: 0 failures in 60 starts. Break-checked only by counting (the fault is a race, about one start in eight before).
- **CI on da0808a, Debian 13 (mpv 0.40): libmpv's GL objects 39, then 42 after 30 fades cut short**, over the leak test's slack of 2. Measured here over 600 fades cut short, with the count read at rest after 30, 60, 90, 120, 150, 180, 240, 300, 360, 480 and 600: 38, 39, 39, 39, 39, 41, 41, 39, 41, 39, 39 (37 before), down as well as up, with the descriptors (9) and threads (81) flat. That is a pool inside a band of about 5, not a leak (one a fade would be 600 more). Most likely it is the textures of the frames libmpv had queued when a load was cut; that is a reading, not traced in libmpv. The test now judges the cut-short run by its trend: three warm-up checkpoints of 20, then four of 30, each later reading at most 4 over the warm-up's highest. Four local runs read 39 to 42 and passed. Breaks caught: one object leaked a fade (258 after the 200), one in ten fades (60 after the 200), and one in ten fades only once fades had been cut short (51 over a limit of 49 at the first later checkpoint).
- **Not done:** matching the start of the fade to loadfile's `playlist_entry_id` (mpv 0.38 on). The counters by start already ignore what an older file of the layer says; the id would only close the case of a raw client that sends loads into the same layer faster than its events, which the panel never does.
- **Found on the way, not fixed here:** in a fresh headless process a clip is often drawn black for its whole run (about five starts of six here, with a still picture fine and the clip fine once a picture has been shown in that process). Master's phase 1 build (b64e23f) does the same, so it is not this change; no phase 1 test ever judged a clip by its pixels. Whether a Pi does it is not known. It is in the journal as open.
